## Phase 1 — Core Domain

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `ServerSleuth.slnx` solution (SDK-default format; functionally equivalent to `.sln`) with `src/ServerSleuth.Core` and `test/ServerSleuth.Core.Tests`.
  - `ServerSleuth.Core`: `DiscoveryEntity` base + 21 concrete entities (`Server`, `OperatingSystem`, `Service`, `Process`, `Port`, `Application`, `Software`, `Package`, `Runtime`, `Sdk`, `Dll`, `ComComponent`, `WebSite`, `ApplicationPool`, `ScheduledTask`, `Container`, `Database`, `Certificate`, `Configuration`, `EnvironmentVariable`, `File`, `ExternalDependency`).
  - Enums: `EntityStatus`, `EntityArchitecture`, `PlatformSupport`, `ScannerStatus`, `ScanProfile`, `EvidenceType`, `DependencyEdgeType`.
  - Evidence model: `EvidenceRecord`, `Confidence` (validated 0.00–1.00, band lookup matching skill.md §22 boundaries exactly).
  - Result model: `DiscoveryResult`, `DiscoveryError`.
  - `IDiscoveryScanner` interface and `DiscoveryContext`.
  - Graph model: `DependencyEdge`, `DependencyGraph` (duplicate-node guard, duplicate-edge merge by evidence union + max confidence).
- **Tests**: 31 xUnit tests across `Evidence/ConfidenceTests`, `Models/DiscoveryEntityTests`, `Results/DiscoveryResultTests`, `Graph/DependencyGraphTests`, `Interfaces/DiscoveryScannerTests` (with a `FakeScanner` test double). Covers: confidence band boundaries and validation, evidence attachment/multiplicity, tag dedup, metadata overwrite, success/failure/partial result shapes, duplicate-node rejection, duplicate-edge merge behavior, edge filtering, scanner cancellation.
- **Build result**: `dotnet build` → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test` → 31/31 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (111ms). Both commands actually executed on 2026-08-23, not assumed.
- **Known limitations**:
  - No scanner registry yet (deferred to Phase 2/9 per plan — only the `IDiscoveryScanner` interface it depends on exists so far).
  - Entity dedup/merge *logic* is not implemented yet — `DependencyGraph.AddNode` currently only guards against accidental duplicate IDs; real merging is Phase 5's job (Correlation), per `skill.md` §32.
  - `.slnx` used instead of the classic `.sln` referenced in `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s original wording — same tooling behavior on this SDK version (10.0.400), noted here rather than treated as a deviation requiring approval.
- **Next phase**: Phase 2 — Common Infrastructure (`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`: `IProcessRunner`, `IFileSystemReader`, `IPortInspector`, `SecretRedactor`, structured logging event names).

## Phase 2 — Common Infrastructure

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` project (added to `ServerSleuth.slnx`), referencing `ServerSleuth.Core` only.
  - `Common/OperationStatus.cs`, `Common/ScannerLogEvents.cs`.
  - `Process/`: `IProcessRunner`, `ProcessRunner`, `ProcessRequest`, `ProcessResult` — argument-list-based execution (no shell string), timeout + cancellation with full process-tree kill.
  - `FileSystem/`: `IFileSystemReader`, `FileSystemReader`, `FileEntryInfo`, `FileSystemResult<T>` — classifies `AccessDenied`/`NotFound`/`IoError` instead of throwing.
  - `Networking/`: `IPortInspector`, `NetworkEndpoint` — interface + DTO only, no concrete implementation (by design, deferred to Phase 3/6).
  - `Security/`: `ISecretRedactor`, `SecretRedactor`, `SecretRedactionRule`, `SecretSeverity` — rule-based, covers all §24 patterns plus PEM/JWT/Bearer, anchored to avoid false positives.
  - `DependencyInjection/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`: `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` registering `IProcessRunner`, `IFileSystemReader`, `ISecretRedactor` (not `IPortInspector`, intentionally).
  - Full detail in `SCANNERS.md` (new) and the ARCHITECTURE.md Phase 2 addendum.
- **Tests**: 45 new xUnit tests in `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests` — `ProcessRunnerTests` (7: success, non-zero exit, stdout, stderr, invalid args, timeout, cancellation, executable-not-found — the not-found case runs on any OS, the other 6 are Windows-fixture-gated and no-op on non-Windows), `FileSystemReaderTests` (12: exists, read text/bytes, missing file, directory-as-file → AccessDenied, locked file → IoError, file info, enumerate files/directories including missing-directory), `SecretRedactorTests` (17: all §24 patterns, case-insensitivity, separator spacing, multi-secret string, key preservation, PEM block, Bearer token, 6 false-positive cases, plain-config negative case), `PortInspectorContractTests` (2: empty result, multiple endpoints with protocol/process correlation, against a test-only `FakePortInspector`).
- **Build result**: Actually executed 2026-08-23:
  ```
  dotnet restore  → up to date, no errors
  dotnet build    → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors (Core, Infrastructure, Core.Tests, Infrastructure.Tests)
  dotnet test     → ServerSleuth.Core.Tests: 31 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
                     ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests: 45 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ```
  76 tests total across the solution, all green.
- **Known limitations**:
  - `IPortInspector` has no real implementation — expected, arrives Phase 3 (Windows) / Phase 6 (Linux).
  - `ProcessRunner` and `FileSystemReader` are cross-platform code, but the test fixtures currently only exercise the Windows path (`cmd.exe`, `ping`) since this dev environment is Windows-only; the 6 Windows-gated `ProcessRunnerTests` no-op (pass trivially) if ever run on Linux until Phase 6 adds `sh`/`sleep` equivalents. This is a test-fixture gap, not a `ProcessRunner` implementation gap.
  - `SecretRedactor`'s default rule set covers exactly the patterns named in skill.md §24 plus three common freestanding-secret shapes (PEM, JWT, Bearer) — provider-specific credential formats (e.g. AWS/Azure key shapes) are not yet covered; adding one is a new `SecretRedactionRule`, no architectural change needed.
  - No scanner registry yet — still correctly deferred; only `IDiscoveryScanner` (from Phase 1) exists so far.
- **Next phase**: Phase 3 — Windows Foundation (`ServerSleuth.Windows`: OS scanner, Process scanner, Port scanner, Windows Service scanner, Installed Software scanner). Not started — stopping here per instructions to report before continuing.

## Phase 3 — Windows Foundation

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `ServerSleuth.Windows` (target `net8.0-windows`, references `Core` + `Infrastructure`) and `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests`, both added to `ServerSleuth.slnx`.
  - Five `IDiscoveryScanner` implementations: `windows-os-scanner`, `windows-process-scanner`, `windows-port-scanner`, `windows-service-scanner`, `windows-installed-software-scanner`. Full detail in `SCANNERS.md`.
  - Supporting abstractions: `IWindowsRegistryReader`/`WindowsRegistryReader` (registry access with AccessDenied/NotFound classification), `IProcessEnumerator`/`ProcessEnumerator`, `IProcessWmiProvider`/`ProcessWmiProvider` (Win32_Process + GetOwner), `INetworkTableProvider`/`NetworkTableProvider` (MSFT_NetTCPConnection/MSFT_NetUDPEndpoint via CIM), `IProcessNameResolver`/`ProcessNameResolver`, `IServiceEnumerator`/`ServiceEnumerator`, `ServiceRegistryDetailReader`, `SoftwareRegistryRowBuilder`/`SoftwareRegistrySource`.
  - `WindowsPortInspector` implements Phase 2's `IPortInspector` contract — the interface's first real implementation.
  - `AddServerSleuthWindows()` DI registration (all abstractions + all 5 scanners against `IDiscoveryScanner`).
  - Phase 1 touch-up: `DiscoveryEntity.Version` changed `init`→`set` (needed for the OS scanner's post-construction version assignment); Core's 31 tests re-verified green after the change.
  - `test/ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests/Integration/WindowsDiscoverySmokeTest.cs` — the Phase 3 smoke test required by skill.md §28.
- **Windows APIs/mechanisms used**: `Environment`/`RuntimeInformation`, `Microsoft.Win32.Registry` (HKLM/HKCU, 64-bit and 32-bit/WOW6432Node views), `System.Diagnostics.Process`, WMI `Win32_Process` (incl. `GetOwner()`), WMI `MSFT_NetTCPConnection`/`MSFT_NetUDPEndpoint` (root\StandardCimv2 — chosen over netstat parsing per instructions), `System.ServiceProcess.ServiceController`.
- **Correlation implemented**: Only the low-level kind explicitly scoped to Phase 3 — `Port.OwningPid` carries the owning process id (from the same CIM query that produced the port, so it's not a separate correlation step but the same fact restated on the entity). No graph edges are built anywhere in Phase 3; that's Phase 5.
- **Permission handling**: Every scanner degrades to `PartiallySupported`/`AccessDenied` rather than throwing — verified both by fixture tests (fake registry reader returning `AccessDenied`) and by real behavior on this dev machine (346 processes discovered, some with `AccessDenied`/`Unavailable` metadata on privileged fields, overall scanner status `PartiallySupported`, not `Failed`).
- **Tests added**: 49 new tests in `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests` — pure/fixture tests for every scanner's mapping logic (`WindowsOsScannerTests`, `WindowsProcessScannerTests` + `WindowsProcessScannerScanAsyncTests`, `WindowsPortScannerTests`, `WindowsServiceScannerTests` + `ServiceRegistryDetailReaderTests`, `SoftwareRegistryRowBuilderTests` + `WindowsInstalledSoftwareScannerTests`) plus 5 real-machine integration tests (`WindowsScannerIntegrationTests`) plus the smoke test.
- **Build result** (actually executed 2026-08-23):
  ```
  dotnet restore → up to date, no errors
  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors (all 8 projects: Core, Infrastructure, Windows + their 3 test projects... )
  ```
- **Test result** (actually executed 2026-08-23):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            31 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  45 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:         49 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 125/125 passed
  ```
  Regression confirmed: Core (31) and Infrastructure (45) counts unchanged from Phase 2.
- **Real Windows smoke test result** (actually executed 2026-08-23, on the Windows 10 Pro dev machine — real registry/WMI/SCM, no mocks):
  ```
  ServerSleuth Windows Discovery

  OS:              Windows 10 Pro (X64)
  Processes:       346 (PartiallySupported)
  Services:        325 (Supported)
  Listening Ports: 104 (Supported)
  Installed Software: 79 (Supported)
  ```
  This same integration run caught and drove the fix for a real bug: `ProcessEnumerator` didn't catch `Win32Exception` from `Process.StartTime` on an access-denied process — fixed by adding that catch clause (see `SCANNERS.md`'s Windows Process Scanner section for detail). A fixture-only test suite would not have found this.
- **Known limitations**:
  - Per-process architecture (x86/x64) is not determined — no safe cross-platform managed API for this without P/Invoke; every `Process` entity currently reports `EntityArchitecture.Unknown`.
  - `WindowsProcessScanner`'s WMI owner lookup (`GetOwner()`) is one WMI call per process — a real per-scan cost on machines with many processes; acceptable now (correctness-first per skill.md §51), flagged for Phase 10 hardening/performance review.
  - Service `FailureActions` (Recovery Configuration) is detected as present/absent only — its binary structure is not parsed (skill.md marks this "if technically feasible"; judged out of Phase 3's scope).
  - `WindowsInstalledSoftwareScanner` deliberately does not merge across the three registry sources — see the ARCHITECTURE.md decisions log for why this was kept as Phase 5's job.
  - No scanner registry/plugin system — `AddServerSleuthWindows()` is a minimal DI registration, not the real orchestration layer (Phase 9).
- **Next phase**: Phase 4 — Windows Enterprise Components (IIS, Application Pools, COM/ActiveX, Scheduled Tasks, Certificates, Runtime/SDK detection, DLL/native dependency discovery, Configuration discovery). Not started — stopping here per instructions to report before continuing.

## Phase 4A — IIS Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/IIS/`: `IIisConfigurationProvider`/`IisConfigurationProvider` (loads `Microsoft.Web.Administration.dll` via late-bound `Assembly.LoadFrom`+`dynamic`, never a compile-time reference), raw DTOs (`IisSiteRow`, `IisBindingRow`, `IisApplicationRow`, `IisAppPoolRow`, `IisSnapshot`, `IisProbeResult`, `IisAvailability`), and `IisScanner` (`windows-iis-scanner`) with its `internal static BuildEntities` pure mapper.
  - `AddServerSleuthWindows()` extended to register `IIisConfigurationProvider` and `IisScanner`.
  - `WindowsDiscoverySmokeTest` extended to include the IIS section (IIS Installed / Status / Sites / Applications / Application Pools).
  - New real-machine integration test covering all four valid `IisScanner` outcomes (`NotInstalled`/`AccessDenied`/`Supported`/`PartiallySupported`).
- **Tests**: 17 new tests — `IisScannerBuildEntitiesTests` (11, pure mapping), `IisScannerScanAsyncTests` (5, fake provider), plus 1 real-machine integration test.
- **Build result** (actually executed 2026-08-23):
  ```
  dotnet restore → up to date, no errors
  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors
  ```
- **Test result** (actually executed 2026-08-23):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            31 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  45 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:         66 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 142/142 passed
  ```
  Regression confirmed: Core (31) and Infrastructure (45) unchanged; Windows grew 49→66 (+17, exactly the new IIS tests).
- **Real IIS smoke test result** (actually executed 2026-08-23, Windows 10 Pro dev machine, W3SVC running, IIS Management components present):
  ```
  IIS Installed:      Yes
  IIS Status:         AccessDenied
  Sites:              0
  Applications:       0
  Application Pools:  0
  ```
  This is a genuine, correctly-handled result, not a shortfall: the shell this session runs under is non-elevated, and IIS's `redirection.config` denies non-admin reads on this specific machine — independently confirmed by running plain, non-elevated `Get-IISAppPool` in PowerShell, which failed with the identical `UnauthorizedAccessException`. `IisScanner` reported `ScannerStatus.AccessDenied` with a permission-flagged `DiscoveryError`, never crashed, and never misreported `NotInstalled` — exactly the behavior skill.md §11 requires. The pure-mapping tests (`IisScannerBuildEntitiesTests`) independently prove the Site→Application→ApplicationPool mapping logic itself is correct using synthetic data, so the `Supported` code path is verified even though this particular execution context couldn't reach it.
- **Known limitations**:
  - IIS discovery requires `Microsoft.Web.Administration.dll` to be present (IIS Management components installed) — a bare WWW-service-only install without management components would report `NotInstalled`.
  - Certificate binding detail is thumbprint/store-name only — full certificate metadata is Phase 4C's job.
  - `WebSite`'s singular Protocol/HostName/Port fields reflect only the first binding (pre-existing Phase 1 model shape); every binding is still fully captured in `Bindings` + per-index metadata.
  - No graph edges built — `Application.ComponentEntityIds` carries the low-level site/pool relationship data for Phase 5 to turn into real edges.
- **Next phase**: Phase 4B (per the operating instructions' Phase 4 breakdown — COM/ActiveX, Scheduled Tasks next). Not started — stopping here per instructions to report before continuing.

## Phase 4B — COM / ActiveX Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:

- **Bug found and fixed during this finalization pass**: `WindowsComScanner.ScanAsync` counted a whole-registry-source `AccessDenied` toward `ScannerStatus` but never recorded a `DiscoveryError` for it — caught by the "Test 4" scenario (`ScanAsync_OneWholeSourceAccessDenied_KeepsOtherSourcesAndReportsPartial`) failing on first run. Fixed by adding the source-level denial to the same `partialFailures` list used for per-CLSID denials (`src/ServerSleuth.Windows/COM/WindowsComScanner.cs`); re-verified passing.

- **Known limitations**:

## Phase 4C — Windows Scheduled Tasks + Certificates

Recursive folder traversal including hidden tasks. `ScriptPathExtractor` pure helper for script-host argument parsing.

## Phase 4D — Windows Runtime / SDK Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Runtimes/`: `IRuntimeDetector` contract, `RuntimeDetectionRow`/`RuntimeDetectionResult`/`RuntimeEntityKind` DTOs, `RuntimeEntityBuilder` (one shared generic mapper), `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner` (`windows-runtime-discovery-scanner`, orchestrates all detectors with per-detector failure isolation), `IExecutableLocator`/`ExecutableLocator` (shared PATH + known-directory resolution), `ArchitectureHint` (cheap path-based heuristic), `RuntimeDetectionDefaults` (shared 10s command timeout).
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Runtimes/Detectors/`: 8 detectors — `DotNetFrameworkDetector` (registry Release-table mapping), `DotNetRuntimeDetector`/`DotNetSdkDetector` (`dotnet --list-runtimes`/`--list-sdks`, sharing `DotNetCliLocator`), `JavaDetector` (registry + command with path-matched merge and conflict preservation), `PythonDetector` (`py -0p` + known-directory scan, every interpreter independently confirmed), `NodeDetector` (Node.js + npm as separate entities), `PhpDetector`, `GoDetector` (with redacted GOROOT/GOPATH).
  - `AddServerSleuthWindows()` extended with `IExecutableLocator`, all 8 `IRuntimeDetector`s, and `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner`.
  - `WindowsDiscoverySmokeTest` and `WindowsScannerIntegrationTests` extended with the runtime/SDK section.
  - No Core changes needed — `Runtime` and `Sdk` entities (Phase 1) reused as-is.
- **Tests**: 49 new tests — `RuntimeEntityBuilderTests` (11), `DotNetFrameworkDetectorTests` (8), `DotNetRuntimeAndSdkDetectorTests` (4), `JavaDetectorTests` (6), `PythonDetectorTests` (4), `NodePhpGoDetectorTests` (8), `RuntimeDiscoveryScannerTests` (5), plus 1 real-machine integration test. One test-authoring mistake was caught and fixed during this same session (see below).
- **Test-writing bug caught and fixed**: the first version of `DotNetFrameworkDetectorTests` incorrectly expected a Release value *above* all known thresholds to fall back to "unknown" — it actually correctly maps to the highest known version (4.8.1, .NET Framework's final release) per Microsoft's own `>=`-threshold methodology. Fixed by splitting into two correct tests: below-lowest-threshold (genuinely unknown → raw fallback + ConflictNote) and above-highest-threshold (correctly resolves to 4.8.1, no conflict).
- **Build result** (actually executed 2026-08-23):

  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors
  ```

- **Known limitations**:
  - Architecture detection is a path-based heuristic, not PE parsing (same rationale as Phase 4B).
  - A runtime installed in a genuinely non-standard location (not PATH, not a known directory) will not be found — reports `NotDetected` for that family, not a false negative disguised as an error.
  - Java vendor detection recognizes only a fixed set of textual markers; an unrecognized vendor's `Edition` is simply absent.
- **Next phase**: Phase 4E per the user's Phase 4 breakdown. Not started — stopping here per the strict stop condition until Phase 4E is explicitly confirmed.

## Phase 4E-1 — Configuration Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Configuration/`: `ScanRoot`/`ScanRootCollector` (derives scan roots from IIS/Service/ScheduledTask entities, deduplicated by normalized path — never an arbitrary path), `ConfigurationFormat`/`ConfigurationFormatDetector`, `ConfigurationParseStatus`, `StructuralValidator` (JSON via `System.Text.Json`, XML via XXE-hardened `XmlReader`), `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer` (universal regex-based endpoint/UNC/env-var/runtime-marker/secret detection), `ExternalEndpointReference`/`DatabaseReference`/`UncPathReference`, `ConfigurationFileRow`, `WindowsConfigurationScanner` (`windows-configuration-scanner`).
  - DI changed: `IisScanner`, `WindowsServiceScanner`, `WindowsScheduledTaskScanner` now also registered as their own concrete types (shared singleton) so the Configuration scanner can reuse them directly; `WindowsConfigurationScanner` added.
  - **Two real, pre-existing gaps fixed in `SecretRedactor` (Phase 2 Infrastructure)**, surfaced by building this scanner: (1) quoted JSON-style keys (`"password": "x"`) weren't matched — a closing quote sat between the key and separator; (2) compound camelCase/snake_case keys (`clientSecret`, `accessToken`, `refreshToken`) weren't matched at all — the existing rules require the key to stand alone. Both fixed with 5 new regression tests in `SecretRedactorTests`.
  - `WindowsDiscoverySmokeTest` and `WindowsScannerIntegrationTests` extended with the Configuration Discovery section.
  - No Core changes needed — `Configuration` entity (Phase 1) reused as-is; richer fields live in `Metadata` per established convention.
- **Test-writing bugs caught and fixed during this session** (both before final validation, not after):
  - Connection-string detection originally required the value to immediately follow a `"connectionString"` key — broke on real ASP.NET Core `"ConnectionStrings": { "Default": "..." }` nesting. Fixed to match by value shape (a recognized ADO.NET key + `=`) instead of key adjacency, verified against a realistic nested-JSON test case.

## Phase 4E-2 — DLL / Native Dependency Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Windows/Binaries/`: `windows-binary-discovery-scanner` — `ScanRootCollector` (Phase 4E-1, reused) + new `ComScanRootCollector` (COM InprocServer32/LocalServer32 directories) → `BoundedDirectoryWalker` (depth ≤8, files ≤10,000/root, never follows reparse points) → `PeAnalyzer` (backed by the official `System.Reflection.Metadata` `PEReader` — PE32/PE32+, machine/architecture, subsystem, image size, timestamp, import table, managed-vs-native via CLR header presence) → `Core.Models.Dll` (Phase 1, unchanged).
  - New `SystemDirectoryExclusion` — excludes `%windir%`-rooted directories from full walks (see the performance fix below), while still checking specifically-referenced files directly.
  - Small Infrastructure addition: `FileEntryInfo.IsReparsePoint` (Phase 2's `IFileSystemReader`), needed for §20's reparse-point skip requirement.
  - `AddServerSleuthWindows()` extended: `WindowsComScanner` now also a shared concrete-type singleton (alongside the existing IIS/Service/Task ones); `IPeAnalyzer` and `WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner` added.
  - `WindowsDiscoverySmokeTest` and `WindowsScannerIntegrationTests` extended with the Binary Discovery section.
- **Real performance problem found and fixed during this session** (not glossed over): the first real-machine run took **6 minutes 39 seconds** and produced 29,461 binaries — several of Phase 4B's ~14,189 COM registrations resolve into `%windir%\System32`/`SysWOW64`, and fully walking those directories (thousands of unrelated OS files each) made per-root bounds insufficient in aggregate, violating the "discovery must be bounded, never a full-disk scan" requirement. Fixed with `SystemDirectoryExclusion`: any scan root under `%windir%` is skipped for full walking (the specific COM-referenced file is still checked directly, so §16's dangling-reference requirement is unaffected). Re-measured: **8.3 seconds**, 18,125 binaries — a >45x speedup, and the remaining count is genuine application/vendor content.
- **Tests**: 33 new Windows tests — `PeAnalyzerRealFileTests` (5, real files including this repo's own compiled DLL and real Windows system binaries), `BoundedDirectoryWalkerTests` (6), `ComScanRootCollectorTests` (5), `SystemDirectoryExclusionTests` (4), `WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScannerBuildEntityTests` (8), `WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScannerScanAsyncTests` (4, real temp files + fakes), plus 1 real-machine integration test.

  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors
  ```

## Phase 5A — Dependency Correlation Foundation

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - New project `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/` (plain `net8.0`, references `ServerSleuth.Core` only — no `ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` dependency, keeping correlation platform-agnostic for future Linux discovery).
  - `Correlation/CorrelationContext` — identity resolution: `ById` index plus Dll-by-normalized-path and Certificate-by-normalized-thumbprint indexes, built once per run.
  - `Correlation/WindowsPathNormalizer` and `Correlation/CommandLineReference` — the reusable path normalization and quoted/ambiguous command-line parsing strategies required for Service ImagePath / COM server path / binary path identity joins.
  - `Correlation/Rules/` — 10 `ICorrelationRule` implementations (see SCANNERS.md for the full table): Site→Hosts→Application, Application→Uses→ApplicationPool, Application→Configures→Configuration, Application→Contains→Binary, Service→Runs→Binary, ScheduledTask→Runs→Binary, COM→References→Binary, IISBinding→Binds→Certificate, Binary→Imports→Binary (same-directory-only resolution), Configuration→References→Runtime.
  - `Correlation/CorrelationEngine` — orchestrates all 10 rules, validates every candidate (unresolved target / missing endpoint / self-edge / no-evidence all rejected with a diagnostic reason), and assembles a `Core.Graph.DependencyGraph` (Phase 1, unchanged — its existing `AddEdge` duplicate-merge logic already implemented exactly what skill.md §13 required).
  - `Correlation/Diagnostics/CorrelationDiagnostics` — candidates-evaluated/edges-created/duplicates-merged counters plus a full list of rejected candidates with reasons.
  - `Core.Enums.DependencyEdgeType` extended with `Contains`, `Imports`, `Configures` (the existing enum already covered `Hosts`/`Runs`/`Binds`/`Uses`/`DependsOn`/`References`).

They remain visible as `Configuration.DetectedDependencyReferences` metadata only, pending a future phase.

## Phase 5B — Application Boundary & Cross-Scanner Correlation

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `Core.Boundaries.ApplicationBoundary` (new Core model — `Id`/`Name`/`MemberEntityIds`/`Evidence`/`Confidence`/`Reason`), deliberately not a reuse of `Core.Models.Application` (which stays IIS-site-scoped, unchanged).
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Correlation/Boundaries/`: `WorkloadAnchorKind`/`WorkloadAnchor` (one anchor per IIS Application, or per Service/ScheduledTask with a resolvable executable path), `BoundaryCandidate`, `BoundaryDiagnostics` (8 categories per skill.md §20), `BoundaryAnalysisResult`, and the orchestrating `ApplicationBoundaryEngine`.
  - `ApplicationBoundaryEngine.Analyze(entities, graph)` reuses Phase 5A's `CorrelationContext` and consumes its `DependencyGraph` as evidence (never re-derives identity or re-scans anything): membership comes from existing `Contains`/`Configures`/`Runs` edges plus a direct (not rediscovered) read of `Configuration.Metadata["OwnerEntityId"]` for Service/Task-owned configuration files that Phase 5A's Application-only rule never covered.
  - Cross-workload merge: Service/Task anchors are grouped by their shared `Runs` target; a group of exactly 2 merges into one boundary at High confidence (skill.md §7); a group of 3 or more is treated as common/shared infrastructure and is never merged, only recorded as a `SharedBinary` diagnostic (skill.md §8) — this specifically prevents multiple unrelated services that happen to share a host executable (e.g. `svchost.exe`) from being incorrectly lumped into one workload.
  - Common-parent-directory pairs are recorded as `AmbiguousCandidate` diagnostics, never merged (skill.md §9); name similarity has no merge code path at all (skill.md §10).
  - Boundary identity is deterministic: `boundary:{anchorId}` for a single anchor, `boundary:{sortedAnchorId1}+{sortedAnchorId2}` for a merged pair (ids sorted ordinally before joining).
- **Tests**: 13 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Correlation/Boundaries/` — `ApplicationBoundaryEngineTests` (10: IIS root ownership, service/task shared-executable merge, name-prefix non-merge, common-dll unresolved-ownership, common-parent ambiguous-candidate, 3-way shared-binary non-merge, determinism, no-executable-path unresolved, no-duplicate-membership, merged-evidence-contains-both-anchors) and `NegativeBoundaryFixtureTests` (3 — the two required negative fixtures from skill.md §24: two apps both containing a same-named `Common.dll` never cross-link, and two workloads under a shared parent directory never auto-merge; plus an independent-merges-don't-interfere case).

  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors
  ```

## Phase 5C — Dependency Expansion

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `Core.Boundaries.DerivedWorkloadDependency` (new Core model — `BoundaryId`/`TargetEntityId`/`Type`/`Confidence`/`Evidence`/`DerivedFrom`), deliberately not a `DependencyEdge` since `ApplicationBoundary` isn't a graph node.
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Correlation/Expansion/`: `ExternalDependencyKinds` (Database/Redis/ExternalApi/FileShare/LDAP/SMTP/Unknown), `ExternalDependencyExtractor` + `ExternalDependencyIdentity` (deterministic, case-folded, never-guesses-a-missing-port identity), `ExpansionDiagnostics`, `DependencyExpansionResult`, and `DependencyExpansionEngine`.
  - `DependencyExpansionEngine.Expand(entities, graph, boundaries)`: clones the Phase 5A graph (never mutates the original), materializes `ExternalDependency` entities from Configuration's already-detected metadata with cross-file deduplication, adds `Configuration --REFERENCES--> ExternalDependency` edges, adds `Configuration --REFERENCES--> Runtime` edges only when an explicit target-framework version is present (matched by major-version prefix against installed `Runtime.Version` values — never selecting one among several installed majors when only a bare family marker exists), and produces boundary-level `DerivedWorkloadDependency` records for Certificates (Application→Site→`BINDS_TO`→Certificate), COM components (direct membership or `IMPORTS`-chain only — no blanket association), and `ExternalDependency` entities (via a member Configuration's `REFERENCES` edge).
  - **Genuine data-contract blocker found and fixed narrowly, not worked around**: Configuration entities never carry raw file text, so Phase 5C could not recover an explicit runtime version (e.g. `net8.0`) from anything Phase 4E-1 previously emitted — only a bare family marker existed. Fixed with one additive regex in `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer` (`RuntimeVersionReferences`, anchored to a `TargetFramework` key) plus three previously-computed-but-never-persisted metadata keys (`Endpoint{i}.Path`, `Database{i}.Port`, `NetworkPath{i}.Path`). Both changes are purely additive — verified by running the full 299-test Windows suite (up from 294) with zero failures.
- **Tests**: 30 new tests — `ExternalDependencyIdentityTests` (5), `ExternalDependencyExtractorTests` (7), `DependencyExpansionEngineTests` (13, including the required 200-unrelated-COM-registration negative stress test and the multi-runtime-version negative test), and `ConfigurationContentAnalyzerTests` grew by 5 (TFM detection).

## Phase 5D — Dependency Graph Validation

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Correlation/Validation/`: `ValidationSeverity`, `ValidationFinding`, `OrphanClassification`/`OrphanFinding`, `CycleClassification`/`CycleFinding`, `GraphValidationSummary`, `GraphValidationResult`, and the orchestrating `GraphValidator`.
  - `GraphValidator.Validate(entities, expansion, boundaries)` — read-only over the Phase 5C `DependencyExpansionResult` and Phase 5B `ApplicationBoundary[]`; never calls `DependencyGraph.AddNode`/`AddEdge`. Implements: Node Integrity (empty Id/Type, duplicate Id in the raw entity list), Edge Integrity (dangling source/target, invalid relationship type, missing evidence), Duplicate Edge Detection, Evidence Validity (relationship-shape-appropriate evidence type expected per edge kind), Provenance Integrity + Confidence Escalation (every `DerivedWorkloadDependency`'s confidence checked against its own boundary's), Confidence Consistency (no confidence claim with zero evidence), Self-Edge Detection (`InvalidSelfEdge` vs `PotentialLegitimateSelfReference` for `DependsOn`), Orphan Analysis (Expected/Potential/Unresolved by entity type), Cycle Detection (iterative Tarjan SCC, Strong/Weak classification), Unresolved Dependency Analysis (`MissingBinary` — file doesn't exist — vs `UnresolvedBinary` — no target entity at all), and domain-specific checks for ExternalDependency identity conflicts, unresolved Certificate bindings, unresolved/dangling COM references, and Runtime version-match consistency.
  - No Core model changes, no scanner changes — this phase validates Phase 5A/5B/5C's existing output shapes without adding to them.

  - Confidence-escalation checking validates `derived.Confidence <= boundary.Confidence` (the invariant Phase 5C's own "weakest link" rule is supposed to guarantee); it does not attempt to trace back to one specific originating graph edge when several could plausibly be the source.
  - Evidence-shape validation ("Evidence Validity") is a soft, `Warning`-level coherence check for a fixed set of well-known relationship shapes (COM/Service/Task/Binding/Import/Configuration) — it does not attempt to validate every possible edge-type/evidence-type combination.

## Phase 6A — Linux Foundation Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - New project `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/` (plain `net8.0`; references `ServerSleuth.Core` + `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` only, no `ServerSleuth.Windows`), plus `test/ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests/`, both added to the solution.
  - `LinuxOsScanner` (`linux-os-scanner`): `/etc/os-release` (primary, `OsReleaseParser`) with `/proc/sys/kernel/{hostname,osrelease,ostype}` fallback; `uname -m` (fixed executable/argument, via `IProcessRunner`) for architecture only. Produces `Server`/`OperatingSystem` (Core, Phase 1, unchanged).
  - `LinuxProcessScanner` (`linux-process-scanner`): `/proc/<pid>/{status,cmdline,exe}` via `IProcProvider`/`LinuxProcProvider`. Bounded to `/proc`'s own numeric subdirectories via `IFileSystemReader.EnumerateDirectories`; the `exe` symlink resolved via `FileInfo.LinkTarget`. Handles access-denied, mid-scan-exited, malformed-entry, zombie, and kernel-thread processes without exception.
  - `LinuxPortInspector` (`linux-port-scanner`): the second real implementation of Phase 2's `IPortInspector`, reading `/proc/net/{tcp,tcp6,udp,udp6}` (`ProcNetParser` — verified IPv4 and IPv6 hex-address decoding) and resolving socket ownership via `/proc/<pid>/fd/*` symlinks (`SocketOwnershipResolver`) — never `netstat`/`ss`/`lsof`, never guessed from a process's command line.
  - `LinuxSystemdServiceScanner` (`linux-systemd-service-scanner`): `systemctl list-units --output=json` + `systemctl show <unit>` (both machine-readable; never `systemctl status` prose). `ExecStartParser` extracts the executable path from systemd's own `{ path=... }` shape. Read-only — never `start`/`stop`/`restart`/`enable`/`disable`/`reload`, never a unit-file write.
  - `AddServerSleuthLinux()` DI registration (independent of `AddServerSleuthWindows()`), registering all four scanners plus their supporting abstractions.
- **Tests**: 83 new tests, all fixture/fake-based (deterministic, no real `/proc`/`systemctl` needed on this Windows dev machine) — `OsReleaseParserTests` (4), `LinuxArchitectureMapperTests` (7), `LinuxOsScannerBuildEntitiesTests` (4), `LinuxOsScannerScanAsyncTests` (3), `ProcStatusParserTests` (5), `LinuxProcessScannerBuildEntityTests` (5), `LinuxProcessScannerScanAsyncTests` (4), `LinuxProcProviderTests` (6), `ProcNetParserTests` (5), `SocketOwnershipResolverTests` (3), `LinuxPortInspectorTests` (5), `LinuxPortScannerTests` (4), `SystemctlKeyValueParserTests` (3), `ExecStartParserTests` (3), `LinuxSystemdServiceScannerBuildEntityTests` (6), `LinuxSystemdServiceScannerScanAsyncTests` (6), `SystemctlProviderTests` (5), `ServiceCollectionExtensionsTests` (4), and `LinuxRealMachineSmokeTest` (1 — reports non-execution).

  - Per-process architecture (ELF header analysis) is not determined — deferred to a later Linux binary-discovery phase, mirroring Windows Phase 4E-2's scope boundary.
  - `SocketOwnershipResolver`'s and `LinuxProcProvider`'s `/proc/<pid>/exe`-and-`fd`-symlink-resolution code paths (the one part of this phase that must call real `FileInfo.LinkTarget` against real Linux magic symlinks) could only be tested via fakes in this session — genuine validation of that specific code path requires a real Linux host, honestly reported as not yet done.
- **Next phase**: Phase 6B (Linux packages/cron/Docker/runtimes/configuration discovery), Phase 7 (Risk Analysis), Migration Assessment, Reporting, CLI, GUI — none started, stopping here per the strict stop condition until explicitly confirmed.

## Phase 6B — Linux Package, Runtime, and Scheduled Job Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Packages/`: `IPackageManagerProvider` (`DpkgPackageProvider`, `RpmPackageProvider`, `ApkPackageProvider`) + `LinuxPackageScanner` (`linux-package-scanner`) — each manager queried directly and independently via a fixed, machine-readable format string (`dpkg-query -W -f=...`, `rpm -qa --queryformat ...`, `apk info -v`), never assuming the distribution from `/etc/os-release` first. `ApkPackageLineParser` splits name/version using Alpine's own `-r<release>` suffix convention. Produces `Core.Models.Package` (Phase 1, unchanged), deduplicated by `(Name, Version, Architecture)` within one manager's own results only — never merged across managers.
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Runtimes/`: `IExecutableLocator`/`LinuxExecutableLocator` (`:`-separated PATH, no extension matching — distinct from Windows's own same-named interface) + seven detectors (`DotNetRuntimeDetector`, `DotNetSdkDetector`, `JavaDetector`, `PythonDetector` — multi-interpreter, never merged, `NodeDetector` — covers Node+npm, `PhpDetector`, `GoDetector` — env vars redacted) + `LinuxRuntimeDiscoveryScanner` (`linux-runtime-discovery-scanner`).
  - **Architectural extraction (the significant cross-cutting change this phase)**: `IRuntimeDetector`, `RuntimeDetectionRow`, `RuntimeDetectionResult`, `RuntimeEntityKind`, `RuntimeEntityBuilder`, `RuntimeDetectionDefaults`, and the `dotnet` CLI output-line parser (previously `DotNetCliLocator.ParseLine`) moved from `ServerSleuth.Windows.Runtimes` to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Runtimes` — Linux cannot reference Windows, and duplicating Phase 4D's ~150 lines of entity-mapping logic would have violated the explicit "do not duplicate RuntimeEntityBuilder logic if it can be reused safely" instruction. `RuntimeEntityBuilder.Build` gained an **optional** `resolveSource` parameter (defaulting to a generic label) so the shared builder never needs to know about either platform's own evidence-source vocabulary; Windows's `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner` was updated with one line to pass its existing `EvidenceSources.WindowsRegistry`/`WindowsProcessApi` labels explicitly, preserving its exact prior entity output.
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Cron/`: `CronLineParser` (system 6-field / user 5-field / `@macro` forms, recognizing and skipping comments/blank lines/environment-variable-assignment lines), `CronCommandPathExtractor` (only ever extracts an explicit leading absolute path, never a PATH-resolved or shell-evaluated one), and `LinuxScheduledTaskScanner` (`linux-scheduled-task-scanner`) reading `/etc/crontab`, `/etc/cron.d/*`, the four `/etc/cron.{hourly,daily,weekly,monthly}` run-parts directories, and `/var/spool/cron/crontabs/*` (Debian) with `/var/spool/cron/*` (RHEL) as fallback. Reuses `Core.Models.ScheduledTask` (Phase 1, unchanged). Every raw command passes through `ISecretRedactor` before being stored.
  - `AddServerSleuthLinux()` extended with the three package providers, the Linux `IExecutableLocator`, the seven runtime detectors, and the two new scanners (now 7 scanners + 15 supporting registrations total).
- **A real, host-specific bug was found and fixed via an actual test failure, not assumed**: `System.IO.Path.GetDirectoryName`/`Path.Combine` silently rewrite `/` to `\` when the assembly happens to run on Windows — confirmed via a throwaway diagnostic test (`Path.GetDirectoryName("/usr/lib/jvm/.../bin/java")` returned `"\usr\lib\jvm\...\bin"` on this Windows dev machine). Since Linux discovery data is always `/`-shaped regardless of which OS performs the analysis, every Linux runtime detector now uses a small internal `LinuxPath` helper (forward-slash-only `GetDirectoryName`/`Combine`) instead of `System.IO.Path`.
- **Tests**: 65 new tests, all fixture/fake-based — `ApkPackageLineParserTests` (5), `DpkgPackageProviderTests` (3), `RpmPackageProviderTests` (4), `ApkPackageProviderTests` (2), `LinuxPackageScannerTests` (6), `DotNetRuntimeAndSdkDetectorTests` (4), `JavaDetectorTests` (3), `PythonDetectorTests` (3), `NodePhpGoDetectorTests` (6), `LinuxRuntimeDiscoveryScannerTests` (3), `CronLineParserTests` (10), `CronCommandPathExtractorTests` (4), `LinuxScheduledTaskScannerBuildEntityTests` (4), `LinuxScheduledTaskScannerScanAsyncTests` (7), plus `ServiceCollectionExtensionsTests` grew by 1 (now 5). The Integration smoke test was extended to cover all seven scanners (still correctly non-executing on this Windows host).

## Phase 6C — Linux Container Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Containers/`: `IContainerRuntimeProvider` implemented by `DockerContainerRuntimeProvider`/`PodmanContainerRuntimeProvider`, both thin wrappers over an internal shared `ContainerCliRuntimeProvider` (parametrized only by executable name — `docker`/`podman`) to avoid duplicating the read-only CLI/JSON parsing logic between the two runtimes. `LinuxContainerScanner` (`linux-container-scanner`) aggregates both providers' snapshots into discovery entities.
  - Container/Image/Volume/Network entities reuse the existing Phase 1 `Core.Models.Container` type via a `Type` string discriminator (`"Container"`/`"Image"`/`"Volume"`/`"Network"`) — no `DockerContainer`/`PodmanContainer` types were created, per the explicit instruction to keep the model generic.
  - Read-only CLI surface only: `ps -aq --no-trunc`, bulk `inspect <ids...>`, `images --no-trunc --format {{json .}}`, `volume ls --format {{json .}}`, `network ls -q --no-trunc` + bulk `network inspect <ids...>` (Podman equivalents via the same provider). All invocations go through `IProcessRunner` with discrete argument lists — never a shell string, never `sh -c`/`bash -c`, never `exec`/`run`/`start`/`stop`/`rm`/`pull`/`push`/`build`/`commit`/`kill`/`pause`/`unpause`.
  - `ContainerRuntimeAvailability` distinguishes `Supported`/`PartiallySupported`/`NotInstalled`/`AccessDenied`/`Unavailable` — classified from process start failure (`NotInstalled`) vs. stderr "permission denied" text (`AccessDenied`, verified against both Docker's and Podman's own phrasing) vs. any other non-zero exit (`Unavailable`). A `docker` executable merely existing on PATH is never treated as "supported" unless the daemon actually answered.
  - Environment variables: only variable **names** are ever stored (`EnvironmentVariableNames`), never values — the raw `KEY=value` line is split and the value is discarded before it reaches any entity or evidence. Labels, Entrypoint, and Command are passed through `ISecretRedactor` before being stored as metadata.
  - Mounts (bind/named volume/tmpfs), port mappings (including exposed-but-unpublished ports), volumes, and networks (Subnet/Gateway/attached container names via `IPAM.Config`) are captured as normalized facts, never as raw inspect JSON. No container filesystem, volume content, or `/var/lib/docker`/`/var/lib/containers` path is ever read.
  - Container/image/network identity is deterministic and runtime-qualified: `container:{runtime}:{id}`, `image:{runtime}:{id}`, `volume:{runtime}:{name}`, `network:{runtime}:{id}` — so coincidentally-identical Docker and Podman IDs never collide.
  - No `Container → host Process` relationship is inferred from PID/command line/port alone — left for Phase 5 correlation if explicit namespace evidence ever exists; Phase 6C only records the container's own reported PID (when exposed) as a fact.
  - `AddServerSleuthLinux()` extended with the two container runtime providers and the container scanner (now 8 scanners + 17 supporting registrations total).
- **Tests**: 36 new tests — `ContainerRuntimeProviderTests` (15: Docker/Podman container parsing incl. env/labels/mounts/ports, NotInstalled, Unavailable, AccessDenied for both runtimes' phrasing, empty-result Supported, multiple containers, missing image reference never guessed, image/volume/network listing parsing, malformed image line skipped, partial inspect failure recorded without losing other data), `LinuxContainerScannerBuildEntityTests` (12: running/exited status mapping, env names-only, label/entrypoint/command redaction, deterministic + runtime-distinct IDs, mount metadata, image/volume/network entity mapping), `LinuxContainerScannerScanAsyncTests` (5: Docker-only Supported, both-runtimes aggregation, neither-installed → NotInstalled, AccessDenied → PartiallySupported with permission error, images/volumes/networks all produce entities), `ContainerRuntimeNegativeSecurityTests` (4: asserts, via `FakeProcessRunner.Invocations`, that no invocation ever uses a forbidden subcommand, a shell executable, or any executable other than `docker`/`podman` themselves). `ServiceCollectionExtensionsTests` updated for the new scanner/provider counts. The Integration smoke test was extended to construct and run the container scanner alongside the existing seven (still correctly non-executing on this Windows host).

## Phase 6D — Linux Kubernetes Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Kubernetes/`: `linux-kubernetes-scanner`, backed by `IKubernetesProvider` → `KubectlKubernetesProvider` (provider-based, single-implementation — kubectl is the only acquisition path, unlike Phase 6C's two-runtime Docker/Podman split). All access goes through the currently configured kubectl context; no kubeconfig credentials (client certs/keys/tokens) are ever read or persisted.
  - Ten new portable Core entities (`Cluster`, `KubernetesNamespace`, `KubernetesNode`, `KubernetesPod`, `KubernetesWorkload`, `KubernetesService`, `KubernetesIngress`, `KubernetesConfigMap`, `KubernetesSecret`, `KubernetesPersistentVolumeClaim`, `KubernetesPersistentVolume`) — the first genuinely new Core models since Phase 1 (every prior Linux phase reused existing Phase 1 entities). Pod containers reuse the existing `Core.Models.Container` (Type = "KubernetesContainer") rather than a new type, per the instruction to prefer reuse over new provider-specific models.
  - 13 read-only, bulk (`--all-namespaces` where namespaced) `kubectl get <kind> -o json` queries plus `kubectl version -o json` (the availability gate) and a best-effort `kubectl config current-context` — never one call per object, never `exec`/`cp`/`apply`/`create`/`delete`/`patch`/`edit`/`rollout`/`port-forward`/`replace`, never a shell.
  - `KubernetesAvailability` (Supported/PartiallySupported/NotInstalled/AccessDenied/Unavailable) classified from actually querying the cluster via `kubectl version -o json` — `kubectl` merely existing on PATH is never treated as "Kubernetes available."
  - **Secret handling (CRITICAL)**: `KubernetesSecret` captures only Namespace/Type/Keys — the provider's `SecretItem.Data` DTO is typed `Dictionary<string, JsonElement>` specifically so a value is never materialized as a usable string, let alone stored; verified by a dedicated test asserting the resulting entity carries zero metadata.
  - ConfigMap text values pass through `ISecretRedactor` before being stored as metadata; binary values are never captured at all, only their key names.
  - PV hostPath handling: only the declared path string is recorded (`PvRow.HostPath`) — never read, never recursively scanned, never symlink-followed (trivially true, since parsing JSON text never touches any filesystem).
  - `AddServerSleuthLinux()` extended with `IKubernetesProvider`→`KubectlKubernetesProvider` and the new scanner (now 9 scanners, 27 total registrations).
- **A real bug found via test failure, not assumed**: a malformed top-level JSON response for one resource kind (e.g. `services`) was silently treated as "zero services found, scan still Supported" until a dedicated test caught it — fixed by having `GetList<T>` record a partial failure whenever deserialization returns `null`, degrading the scanner to `PartiallySupported` instead of silently reporting an empty, seemingly-successful result.
- **Tests**: 48 new tests — `KubectlKubernetesProviderTests` (21 — kubectl not installed, version-query AccessDenied/Unavailable, all-empty Supported with cluster version/context, multi-namespace with kube-system-derived cluster identifier, node role/version extraction, pod phase/IP/container parsing, Deployment/StatefulSet/DaemonSet parsing (three distinct JSON shapes), Service port/selector parsing, Ingress host/path/TLS-secret parsing, ConfigMap key+text-value parsing, Secret key-names-only parsing, PVC parsing, PV hostPath and NFS volume-source detection, malformed JSON skipped without failing the whole scan, one resource's failure not blocking others, 500-item large list, duplicate-named resources across namespaces), `LinuxKubernetesScannerBuildEntityTests` (16 — cluster/namespace/node/pod/pod-container/workload/service/ingress/configmap/secret/pvc/pv mapping, deterministic + cluster-scoped identity, ConfigMap redaction, Secret zero-metadata guarantee), `LinuxKubernetesScannerScanAsyncTests` (6 — NotInstalled/AccessDenied/Unavailable→Failed/Supported-aggregates-all-12-entity-kinds/PartiallySupported/provider-throws-never-propagates), `KubernetesNegativeSecurityTests` (4 — no forbidden subcommand, no shell executable, exactly one bulk call per resource kind, no secret value anywhere in metadata/evidence across a full scan). `ServiceCollectionExtensionsTests` updated for the new scanner/provider counts.

## Phase 6E — Linux Configuration Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - **Cross-cutting extraction (the significant architectural change this phase)**: the platform-neutral half of Windows's Phase 4E-1/4B configuration-analysis architecture — `ConfigurationFormat`, `ConfigurationParseStatus`, `ScanRoot`, `ConfigurationFileRow`, `ConfigurationAnalysisResult`, `ExternalEndpointReference`, `DatabaseReference`, `UncPathReference`, `ConfigurationFormatDetector`, `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer`, `StructuralValidator`, and the generic (Service/ScheduledTask-derived) half of `ScanRootCollector` — moved from `ServerSleuth.Windows.Configuration` to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Configuration`, mirroring Phase 6B's `RuntimeEntityBuilder` extraction. `ServerSleuth.Windows.Configuration.ScanRootCollector`/`WindowsConfigurationScanner` remain in Windows as thin wrappers that add IIS-specific roots and case-insensitive path deduplication on top of the shared pieces. **Verified**: the full pre-existing 299-test Windows suite passed unmodified after the move.
  - New shared types added specifically for Linux: `NetworkStorageReference` (NFS/CIFS), `ConfigurationFormat.EnvFile`, and `ConfigurationAnalysisResult.NetworkStorageReferences`/`UnixSocketReferences`. `ConfigurationContentAnalyzer` extended with bare `$VAR`, `${VAR:-default}`, Unix socket (`/run|/var/run|/tmp/*.sock`), CIFS (`//server/share`), and NFS (`server:/path`) detection — all additive, none of which change any existing Windows-oriented regex behavior.
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Configuration/`: `LinuxScanRootCollector` (well-known technology directories for systemd/nginx/Apache/PHP/MySQL/PostgreSQL/Docker/SSH, plus Service/ScheduledTask-derived application roots via a documented, deterministic bin/sbin-climb rule — see Known Limitations), `LinuxConfigurationTechnologyAnalyzer` (per-technology fact extraction for systemd units, nginx, Apache, PHP, MySQL/MariaDB, PostgreSQL, SSH — conservative line/regex parsing, never a full grammar parser), and `LinuxConfigurationScanner` (`linux-configuration-scanner`) tying it together, including a second pass that discovers systemd `EnvironmentFile=` references as additional explicit-file Configuration entities owned by the referencing Service.
  - Reuses `Core.Models.Configuration` (Phase 1, unchanged) — no `LinuxConfiguration` type introduced, per instructions.
  - `AddServerSleuthLinux()` extended: `LinuxSystemdServiceScanner`/`LinuxScheduledTaskScanner` are now also registered as their own concrete types (mirroring Windows's `IisScanner`/`WindowsServiceScanner`/`WindowsScheduledTaskScanner` pattern) so `LinuxConfigurationScanner` can share the same instances rather than re-registering second copies; `LinuxConfigurationScanner` itself registered as the 10th scanner.
- **A real, pre-existing gap found and fixed via an actual test failure, not assumed**: `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Security.SecretRedactor`'s key-value rules (Password/Token/Secret/ApiKey/etc.) anchored their leading boundary with a plain `\b`, which — because `_` is a word character — never matched the ubiquitous Linux `.env`/systemd convention of an underscore-prefixed key (`DB_PASSWORD=...`). Caught by a synthetic `.env` fixture test expecting redaction, per this phase's own explicit "`DB_PASSWORD=secret` must become `DB_PASSWORD=[REDACTED]`" instruction. Fixed by changing the leading anchor to `(?<![A-Za-z])` (reject only an immediately-preceding letter, not an underscore) — verified to introduce zero regressions across the full 758-test pre-fix baseline (all five test projects re-run green, plus the 10 new Linux-extension tests).
- **Tests**: 51 new tests — `ConfigurationContentAnalyzerLinuxExtensionsTests` (10, in `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests` — bare/braced/default-value env vars, Unix sockets, NFS/CIFS, never conflated with UNC or `https://`), `LinuxScanRootCollectorTests` (11 — well-known roots present, `/etc/cron.d` deliberately absent, bin/sbin-climb rule and its no-climb/never-climb-to-`/` guards, scheduled-task-derived roots, case-sensitive deduplication), `LinuxConfigurationTechnologyAnalyzerTests` (10 — systemd/nginx/Apache/PHP/MySQL/PostgreSQL/SSH parsing, multiple `EnvironmentFile=` lines, unrecognized source), `LinuxConfigurationScannerBuildEntityTests` (8), `LinuxConfigurationScannerScanAsyncTests` (8 — including the systemd-EnvironmentFile second-pass discovery), `LinuxConfigurationNegativeSecurityTests` (3, plus 1 DI test) — asserting no secret value ever appears in any entity/metadata/evidence across a realistic multi-file scan, and that the scanner has no process-execution-capable dependency at all (structurally incapable of running nginx/apachectl/php/mysql/psql or touching the Docker socket/Kubernetes API). `ServiceCollectionExtensionsTests` updated for the 10-scanner/30-service counts.

  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors

All 273 Linux tests completed in ~125ms total.
- **Known limitations**:
  - Application-root derivation only recognizes the `bin`/`sbin` executable-directory convention (climbing exactly one level); any other directory layout uses the executable's own directory as-is, never guessed further.
  - `/etc/cron.d` is deliberately never scanned as a Configuration root — Phase 6B's `LinuxScheduledTaskScanner` already owns those files as `ScheduledTask` entities, and scanning them again here would produce duplicate entities for the same file.
  - Per-technology parsers (nginx/Apache/systemd/MySQL/PostgreSQL/SSH) are conservative line/regex extractors, not full grammar parsers — an unusual or heavily macro-driven configuration (e.g. nginx `include`-based dynamic server blocks assembled at runtime) may not have every directive captured; the generic cross-format analysis (secrets/endpoints/database references/etc.) still runs regardless.
  - Docker's `/etc/docker/daemon.json` has no dedicated per-technology parser — it is plain JSON, so it is fully captured by the generic JSON structural validator and content analyzer alone.
  - No real-machine validation was performed for the new scanner — the development host is Windows; the smoke test (extended to cover all ten scanners) honestly reports this rather than faking it.
- **Next phase**: Not started. Linux Native Dependency Discovery, cross-platform integration, Risk Analysis, Migration Assessment, Reporting, and GUI remain explicitly out of scope until further confirmation.

## Phase 6F — Linux Native Dependency Discovery

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Linux/Native/`: a hand-written, minimal, read-only ELF32/ELF64 little-endian parser (`ElfParser`/`ILinuxElfParser`) covering exactly what the phase asked for — magic, EI_CLASS, EI_DATA, `e_machine`, program headers, the dynamic section, DT_NEEDED, DT_RPATH, DT_RUNPATH — and nothing more (no disassembler, no DWARF, no symbol analysis). Never throws on malformed/truncated input; every failure path is caught and classified as one of `Parsed`/`PartiallyParsed`/`UnsupportedEndian`/`NotAnElf`/`Truncated`/`MalformedElf`.
  - **This phase resolves the previously documented "per-runtime architecture detection remains out of scope" limitation** — `EntityArchitecture` gained a `RiscV` member, and machine-value mapping now covers x86/i386 (EM_386), x86_64 (EM_X86_64), ARM (EM_ARM), AArch64 (EM_AARCH64), and RISC-V (EM_RISCV); an unrecognized `e_machine` value is `Unknown` but the raw value is still preserved as metadata (`EM_<value>`), never silently discarded.
  - Big-endian ELF is recognized (`EI_DATA == 2`) but deliberately not parsed further — the parser stops immediately after reading `EI_CLASS`/`EI_DATA` and returns `UnsupportedEndian` with a diagnostic, rather than guessing at multi-byte fields whose byte order it doesn't decode.
  - `ILibraryResolver`/`LinuxLibraryResolver`: resolves each DT_NEEDED name through a fixed, documented five-tier order — explicit RPATH entries, then RUNPATH entries (both in file order, first existing candidate wins), then already-discovered binary paths sharing the exact filename (`Ambiguous` if more than one distinct path matches — never arbitrarily chosen), then a fixed list of well-known Linux library directories, then the optional `ldconfig` cache. `$ORIGIN` is the one RPATH/RUNPATH token expanded (to the importing binary's own directory); all resulting paths are lexically normalized so `..` can never walk above `/`, regardless of how many segments an ELF-supplied path contains.
  - `ILdconfigProvider`/`LdconfigProvider`: the only process ever invoked is `ldconfig -p` (never bare `ldconfig`, never used to rebuild/modify the cache); unavailability or unparseable output simply yields an empty cache and never fails the scan. `ldd` was **not** implemented at all, per the phase's explicit preference.
  - `LinuxNativeDependencyScanner` (`linux-native-dependency-scanner`) gathers executable paths only from already-discovered evidence — re-running `LinuxProcessScanner`/`LinuxSystemdServiceScanner`/`LinuxScheduledTaskScanner`/`LinuxRuntimeDiscoveryScanner` (all four also now registered as their own concrete types in DI, extending the pattern Phase 6E introduced for the systemd/cron scanners) purely to obtain `Process.Path`/`Service.ExecutablePath`/`ScheduledTask.Action`/`Runtime.Path`/`Sdk.Path` — never a filesystem-wide ELF search. Deduplicates by exact (case-sensitive) path so the same binary is never parsed twice even when multiple entities reference it; every referencing entity's ID is preserved as an owner.
  - Reuses `Core.Models.Dll` (Phase 1, unchanged) for every discovered native binary (`Type = "NativeBinary"`) — no `LinuxBinary`/`ElfBinary` type introduced, per instructions. `Architecture` (already on the shared `DiscoveryEntity` base, used by Windows PE analysis too) carries the ELF-derived value; ELF class/endianness/machine/RPATH/RUNPATH/per-dependency resolution facts are recorded as metadata, mirroring exactly how Windows's PE scanner records its own header facts.
  - `AddServerSleuthLinux()` extended with `ILinuxElfParser`→`ElfParser`, `ILibraryResolver`→`LinuxLibraryResolver`, `ILdconfigProvider`→`LdconfigProvider`, and the new scanner (now 11 scanners, 36 total registrations).
- **Tests**: 59 new tests, all against deterministic synthetic fixtures — `ElfParserTests` (19, using a hand-written `SyntheticElfBuilder` producing real, structurally valid ELF32/ELF64 byte arrays with an identity virtual-address mapping so DT_STRTAB always translates correctly: ELF32/ELF64 parsing, all five recognized machine mappings plus unknown-machine preservation, big-endian never crashes, malformed magic, truncated-before-header, corrupted program-header-offset never throws, no-dynamic-section (static binary), single/multiple/large (200-entry) DT_NEEDED chains, DT_RPATH/DT_RUNPATH extraction and independence, determinism across repeated parses of the same bytes), `LinuxLibraryResolverTests` (12 — RPATH-before-RUNPATH precedence, `$ORIGIN` expansion, path-traversal attempt never escapes/never throws, known-binary single-match vs. multi-match-Ambiguous, well-known-location fallback, ldconfig-only resolution, not-found never fabricates a path, AccessDenied distinct from NotFound, deterministic repeated calls), `LdconfigProviderTests` (4), `LinuxNativeDependencyScannerBuildEntityTests` (10), `LinuxNativeDependencyScannerScanAsyncTests` (10 — including cross-entity path deduplication, known-binary-tier resolution against another discovered binary, unresolvable dependency never fabricated, AccessDenied/NotFound/ldconfig-unavailable never fail the whole scan), `LinuxNativeDependencyNegativeSecurityTests` (3 — the only process ever invoked across a full discovery run is `ldconfig -p`, the scanner's own dependency graph has no other execution-capable surface, the analyzed binary itself is never executed). `ServiceCollectionExtensionsTests` updated for the 11-scanner/36-service counts.
- **Build result** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  dotnet restore → up to date, no errors
  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors
  ```
- **Test result** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```

  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          332 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped

  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 868/868 passed

  Regression confirmed: Core (31), Analysis (146), Infrastructure (60), Windows (299) unchanged; Linux grew 273→332 (+59, exactly the new Phase 6F tests). Full 809-test Phase 6E baseline intact. (One Windows real-machine performance-timing test flagged a transient 5m42s duration on the first full-suite run under concurrent load; re-run in isolation immediately after, it completed in 7s — confirmed environmental, not a regression, and the second full-suite run was fully green with no isolation needed.)
- **Real Linux smoke test status**: **Not executed** — development host is Windows (confirmed via `RuntimeInformation.IsOSPlatform(OSPlatform.Linux)` at actual run time this session). The extended smoke test (now covering all 11 scanners) printed the same non-execution message and returned without asserting any fabricated Linux data.
- **Performance**: no filesystem-wide ELF search of any kind; only already-discovered executable paths are analyzed, each exactly once regardless of how many entities reference it. A 200MB-per-file analysis cap bounds memory usage against a pathological input (documented, not reflective of any real Linux binary size norm). Library resolution never enumerates a directory's contents — only ever checks the existence of one exact candidate path per tier/entry. All 332 Linux tests completed in ~146ms total.
- **Known limitations**:
  - Only `$ORIGIN` is expanded in RPATH/RUNPATH entries — `$LIB`/`$PLATFORM` (rarer dynamic-linker tokens) are not substituted and are left as literal text, which will simply fail to resolve rather than being guessed at.
  - The well-known library directory list is a fixed, documented set (`/lib`, `/lib64`, `/usr/lib`, `/usr/lib64`, and the Debian/Ubuntu multiarch `x86_64`/`aarch64` triplet directories) — a distribution using a different multiarch triplet (e.g. `armv7l`) will not find libraries that live only in that triplet's directory unless RPATH/RUNPATH, known-binary, or ldconfig resolution already covers it.
  - `ldd` was deliberately not implemented, per the phase's own preference — DT_NEEDED parsing is the sole authoritative dependency source.
  - No real-machine validation was performed for the new scanner — the development host is Windows; the smoke test (extended to cover all eleven scanners) honestly reports this rather than faking it.
- **Next phase**: Not started. Cross-platform integration, Risk Analysis, Migration Assessment, Reporting, and GUI remain explicitly out of scope until further confirmation.

## Phase 6G — Cross-Platform Integration & Real Linux Validation

- **Status**: Complete
- **Implemented**:
  - `src/ServerSleuth.Core/Orchestration/`: `IDiscoveryScannerRegistry`/`DiscoveryScannerRegistry` (deterministic, `Id`-ordinal-sorted catalog of every registered `IDiscoveryScanner` — never DI-container or dictionary enumeration order) and `IDiscoveryEngine`/`DiscoveryEngine` (runs every registered scanner once, catches an unhandled exception from any single scanner and degrades that one scanner to `Failed` without aborting the rest, and produces one deterministic `AggregateDiscoveryResult`). Neither type references `ServerSleuth.Windows` or `ServerSleuth.Linux` — platform availability is entirely a function of which composition-root method (`AddServerSleuthWindows()`/`AddServerSleuthLinux()`) was called, never an `if (OperatingSystem.IsWindows())` branch inside the orchestrator.
  - `AggregateDiscoveryResult`: entities/errors (flattened, scanner-registry order), the full per-scanner `ScannerResults` list, a `ScannerStatuses` lookup, and engine-level `Diagnostics` — a scanner reporting `AccessDenied` remains fully visible, never silently dropped.
  - `AddServerSleuthDiscoveryEngine()` (new `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.DependencyInjection` extension, separate from `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` since it must run after the platform-specific registration methods have already populated `IDiscoveryScanner`).
  - **New `ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests` project** (targets `net8.0-windows` so it can reference both `ServerSleuth.Windows` and `ServerSleuth.Linux` — confirmed this cross-compiles and runs correctly on real Linux too): `CrossPlatformCompositionTests` (composes `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()+AddServerSleuthWindows()+AddServerSleuthLinux()+AddServerSleuthDiscoveryEngine()` in one `ServiceCollection` and proves the resulting registry is exactly the union of both platforms' own scanner counts, with no duplicates, correct per-scanner `PlatformSupport`, and deterministic ordering) and `CrossPlatformAnalysisFixtureTests` (a synthetic fixture with genuinely mixed Windows-shaped — IIS Application, Windows Service, Configuration, Runtime, managed `Dll`, Certificate — and Linux-shaped — systemd Service, Configuration, Runtime, native ELF `Dll`, `ExternalDependency` — entities, fed through the exact same `CorrelationEngine`→`ApplicationBoundaryEngine`→`DependencyExpansionEngine`→`GraphValidator` pipeline in one run).
  - **Two real, cross-platform correctness bugs found and fixed via genuine real-Linux execution** (WSL Ubuntu 24.04 LTS — see below), not assumed or simulated:
    1. `ServerSleuth.Analysis`'s `BinaryImportsBinaryRule`/`ApplicationBoundaryEngine` used raw `System.IO.Path.GetDirectoryName`/`Path.Combine` on already-`WindowsPathNormalizer`-canonicalized (backslash) paths — correct on a Windows host, silently broken on a POSIX host, since `System.IO.Path` interprets separators according to the *current* runtime OS, not the path's own origin. Fixed by adding host-OS-independent `WindowsPathNormalizer.GetDirectoryName`/`Combine`/`GetFileName` (hand-rolled backslash-only logic, mirroring Linux's own `LinuxPath` helper in the opposite direction) and updating both call sites plus the shared `EntityFactory` test fixture. Verified against the full pre-fix Windows Analysis-suite baseline (146/146 unchanged) before and after.
    2. `KubectlKubernetesProvider.Classify`/`ContainerCliRuntimeProvider.Classify` only checked `OperationStatus.StartFailed` to detect a missing executable, but the real `ProcessRunner` (confirmed by its own existing test) classifies a genuinely-missing executable as the more specific `OperationStatus.NotFound` — a distinction `FakeProcessRunner`'s hard-coded StartFailed default never exercised. This meant a real machine with `kubectl` genuinely absent reported `ScannerStatus.Failed` instead of the correct `NotInstalled`. Found on the real WSL Ubuntu instance (kubectl genuinely not installed) and fixed by checking both statuses; re-verified on the same real machine after the fix.
- **Tests**: 27 new tests — `DiscoveryScannerRegistryTests` (3), `DiscoveryEngineTests` (8, in `ServerSleuth.Core.Tests/Orchestration/`), `ServiceCollectionExtensionsTests` (2, in `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests/DependencyInjection/`), `CrossPlatformCompositionTests` (6) + `CrossPlatformAnalysisFixtureTests` (8) (in the new `ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests`). Plus 2 new `KubectlKubernetesProviderTests`/`ContainerRuntimeProviderTests` cases pinning the `OperationStatus.NotFound` fix against the exact status the real `ProcessRunner` produces.
