## Phase 7A — Evidence-Based Risk Engine

- **Architecture**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis/Risk/` — Models (`RiskFinding`, `RiskCategory`, `RiskSeverity`, `RiskAnalysisResult`), `RiskAnalysisContext` (immutable, pre-indexed view over already-produced Discovery/Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation output), `Rules/` (`IRiskRule` + twelve independently-testable rule classes), `Engine/RiskRuleEngine` (ordering/failure-isolation/evidence-enforcement/dedup/sort), `Diagnostics/RiskDiagnostics`. Provider/rule-based by construction — the engine itself contains no rule-specific logic. Full detail in `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s Phase 7A Addendum.
- **RiskFinding identity**: `risk:{RuleId}:{SourceEntityId}:{normalized-related-ids}` (related-ids de-duplicated, ordinally sorted, comma-joined) — never a random GUID, so the same underlying risk produces the same Id across repeated runs.
- **Risk taxonomy** (closed, 12 categories): MissingDependency, AccessDenied, MissingBinary, MissingRuntime, Certificate, Service, ScheduledTask, Com, ExternalDependency, SharedInfrastructure, Configuration, GraphIntegrity.
- **Severity**: Critical/High/Medium/Low/Info, set directly by the rule that produced the finding — no numeric score anywhere in this phase.
- **Confidence**: derived from the confidence of the specific entity/edge/evidence that justified the finding — never asserted higher than what backs it (e.g. `ComDependencyRule` takes the lower of the COM registration's and the resolving edge's confidence).
- **Evidence invariant**: enforced by `RiskRuleEngine.Analyze` itself, not merely documented — a non-`Info` finding with zero evidence is dropped and recorded as an `EvidenceInvariantViolation` diagnostic rather than published unproven.
- **Twelve rules implemented**: RR1 MissingDependencyRule (unresolved binary import from `GraphValidationResult`), RR2 MissingBinaryRule (discovered-but-missing-on-disk binary with an actual dependent — Critical for a Service/ScheduledTask's own executable, High otherwise), RR3 AccessDeniedRule (Configuration/Dll AccessDenied — phrased strictly as uncertainty, never as "broken"; High if boundary member, else Medium), RR4 MissingRuntimeRule (explicit, version-bearing `RuntimeVersion:` reference with no matching installed Runtime/Sdk major version — never a bare family marker), RR5 CertificateExpiryRule (Critical if expired, High within 30 days, Medium within 90 days, no finding beyond that or for unknown/not-yet-valid expiry), RR6 ServiceDependencyRule (Critical), RR7 ScheduledTaskDependencyRule (High), RR8 ComDependencyRule (High, escalating to Critical only with explicit ApplicationBoundary membership — never by naming similarity), RR9 ExternalDependencyRule (FileShare/LDAP High, Database/Redis/ExternalApi Medium, unclassified kinds never flagged), RR10 SharedInfrastructureRule (Medium, reads Phase 5B's already-computed `BoundaryDiagnostics.SharedBinaries` — never re-derived), RR11 ConfigurationRiskRule (FileShare/NetworkStorage High, UnixSocket Low, EnvVar/Endpoint/Database Info), RR12 GraphIntegrityRule (converts only Error-severity `GraphValidator` findings — Warning/Info are never duplicated as migration risks).
- **Deduplication**: narrow, explicit opt-in — only findings sharing the exact same `Metadata["MissingBinaryEntityId"]` anchor merge; takes max severity, the highest of the contributing confidences, unions evidence (deduped by Type/Location/Detail), and records every contributing `RuleId` in `Metadata["ContributingRules"]` so provenance is never silently lost.
- **Diagnostics**: `RiskDiagnostics` tracks rules evaluated, findings created, findings deduplicated, rule failures (with exception type/message), and evidence invariant violations — one rule throwing is caught, recorded, and never aborts the run.
- **Security**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj` has exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Core`) and zero `PackageReference`s — confirmed by direct inspection, not inferred — so there is no reachable Windows/Linux/Infrastructure assembly and therefore no registry/service-controller/process/socket/systemd/Docker/Kubernetes API surface anywhere in the Risk layer; `RiskAnalysisContext` only ever reads the five already-produced artifacts passed into its constructor.
- **ERP Risk fixture** (`ErpRiskFixtureTests`, 10 tests): a synthetic ERP scenario with eight deliberate migration risks (missing worker executable, missing imported native dependency, expiring HTTPS certificate, external SQL Server dependency, external file share, unsatisfied explicit net8.0 requirement, a binary shared across three workloads, partially inaccessible configuration) run through the full Discovery→Correlation→Boundary→Expansion→Validation→Risk pipeline — each produces its expected category/severity, and unrelated healthy entities (site/pool/application, a healthy DLL, a far-from-expiry unreferenced certificate) produce zero findings.
- **Negative fixtures** (`NegativeRiskFixtureTests`, 8 tests): confirms zero findings for an orphan installed-but-unreferenced Runtime, an orphan far-from-expiry Certificate, an unused COM registration whose server resolves on disk, an unclassified/informational-only ExternalDependency kind, two Services with a shared parent directory but distinct executables (ambiguous-candidate, never merged), two same-named-but-differently-pathed DLLs across unrelated roots (never falsely treated as shared infrastructure), a family-only runtime marker without an explicit version, and AccessDenied metadata on an entity type (`Certificate`) the rule doesn't consider.
- **Determinism + no-mutation** (`RiskDeterminismTests`, 2 tests): a 220-ish-entity fixture run twice through the same `RiskRuleEngine`/`RiskAnalysisContext` produces identical finding Ids/severities/confidences/evidence/diagnostics both times; a separate test snapshots entity/graph-node/graph-edge/boundary/external-dependency/validation-finding counts and Ids before and after `Analyze` (run twice) and confirms none changed.
- **Performance** (`RiskPerformanceTests`, 1 test, actually executed): a synthetic in-memory graph of 5,081 entities / 10,258 edges (4,000 binaries — 250 deliberately missing, 150 Services + 60 ScheduledTasks, 700 Configurations, 120 ExternalDependencies, 50 Certificates, 1 Runtime) built directly (mirroring `GraphValidatorLargeGraphTests`' pattern, no CorrelationEngine re-run needed since only `RiskRuleEngine`'s own performance is being measured) — `RiskRuleEngine.Analyze` completed in well under the 10-second bound (full-suite run reported 446ms for this test alone; no filesystem/network access occurs anywhere in the Risk layer).
- **Tests**: 27 new tests — `SharedInfrastructureRuleTests` (3), `GraphIntegrityRuleTests` (3), `ErpRiskFixtureTests` (10), `NegativeRiskFixtureTests` (8), `RiskDeterminismTests` (2), `RiskPerformanceTests` (1), added on top of the pre-existing 204 Risk-layer tests (`RiskRuleEngineTests`, `RiskFindingIdentityTests`, and ten of the twelve rules' own dedicated test files) already present in `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Risk/` at the start of this session.
- **Build result** (actually executed 2026-08-24, on Windows):
  ```
  dotnet restore → up to date, no errors
  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors (all 11 projects)
  ```
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24, on Windows):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       231 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (204 pre-existing Risk/Correlation tests + 27 new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 982/982 passed
  ```
  Regression confirmed: no existing test was deleted or modified; the full pre-Phase-7A baseline (897 documented at the end of Phase 6G, actually 955 once the Risk layer already present at session start is counted — see Known Limitations) plus this session's 27 new tests is exactly 982.
- **Known Limitations**:
  - At the start of this session, `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Risk`'s core implementation (all twelve rules, the engine, the context, and 204 of the eventual 231 Risk-layer tests) was already present in the repository, fully built and passing, but entirely undocumented — no `PROGRESS.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` entry existed for it, and `CLAUDE.md` still described the repository as containing no source code at all. This phase's work was therefore gap-closing (missing rule tests, the ERP/negative fixtures, determinism/no-mutation/performance tests, and all documentation) against an already-correct implementation, not building the Risk engine from scratch; no defect was found in the pre-existing `Risk/` code during this session's review.
  - No Risk Scoring (0-100), aggregation, Migration Assessment, Reporting, GUI, or CLI hardening exists yet — explicitly deferred to Phase 7B and beyond per this phase's own strict-stop instruction.
  - The ERP risk fixture is synthetic (built the same way every other Analysis-layer fixture in this codebase is — no real server was scanned for Phase 7A); real-server risk output has not yet been exercised end-to-end.
- **Next phase**: Not started. Risk Scoring (Phase 7B), Migration Assessment, Reporting, GUI, and CLI production hardening remain explicitly out of scope until further confirmation.

## Phase 7B — Risk Scoring & Risk Aggregation

- **Architecture**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis/Risk/Aggregation/` — `RiskAggregator` (public entry point, consumes a `RiskAnalysisContext` + Phase 7A `RiskAnalysisResult`, produces a `RiskAggregationResult`), `ApplicationRiskAggregator`/`ServerRiskAggregator` (internal, build the two summary levels), `RiskSummaryCalculator` (internal, the single shared implementation of every metric both summary levels use, so they can never silently diverge). New models in `Risk/Models/`: `AggregateSeverity`, `RiskSummaryBase` (abstract, common fields), `ApplicationRiskSummary`, `ServerRiskSummary`, `RiskAggregationResult`. New `Risk/Diagnostics/RiskAggregationDiagnostics`. Pipeline position: Discovery→Correlation→Boundary→Expansion→Validation→Risk Analysis (7A)→**Risk Aggregation (7B)**. Nothing in Phase 7A (`Rules/`, `Engine/`, `RiskAnalysisContext`) was modified — 7B is a pure downstream consumer.
- **Aggregate severity policy**: `AggregateSeverity` — `None`/`Info`/`Low`/`Medium`/`High`/`Critical`, declared in ascending order so ordinal enum comparison is the deterministic ordering (never string sort). `RiskSummaryCalculator.ComputeOverallSeverity` implements the exact escalation table from skill.md (Phase 7B) §2/§20: the highest severity present among the scope's findings wins outright — a single Critical finding can never be averaged, diluted, or otherwise hidden by lower-severity findings; zero findings aggregates to `None`, a state no single `RiskFinding` can ever be in (`RiskSeverity` has no `None` value).
- **Application aggregation**: `ApplicationRiskAggregator.Build` groups findings by resolved `ApplicationBoundaryId` and builds one `ApplicationRiskSummary` per boundary that has at least one attributed finding — a boundary with zero attributed findings produces no summary at all (never an empty/`None` one). Boundary attribution (`RiskAggregator.ResolveBoundaryId`) is: the finding's own explicit `ApplicationBoundaryId` if the originating rule stamped one (today: `AccessDeniedRule`, `CertificateExpiryRule`, `ComDependencyRule`, `ExternalDependencyRule`), else a fallback lookup of the finding's `SourceEntityId` in the already-computed `RiskAnalysisContext.BoundaryIdByEntityId` map (Phase 5B's `ApplicationBoundaryEngine` output) — never invented from naming similarity.
- **Server aggregation**: `ServerRiskAggregator.Build` covers **every** finding — application-scoped and server-scoped alike — never dropping a finding merely because it resolved to no boundary. `ServerScopedFindingCount` reports how many findings in the total have no boundary attribution (platform-level AccessDenied, graph integrity, unresolved external dependencies, etc., per skill.md (Phase 7B) §13); `AffectedBoundaryCount` on the server summary equals the number of application summaries actually produced.
- **Impact metrics**: `AffectedEntityCount` = distinct entity Ids referenced across a scope's findings (`SourceEntityId` + every `RelatedEntityIds` entry, deduplicated) — purely from each finding's own explicit Ids, never naming similarity. `CategoryCounts` groups findings by `RiskCategory`, omitting categories with zero findings in scope rather than listing them as 0.
- **Shared infrastructure handling**: `SharedDependencyCount` counts findings whose `Category == SharedInfrastructure` — deliberately never raises `OverallSeverity` on its own; impact (how many workloads are affected) and severity (how bad the risk is) are kept as two separate, independently-computed concepts, per skill.md (Phase 7B) §9.
- **Confidence aggregation**: `AggregateConfidence` = the highest single `Confidence.Value` among a scope's contributing findings — i.e. "the confidence of the single most-confident contributing finding" — never a sum/average/Bayesian combination that could push the aggregate above what any one piece of evidence individually supports (skill.md (Phase 7B) §10). `Confidence(0.0)` when a scope has zero findings. Verified with a dedicated test asserting several low-confidence findings never dilute/inflate the aggregate away from the single strongest one.
- **Top-risk ordering**: `RiskSummaryCalculator.ComputeTopRisks` — Severity descending, then per-finding Impact (distinct entities touched) descending, then Confidence descending, then `RuleId` ordinal, then `Id` ordinal — capped at `TopRisksLimit = 10` (a fixed, documented constant, never arbitrary dictionary/registration order). Every tie-break level verified independently by a dedicated test that isolates each key in turn.
- **Deduplication compatibility**: Aggregation never re-deduplicates findings itself — Phase 7A's `RiskRuleEngine` already merged same-anchor findings (e.g. `MissingBinaryRule` + `ServiceDependencyRule` on the same missing executable) before Aggregation ever sees them; `RiskAggregationDiagnostics.FindingsDeduplicatedUpstream` simply carries the Phase 7A dedup count forward for provenance, and a dedicated test confirms it equals `RiskAnalysisResult.Diagnostics.FindingsDeduplicated` exactly.
- **Diagnostics**: `RiskAggregationDiagnostics` — `FindingsProcessed`, `FindingsDeduplicatedUpstream`, `ApplicationSummariesCreated`, `ServerLevelFindingCount`, `TopRisksSelected`, `UnresolvedOwnershipCount`, `Issues`. A dedicated test confirms internal consistency (e.g. `ApplicationSummariesCreated == ApplicationSummaries.Count`, `ServerLevelFindingCount == ServerScopedFindingCount == UnresolvedOwnershipCount`).
- **No score, no readiness claim**: no `0-100` score, aggregation formula, business-impact multiplier, or `MigrationReady`/`MigrationBlocked`/readiness-score concept exists anywhere in this phase — `AggregateSeverity` plus the explicit counts/metrics above are the entire output, exactly as skill.md (Phase 7B) §2/§14-15 requires. Those concepts remain reserved for a future Migration Assessment phase.
- **Security**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj` still has exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Core`) and zero `PackageReference`s after adding the Aggregation layer — confirmed by direct inspection; the new code pulls in no filesystem/registry/process/network capability. `RiskAggregator.Aggregate` only ever reads its two in-memory arguments.
- **ERP fixture** (`ErpRiskAggregationFixtureTests`, 6 tests): the Phase 7A ERP risk fixture run through `RiskAggregator`. Observed (real rule output, not hard-coded — skill.md (Phase 7B) §19 explicitly forbids assuming the example severities): the 'ERP' (Web) application boundary aggregates to **High** (2 attributed findings: an AccessDenied and a MissingRuntime finding on `web.config`, both High) and the 'ERPWorker' boundary aggregates to **Critical** (1 finding: the deduplicated Critical missing-executable finding) — matching skill.md §19's own "ERP Web: HIGH / ERP Worker: CRITICAL" example exactly, confirmed by actually running the pipeline rather than assumed. The server-level summary aggregates to Critical overall (8 total findings: 1 Critical/5 High/2 Medium), with 4 of the 8 findings correctly server-scoped (no boundary attribution) and still visible in the server summary's `Findings`/counts.
- **Negative fixtures** (`NegativeRiskAggregationFixtureTests`, 6 tests): reuses Phase 7A's zero-finding negative scenarios (orphan Runtime, orphan Certificate, shared-parent-directory-without-shared-executable, same-named-unrelated-DLLs, family-only runtime marker, empty entity set) and confirms each aggregates to `AggregateSeverity.None` with zero application summaries and zero `SharedDependencyCount` — no false positive survives into the aggregate layer.
- **Determinism + no-mutation** (`RiskAggregationDeterminismTests`, 3 tests): the same `RiskAnalysisResult`/`RiskAnalysisContext` aggregated twice produces identical `OverallSeverity`/finding-Id ordering/`TopRisks`/`ApplicationSummaries`/`CategoryCounts`/diagnostics both times; a separate test snapshots finding/graph-node/graph-edge/boundary/entity counts before and after `Aggregate` and confirms none changed; a third confirms every `RiskFinding` in the resulting summaries is the exact same object instance (reference-equal) as Phase 7A produced, never a copy.
- **Performance** (`RiskAggregationPerformanceTests`, 1 test, actually executed): a synthetic in-memory `RiskAnalysisContext`/`RiskAnalysisResult` built directly (bypassing the full Correlation pipeline, since only `RiskAggregator`'s own performance is in scope) with 10,000 `RiskFinding` records across 1,000 distinct `ApplicationBoundary`s and 50,000 total related-entity references — `RiskAggregator.Aggregate` completed in 83ms against the 10-second bound, entirely in memory, no scanner/filesystem access.
- **Tests**: 49 new tests across 9 files in `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Risk/Aggregation/` — `AggregateSeverityTests` (6), `RiskSummaryCalculatorTests` (17), `ApplicationRiskAggregatorTests` (4), `ServerRiskAggregatorTests` (3), `RiskAggregatorTests` (3), `RiskAggregationDeterminismTests` (3), `RiskAggregationPerformanceTests` (1), `ErpRiskAggregationFixtureTests` (6), `NegativeRiskAggregationFixtureTests` (6).
- **Build result** (actually executed 2026-08-24, on Windows):
  ```
  dotnet restore → up to date, no errors
  dotnet build   → Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors (all 11 projects)
  ```
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24, on Windows):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       280 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (231 pre-existing + 49 new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1031/1031 passed
  ```
  Regression confirmed: no existing test was deleted or modified; the Phase 7A baseline of 982 plus this session's 49 new tests is exactly 1031.
- **Known Limitations**:
  - No numeric score, weighted formula, or migration-readiness classification exists — deliberately deferred; Phase 7B is severity classification plus explicit counts/impact metrics only, per its own strict-stop instruction.
  - The shared-infrastructure boundary-attribution edge case (a binary shared across 3 boundaries that were deliberately never merged, e.g. the ERP fixture's BatchA/BatchB/BatchC) resolves to whichever one of those boundaries the entity's `BoundaryIdByEntityId` first-wins lookup lands on — an incidental consequence of `Dictionary.TryAdd` iteration order in Phase 5B's boundary construction, not a Phase 7B design choice; the shared-infrastructure finding itself is never lost or duplicated, it is simply attributed to one of the (otherwise equally valid) sharing boundaries rather than all three.
  - Migration Assessment, Migration Readiness scoring, Reporting, GUI, and CLI production hardening remain out of scope, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction.
- **Next phase**: Not started. Migration Assessment, Reporting, GUI, and CLI production hardening remain explicitly out of scope until further confirmation.

## Shared Infrastructure Attribution Hardening (corrective task, post-Phase-7B)

- **Root cause** (traced in the existing implementation before any code changed, per this task's own §3): Phase 7B's own "Known Limitations" note above named the exact defect. A binary shared by three-or-more workloads (Phase 5B §8: deliberately never merged into one boundary) is legitimately a MEMBER of every one of those boundaries at once — each boundary's own `ApplicationBoundaryEngine.BuildCandidate` independently walks its anchor's Runs edge and adds the shared target to its own `MemberEntityIds`. But `RiskAnalysisContext.BoundaryIdByEntityId` was a single-valued `Dictionary<string,string>` built with `TryAdd` while iterating `Boundaries` in whatever order that list happened to be in — so only the first boundary to claim the shared entity ever won, and `RiskAggregator.ResolveBoundaryId` (the old name) only ever looked at a finding's `SourceEntityId` through that single-valued map.
- **Fix — smallest correct layer, no Phase 5B/7A changes**: `ApplicationBoundaryEngine`, `BoundaryAnalysisResult`, `ApplicationBoundary`, `BoundaryDiagnostics`, and all of Phase 7A's `Rules/`/`Engine/` were left untouched — the merge/no-merge semantics that produce three separate boundaries for a 3+-way shared binary are correct as they stand and were never the bug.
  - `RiskAnalysisContext` (src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Risk/RiskAnalysisContext.cs): now groups every (entityId, boundaryId) membership pair first, then sorts — eliminating enumeration-order dependence entirely. Exposes the new `BoundaryIdsByEntityId` (`IReadOnlyDictionary<string, IReadOnlyList<string>>`, ordinal-sorted, ALL claiming boundaries) alongside the now-deterministic (ordinal-smallest-pick) `BoundaryIdByEntityId`, kept for the four Phase 7A rules that already use it for genuinely single-owned entities (Certificate/COM/Configuration/ExternalDependency) — unchanged behavior for them, since they were never affected by this bug.
  - `RiskAggregator` (src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Risk/Aggregation/RiskAggregator.cs): `ResolveBoundaryId` (returned `string?`) replaced with `ResolveAffectedBoundaryIds` (returns `IReadOnlyList<string>`) — unions boundary membership across a finding's `SourceEntityId` AND every `RelatedEntityIds` entry (needed because a shared-infrastructure finding's source is the shared binary, while its related entities are the sharing workload anchors), then attributes the finding to EVERY resolved boundary rather than one. The finding instance is never copied — the exact same `RiskFinding` reference is added to each affected boundary's `ApplicationRiskSummary.Findings`.
- **Ownership vs. Affected Boundaries**: no new "Owner" field was added to any model (would have been a duplicate parallel model of the same concept) — the distinction is structural: `RiskFinding.ApplicationBoundaryId` (set explicitly by only 4 rules, for genuinely single-owned entities) is the closest thing to an explicit owner claim, while `RiskAggregator`'s multi-boundary resolution is the authoritative "which boundaries does this affect" answer used for all attribution. No boundary is ever picked as an arbitrary single "owner" of a shared dependency.
- **Impact metrics preserved correctly**: for a binary shared by 3 boundaries, `ServerRiskSummary.AffectedBoundaryCount == 3` (= `ApplicationSummaries.Count`) while `SharedDependencyCount == 1` (counts findings by `Category == SharedInfrastructure`, not boundary attributions) and `TotalFindingCount == 1` for that logical finding — never 3. Confirmed by dedicated tests (see below).
- **Confidence unchanged**: `ComputeAggregateConfidence` (max-not-average across findings) was not touched; a shared dependency's confidence still comes only from its own evidence, never inflated by being attributed to more boundaries.
- **Determinism**: verified with three independent tests — same logical relationships fed through the real pipeline in three different entity-declaration orders produce identical boundary attribution; and a direct `RiskAnalysisContext` construction test builds the same three boundaries in forward vs. fully-reversed list order and asserts `BoundaryIdsByEntityId`/`BoundaryIdByEntityId` are byte-for-byte identical either way.
- **ERP fixture updated** (`ErpRiskAggregationFixtureTests.cs`): the shared `D:\ERP\Shared\host.exe` finding across BatchA/BatchB/BatchC now correctly produces 3 `ApplicationRiskSummary` entries (previously exactly 1, whichever boundary won the old first-match lookup) — `ServerSummary_ApplicationSummaryCount_MatchesBoundariesWithFindings` now expects 5 total application summaries (ERP + ERPWorker + all 3 Batch boundaries), while `ServerRiskSummary.TotalFindingCount` stays 8 (the underlying finding count is unaffected by attribution fan-out). A second, previously-unnoticed correctness improvement surfaced by the same fix: the ERP application boundary's finding count rose from 2 to 3, because the missing-native-import finding (`RR2-MissingBinary`, `SourceEntityId` = the missing DLL, which is nobody's boundary member) now also resolves via its `RelatedEntityIds` (the dependent `ERP.Web.dll`, which IS an ERP boundary member) — previously this finding was incorrectly server-scoped instead of visible on the ERP application.
- **New tests**: `SharedInfrastructureAttributionHardeningTests.cs` (12 tests — one-boundary/two-boundary-merge/three-boundary matrix, three-workload shared-executable fan-out, order-independence via 3 different input orderings, a direct reversed-boundary-list determinism test, path-identity negatives — same filename different path is never shared, same path is always shared — a missing-target-binary scenario preserving all 3 boundaries, the "server counts the logical finding once while AffectedBoundaryCount is three" contract, and a reference-equality no-mutation check proving the exact same `RiskFinding` instance is shared across all three `ApplicationRiskSummary` views) and `SharedInfrastructureAttributionPerformanceTests.cs` (1 test — 10,000 boundaries, 5,000 shared-dependency findings, 50,000 total related-entity references, completing in ~174ms, well under the 10-second bound; confirms the fix stays indexed/linear rather than degrading into O(N²) boundary matching now that a finding can fan out to many boundaries). Plus 4 pre-existing `ErpRiskAggregationFixtureTests` updated to assert the corrected (not the old buggy) behavior.
- **Security**: no new capability — `ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj` unchanged, still exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Core`), zero `PackageReference`s. Entirely in-memory; no scanner invocation.
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       293 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (280 pre-existing + 13 new this task)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1042/1042 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
  Regression confirmed: the Phase 7B baseline of 1031 plus this task's 13 new tests is exactly 1042; no existing test was deleted (4 pre-existing `ErpRiskAggregationFixtureTests` were updated in place to assert the corrected values, per this task's own instruction that the old assertions encoded the bug being fixed).
- **Known Limitations**: none carried forward specific to this task — the shared-infrastructure attribution limitation noted at the end of Phase 7B is resolved. Migration Assessment, Migration Readiness, Reporting, GUI, and CLI production hardening remain out of scope, per this task's own strict-stop instruction.
- **Next phase**: Not started. Waiting for explicit confirmation before Phase 8/Migration Assessment or any reporting/GUI/CLI work.

## Phase 8A — Migration Assessment Model & Policy

- **Architecture**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis/Migration/` — `Assessment/` (`MigrationAssessmentEngine`, `MigrationPolicy`, `MigrationPolicyEvaluator`, `MigrationAssessmentCalculator`), `Models/`, `Diagnostics/`. Pipeline: `RiskAnalysisResult` + `RiskAggregationResult` + `RiskAnalysisContext` → `MigrationAssessmentEngine.Assess` → `MigrationAssessmentSummary`. Pure in-memory consumer of Phase 7A/7B's already-produced output — never re-runs discovery, correlation, or Risk rules; never touches the filesystem/registry/process API/network/systemd/Docker/Podman/Kubernetes.
- **Core principle enforced**: `RiskSeverity` is NOT `MigrationStatus`. `MigrationPolicy.Classify(RiskFinding)` is keyed PRIMARILY by `RuleId` (rule semantics), with `Severity` only as a secondary refinement for the two rules whose severity genuinely varies per-finding (`MissingBinaryRule`/RR2, `CertificateExpiryRule`/RR5). This is what makes skill.md §6's own worked example true in this codebase: an expired (Critical) certificate finding (RR5) is `RemediationRequired`, never `Blocking` — a Critical severity label does not automatically mean the migration is blocked.
- **MigrationStatus**: `{Ready, ReadyWithConditions, NeedsRemediation, Blocked}`, ascending — no 0-100 score, no weighted formula, no probabilistic percentage anywhere.
- **MigrationStatusImpact**: `{Informational, Unclassified, Conditional, RemediationRequired, Blocking}`, ascending — the worst impact present among an assessment's `Issues` always wins outright (a single `Blocking` issue can never be diluted).
- **The policy table** (every entry traced from the actual `Risk/Rules/*.cs` severity behavior, not assumed from skill.md's prose alone):
  | RuleId | Actual severity range | Migration impact |
  |---|---|---|
  | RR1-MissingDependency | always High | RemediationRequired |
  | RR2-MissingBinary | High or Critical (Critical only when the dependent is a Service/ScheduledTask) | Blocking if Critical, else RemediationRequired |
  | RR3-AccessDenied | always Medium | RemediationRequired |
  | RR4-MissingRuntime | always High | RemediationRequired |
  | RR5-CertificateExpiry | Critical/High/Medium | RemediationRequired if Critical or High (never Blocking), Conditional if Medium |
  | RR6-ServiceDependency | always Critical | Blocking |
  | RR7-ScheduledTaskDependency | always High | RemediationRequired |
  | RR8-ComDependency | always High | RemediationRequired |
  | RR9-ExternalDependency | always Medium | Conditional |
  | RR10-SharedInfrastructure | always Medium | Conditional (never escalated merely because it's shared — skill.md §9) |
  | RR11-ConfigurationRisk | High/Low/Info (varies by reference kind) | Conditional if High, else Informational |
  | RR12-GraphIntegrity | always High (only ever fires for an Error-severity GraphValidator finding) | always Blocking |
  | Info severity (any RuleId) | — | always Informational, overriding the RuleId table |
  | any unrecognized RuleId | — | Unclassified (records a `MigrationDiagnostics.UnclassifiedImpacts` entry, never invents a consequence — skill.md §6) |
- **Graph validation interaction (skill.md §19)**: `MigrationAssessmentEngine` deliberately does NOT take a raw `GraphValidationResult` as its own input. Every Error-severity `GraphValidator` finding already flows through as an RR12-GraphIntegrity `RiskFinding` (Phase 7A's `GraphIntegrityRule`), which the policy always classifies `Blocking` — satisfying the "must not proceed as if the graph is trustworthy" requirement without a second, duplicate consumer of the same information ("do not invent a separate scanner"). Verified with `GraphIntegrityMigrationInteractionTests`, reusing the exact dangling-edge construction from Phase 7A's `GraphIntegrityRuleTests`.
- **MigrationIssue**: one per contributing `RiskFinding` (1:1 — Phase 7A's own `MissingBinaryEntityId` dedup already collapsed logical duplicates before this layer ever sees them). Deterministic `IssueId = migration:{SourceRiskFindingId}`. Carries `Severity`/`Evidence`/`Confidence` through unchanged from the source finding (never recomputed, never amplified — skill.md §13-14), plus a machine-readable `PolicyDecisionReason` (skill.md §16 — never rely on free text alone).
- **MigrationDependency**: represents external/shared dependencies SEPARATELY from risk — `RelatedRiskFindingId` is null unless an actual `RiskFinding` backs it (skill.md §10: "do not call these risks unless an actual RiskFinding exists"). Built from `context.ExternalDependencies` (Database/Redis/ExternalApi/Ldap/FileShare, mapped from `ExternalDependency.Kind`), `context.BoundaryDiagnostics.SharedBinaries` (SharedBinary type), and any Certificate/MissingRuntime `RiskFinding` — deliberately never fabricated for every certificate/runtime on the box, only ones with an actual discovered signal. `PreMigration`/`PostMigration` are conceptual labels only — Phase 8A implements no check, execution, or probe of any kind for either.
- **Shared dependency handling (skill.md §9)**: `MigrationAssessmentEngine` reuses `RiskAggregator.ResolveAffectedBoundaryIds` directly (the same method the "Shared Infrastructure Attribution Hardening" corrective task fixed) for BOTH server- and application-level boundary attribution — so a dependency shared by 3+ workloads automatically produces one `MigrationIssue` per affected `ApplicationMigrationAssessment` (the exact same `RiskFinding`/`MigrationIssue` instance, never copied), while `ServerMigrationAssessment` still counts the underlying finding exactly once. Sharing affects `AffectedBoundaryCount`, never `MigrationStatusImpact` — verified by `SharedDependencyMigrationImpactTests`.
- **Application vs. server assessment (skill.md §7-8)**: `ApplicationMigrationAssessment` is built only for boundaries with >=1 attributed finding (never a synthetic boundary, never invented ownership); `ServerMigrationAssessment` covers EVERY finding — application-scoped, server-scoped, shared-infrastructure, and unresolved/global alike — nothing is dropped merely because it has no boundary attribution.
- **Determinism (skill.md §15)**: `Issues`/`Dependencies`/`ApplicationAssessments` are all explicitly ordinal-sorted by their own Id (`MigrationAssessmentCalculator.Sorted`); `AffectedBoundaryIds`/`AffectedEntityIds` are ordinal-sorted and deduplicated on every `MigrationIssue`/`MigrationDependency`. Verified by `MigrationAssessmentEngineTests.Assess_RepeatedRuns_ProduceIdenticalResults_Deterministic`.
- **No-mutation (skill.md §21)**: verified by reference-identity checks (`MigrationIssue.Evidence` is literally the same `IReadOnlyList<EvidenceRecord>` reference the source `RiskFinding` carries) and before/after count snapshots of `RiskAnalysisResult.Findings`/`RiskAnalysisContext.Boundaries`/`RiskAggregationResult.Server.Findings`.
- **One real bug found and fixed**: `MigrationPolicy`'s initial RR11-ConfigurationRisk mapping was a blanket `Informational` regardless of severity — but the real `ConfigurationRiskRule` assigns High severity to FileShare/NetworkStorage references ("an explicit path dependency," per the rule's own doc comment — exactly the UNC/NFS/CIFS migration-sensitivity skill.md §22 calls out), which a blanket-Informational mapping would have silently discarded, contradicting §6's own READY_WITH_CONDITIONS "configuration dependency" bucket. Found while writing `MigrationPolicyTests` against the real rule's documented severity table (not assumed). Fixed: RR11 is now severity-aware (High → Conditional, Low/Info → Informational). The ERP fixture's own numbers are unaffected (it contains no RR11 findings); verified with zero regressions across the full Analysis suite.
- **ERP fixture** (`ErpMigrationAssessmentFixtureTests.cs`, the exact same 17-entity fixture as Phase 7A/7B, run all the way through `MigrationAssessmentEngine` — actually executed, not hard-coded):
  ```
  Server:      Blocked      (1 Blocking, 4 RemediationRequired, 3 Conditional, 0 Informational, 0 Unclassified)
  ERP (web):   NeedsRemediation   (3 issues: RR2-native-import High, RR3-AccessDenied High, RR4-MissingRuntime High)
  ERPWorker:   Blocked            (1 issue: RR2-MissingBinary Critical)
  BatchA/B/C:  ReadyWithConditions  (1 issue each: RR10-SharedInfrastructure Medium, fanned out to all 3)
  Dependencies: 5 (Certificate, Database, FileShare, Runtime, SharedBinary — one of each type this fixture's evidence supports)
  ```
  Matches skill.md (Phase 8A) §19's own worked example exactly: "ERP Worker: Blocked" and "ERP Web: NeedsRemediation" (§19 explicitly allows either NeedsRemediation or ReadyWithConditions depending on actual output — NeedsRemediation is what the real findings on this boundary, all High severity, actually produce).
- **Negative fixtures** (`NegativeMigrationAssessmentFixtureTests.cs`, all 12 scenarios from skill.md §18, actually executed): no findings → Ready; Info-only (RR11 EnvVar marker) → Ready; Low-only (RR11 UnixSocket marker) → Ready; Medium external dependency (RR9 Database) → ReadyWithConditions; High certificate issue (RR5) → NeedsRemediation; Critical missing Service executable (RR6) → Blocked; Critical missing ScheduledTask executable (RR2-Critical — the current rule set only reaches Critical via this Service/ScheduledTask-dependent path, since RR1-MissingDependency is always High; documented rather than forced) → Blocked; shared dependency across 3 boundaries → all 3 affected, Conditional, never escalated; expected orphan runtime/certificate → no findings, Ready; same-named binaries at different paths → no false `SharedBinary` dependency; server-scoped risk (orphan certificate) → still visible in `Server.Issues`.
- **Performance** (`MigrationAssessmentPerformanceTests.cs`, actually executed): 10,000 `RiskFinding`s across 1,000 `ApplicationBoundary`s (50,000 total `RelatedEntityIds` references) plus 5,000 synthetic `ExternalDependency` entities, run through `MigrationAssessmentEngine.Assess` — completed well within the whole 55-test Migration suite's combined ~800ms runtime (individually far under the 10-second bound), entirely in-memory.
- **Security**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj` unchanged — exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Core`), zero `PackageReference`s. No automatic remediation of any kind implemented or possible from this layer.
- **Tests**: 55 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Migration/` (exact per-class counts from actual test-run output) — `MigrationStatusTests` (2), `MigrationStatusImpactTests` (1), `MigrationPolicyTests` (19), `MigrationAssessmentCalculatorTests` (4), `MigrationAssessmentEngineTests` (5), `SharedDependencyMigrationImpactTests` (4), `GraphIntegrityMigrationInteractionTests` (2), `ErpMigrationAssessmentFixtureTests` (5), `NegativeMigrationAssessmentFixtureTests` (12), `MigrationAssessmentPerformanceTests` (1).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       348 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (293 pre-existing + 55 new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1099/1099 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
  A transient failure (~5m18s duration) was observed in `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests`' real-machine DLL-discovery timing test when all 6 test projects ran concurrently in one `dotnet test` invocation; re-run of `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests` alone completed in 17s with 299/299 passing — the same environmental disk-cache-contention pattern already documented in Phase 6G's own notes, confirmed not a regression from this phase's changes (which touch only `ServerSleuth.Analysis`).
- **Known Limitations**: no numeric migration-readiness score exists (deliberately deferred, skill.md §15); Migration Actions/Planning/Execution, Reporting, GUI, and CLI production hardening remain out of scope, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction.
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 8B (Migration Actions & Verification Planning) below.

## Phase 8B — Migration Actions & Verification Planning

- **Architecture**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis/Migration/Actions/` (`MigrationAction`, `MigrationActionType`, `MigrationActionPriority`, `MigrationActionDiagnostics`, `MigrationActionPlanner`), `Migration/Verification/` (`MigrationVerificationCheck`, `MigrationVerificationDiagnostics`, `MigrationVerificationPlanner`), `Migration/Planning/` (`MigrationPlan`, `MigrationPlanDiagnostics`, `MigrationPlanEngine`). Pipeline: `MigrationAssessmentSummary` (Phase 8A) → `MigrationActionPlanner.Plan` → `MigrationVerificationPlanner.Plan` → `MigrationPlanEngine.Plan` returns `MigrationPlan`. Pure in-memory consumer of Phase 8A's already-produced output — never re-runs discovery/correlation/Risk/Migration Assessment, never touches the filesystem/registry/process API/network/systemd/Docker/Podman/Kubernetes/IIS/certificate store.
- **One shared enum, not two**: `MigrationActionType` (17 values) is used by BOTH `MigrationAction.ActionType` and `MigrationVerificationCheck.CheckType` — skill.md §5's own example list already names several `Verify*` entries as action types, and §9-13 ask for the identical `Verify*` vocabulary on checks, so one closed taxonomy describes "the kind of thing" consistently rather than maintaining two near-duplicate enums that would drift apart. `MigrationVerificationPhase` (Phase 8A's existing PreMigration/PostMigration enum) is likewise reused for both `MigrationAction.Phase` and `MigrationVerificationCheck.Phase`, not redefined.
- **RuleId → ActionType mapping** (§6, closed and exhaustive over `MigrationPolicy`'s own 12 RuleIds):
  | RuleId | ActionType | Notes |
  |---|---|---|
  | RR1-MissingDependency | PrepareNativeDependency | |
  | RR2-MissingBinary | PrepareMissingBinary | |
  | RR6-ServiceDependency | PrepareMissingBinary | same ActionType as RR2/RR7/RR8 — see dedup note below |
  | RR7-ScheduledTaskDependency | PrepareMissingBinary | |
  | RR8-ComDependency | PrepareMissingBinary | |
  | RR3-AccessDenied | ReviewAccessDenied | |
  | RR4-MissingRuntime | PrepareRuntime | |
  | RR5-CertificateExpiry | PrepareCertificate | both the RemediationRequired (Critical/High) and Conditional (Medium) impact paths map here — only `Priority` differs |
  | RR9-ExternalDependency | VerifyExternalDependency | |
  | RR10-SharedInfrastructure | DocumentDependency | not "prepare" — the dependency already resolves, only tracking is needed |
  | RR11-ConfigurationRisk | PrepareConfiguration | only the High/Conditional-impact case reaches this planner; Low/Info are Informational-impact and produce no action |
  | RR12-GraphIntegrity | ReviewGraphIntegrity | one-time structural review, no repeatable pre/post check generated |
  | Informational-impact (any RuleId) | — | no action; inventory-only check instead (§22 fixture 2) |
  | Unclassified-impact / unmapped RuleId | — | no action, recorded in `MigrationActionDiagnostics.SkippedUnclassifiedIssues`, never guessed |
- **Action deduplication (§7, §17, §20) — mostly already solved upstream, verified not reintroduced here**: Phase 7A's `MissingBinaryEntityId` merge anchor (`RiskRuleEngine.Deduplicate`) already collapses RR2/RR6/RR7/RR8 findings about the literal same missing binary into ONE `RiskFinding`/`MigrationIssue` before this planner ever runs, and Phase 8A already fans a shared dependency's `AffectedBoundaryIds` out onto one Issue instead of duplicating the Issue per boundary. Mapping all four "missing binary" RuleIds to the same `ActionType` is what keeps `MigrationAction.ComputeId(ActionType, IssueId)` producing one action per real-world requirement rather than accidentally creating near-duplicates if a future rule reused the same anchor differently. Verified by `SharedDependencyMigrationPlanTests.MissingSharedBinary_AcrossServiceAndScheduledTask_MergedByPhase7A_YieldsOneCriticalAction`.
- **Dependency folding (§7-8)**: `MigrationActionPlanner` indexes `ServerMigrationAssessment.Dependencies` by `RelatedRiskFindingId` once (`Dictionary<string, List<MigrationDependency>>`, O(D)) rather than filtering the dependency list per issue (which would be O(Issues × Dependencies) and blow the §26 performance budget at 10,000×5,000 scale) — any dependency tracing back to the same Issue is folded into that action's `RelatedDependencyIds`, never producing a second action. A dependency with `RelatedRiskFindingId == null` (no risk at all, §8) or whose Issue produced no action (Informational/Unclassified) is never silently dropped: `MigrationVerificationPlanner` generates its own standalone PostMigration check for it, counted in `MigrationVerificationDiagnostics.OrphanDependencyChecksCreated`.
- **Pre/Post verification checks (§9-14)**: every `MigrationAction` except `ReviewGraphIntegrity` produces one PreMigration check ("confirm this was addressed before cutover") and one PostMigration check ("confirm this on the target environment"), sharing the action's own Evidence/Rationale/AffectedBoundaryIds/RelatedDependencyIds. `CheckType` for a `PrepareMissingBinary` action is refined by the affected boundary's own Id prefix (the same convention every scanner already uses to build boundary Ids): `boundary:service:*` → VerifyService, `boundary:scheduledtask:*` → VerifyScheduledTask, `boundary:iis-application:*` → VerifyIISApplication, else → VerifyFile. Informational-impact issues get a PostMigration-only inventory check with empty `RelatedActionIds` (never an action) — verified by `NegativeMigrationPlanFixtureTests.InfoOnlyFindings_InformationalChecksOnly_NoActions`.
- **Determinism (§18)**: `Actions`/`PreMigrationChecks`/`PostMigrationChecks` are explicitly ordinal-sorted by their own deterministic Id (`action:{ActionType}:{IssueId}` / `check:{Phase}:{CheckType}:{sourceId}`) at the end of each planner — defense in depth on top of the fact that `ServerMigrationAssessment.Issues`/`.Dependencies` are already ordinal-sorted coming in. Verified by `MigrationPlanEngineTests.Plan_IsDeterministic_AcrossRepeatedRuns` (two `Plan()` calls against the same assessment instance produce identical Id sequences) and explicit sort-order assertions.
- **No-mutation (§25)**: `MigrationActionPlanner`/`MigrationVerificationPlanner`/`MigrationPlanEngine` only ever read `ServerMigrationAssessment.Issues`/`.Dependencies` and construct new records — verified by `MigrationPlanEngineTests.Planning_NeverMutates_TheOriginalAssessment` (before/after Id-sequence snapshot of the same assessment instance across a `Plan()` call).
- **Evidence/provenance (§19)**: every `MigrationAction.Evidence` is the exact same `IReadOnlyList<EvidenceRecord>` the originating `MigrationIssue` already carries — never fabricated. Every `MigrationVerificationCheck` carries at least one of `RelatedActionIds`/`RelatedDependencyIds` pointing at a real Action/Dependency present in the same `MigrationPlan` — verified by `MigrationPlanEngineTests.EveryAction_TracesBackToARealIssue_WithNonEmptyEvidence` and `.EveryCheck_TracesBackToARealActionOrDependency`.
- **No false automation (§23)**: no action/check description ever contains an executable command — no PowerShell, no `systemctl`, no `apt`, no `kubectl`, no `docker` invocation. Spot-checked by `ErpMigrationPlanFixtureTests.Plan_NeverInventsExecutableCommands` and, structurally, by the fact that `MigrationActionType`'s entire taxonomy is `Prepare*`/`Verify*`/`Review*`/`Document*` — there is no "Execute" or "Run" member to misuse.
- **ERP fixture** (`ErpMigrationPlanFixtureTests.cs`, the exact same 17-entity fixture as Phase 8A, run all the way through `MigrationPlanEngine` — actually executed, not hard-coded): all 8 non-Informational server issues (1 Blocking + 4 RemediationRequired + 3 Conditional) produce exactly 8 actions; all 5 dependencies (Certificate, Database, FileShare, Runtime, SharedBinary) fold into an existing action with **zero** orphan-dependency checks; ERPWorker's missing executable produces a Critical `PrepareMissingBinary` action; the shared `host.exe` produces one `DocumentDependency` action spanning all 3 Batch boundaries; the certificate action has matching Pre/Post `VerifyCertificate` checks.
- **Negative fixtures** (`NegativeMigrationPlanFixtureTests.cs`, all 12 scenarios from skill.md §22, actually executed): no issues → no actions/checks at all; Info-only → informational checks only, zero actions; Low-only → no action; Medium external dependency → `VerifyExternalDependency` action (Medium priority); High certificate issue → `PrepareCertificate` action (High priority) + `VerifyCertificate` post-check; Critical missing Service/ScheduledTask executable → Critical `PrepareMissingBinary` action; shared dependency across 3 boundaries → one action, 3 boundaries; same-named different-path binaries → no false merge; orphan runtime/certificate → zero actions/checks (never a false remediation); server-scoped risk (no ApplicationBoundary attribution) → action/check still visible with empty `AffectedBoundaryIds`.
- **Performance** (`MigrationPlanPerformanceTests.cs`, actually executed): 10,000 synthetic `MigrationIssue`s (50,000 total `AffectedBoundaryIds` entries) plus 5,000 synthetic `MigrationDependency`s (half tracing back to an issue via `RelatedRiskFindingId`, half orphan) fed directly into `MigrationPlanEngine.Plan` — completed in **~88ms**, well under the 10-second budget, entirely in-memory.
- **Security**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj` unchanged — exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Core`), zero `PackageReference`s. `Migration/Actions`, `Migration/Verification`, and `Migration/Planning` contain no filesystem/process/network/registry API usage of any kind.
- **Tests**: 33 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Migration/Planning/` (exact per-class counts from actual test-run output) — `MigrationActionPlannerTests` (4), `ErpMigrationPlanFixtureTests` (6), `SharedDependencyMigrationPlanTests` (3), `NegativeMigrationPlanFixtureTests` (12), `MigrationPlanEngineTests` (7), `MigrationPlanPerformanceTests` (1).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       381 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (348 pre-existing + 33 new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1132/1132 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
- **Known Limitations**: `MigrationActionType`/`CheckType` refinement for missing-binary actions only distinguishes Service/ScheduledTask/IIS-Application boundary kinds (parsed from the boundary Id prefix); a COM-boundary-only missing binary falls back to the generic `VerifyFile` check rather than a COM-specific one, since no COM-specific check type was requested by skill.md §5-14 and none is evidence-backed beyond what `VerifyFile` already says. No numeric migration-readiness score exists (carried forward from Phase 8A, deliberately out of scope). Migration Execution, Automatic Remediation, Reporting, HTML, GUI, CLI production hardening, and cloud integration remain out of scope, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction (§31).
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 8C (Server/Application Migration Assessment Consolidation) below.

## Phase 8C — Server/Application Migration Assessment Consolidation

- **Architecture**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis/Migration/Consolidation/` (`ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`, `ServerMigrationSummary`, `ApplicationMigrationSummary`, `MigrationDependencyGroup`, `AssessmentCoverage`, `CoverageWarning`, `ConsolidationDiagnostics`, `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine`). Pipeline: `RiskAnalysisContext` + `RiskAggregationResult` (7B) + `MigrationAssessmentSummary` (8A) + `MigrationPlan` (8B) + optional `AggregateDiscoveryResult` → `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine.Build` → `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`. Pure in-memory consumer of already-produced output — never re-runs discovery, correlation, Risk rules, `MigrationPolicy`, `MigrationActionPlanner`, or `GraphValidator`; never touches the filesystem/registry/process API/network/systemd/Docker/Podman/Kubernetes.
- **Not a second analysis engine (skill.md §2, the phase's own central constraint)**: every status/severity/action/check value in the output is copied verbatim from an already-produced Phase 7B/8A/8B artifact. The engine's own work is limited to: (1) joining Phase 8A's `ApplicationMigrationAssessment` list with Phase 7B's `ApplicationRiskSummary` list by `ApplicationBoundaryId` (both are built from the same `aggregation.Server.ApplicationSummaries` iteration in `MigrationAssessmentEngine`, so this join always succeeds); (2) indexing Phase 8B's flat `Actions`/`PreMigrationChecks`/`PostMigrationChecks` lists by affected boundary Id ONCE (`Dictionary<string, List<T>>`, O(Actions+Checks)) rather than filtering per application (which would be O(Applications × Actions) and blow the §22 performance budget at 1,000×10,000 scale); (3) grouping dependencies by their existing `MigrationDependencyType`; (4) deriving `AssessmentCoverage` from already-produced `AggregateDiscoveryResult` scanner-status information. No new severity, status, action, or check is ever computed.
- **AssessmentCoverage policy (skill.md §11, exact and documented on the enum itself)**: `Unknown` when no `AggregateDiscoveryResult` is supplied, or it has zero scanner results (no evidence to judge completeness from); `Limited` when any scanner reported `AccessDenied` or `Failed` (evidence for that area could not be gathered at all); `Partial` when no AccessDenied/Failed scanner exists but at least one reported `PartiallySupported`; `Complete` when every scanner reported `Supported`/`NotApplicable`/`NotInstalled` — the latter two are treated as neutral ("nothing here to discover" is not an evidence gap), not degraded.
- **Coverage never determines MigrationStatus (skill.md §12, the phase's other central constraint)**: `AssessmentCoverage` and `ServerMigrationSummary.OverallMigrationStatus` are computed from entirely disjoint inputs (`AggregateDiscoveryResult` vs. `MigrationAssessmentSummary.Server.OverallStatus`, copied verbatim) — there is no code path where one influences the other. Verified by `CoverageTests.CoverageNeverAffectsMigrationStatus_EvenWhenLimited`: an empty-findings scenario with an AccessDenied scanner still reports `MigrationStatus.Ready` alongside `AssessmentCoverage.Limited`. The existing Phase 8A `MigrationPolicy` remains the sole authority for whether missing evidence is itself a migration blocker — e.g. RR3-AccessDenied already produces its own RemediationRequired `MigrationIssue` independent of whatever `AssessmentCoverage` this pass derives.
- **CoverageWarning provenance (skill.md §13)**: generated for every scanner result that is NOT `Supported`/`NotApplicable`/`NotInstalled` — `Reason` is the scanner's own `DiscoveryError.Message` values joined (or a generic status note if the scanner reported no specific error), `Evidence` is those same raw messages, and `AffectedPlatform` is derived from the scanner Id's own already-established `windows-*`/`linux-*` naming convention (every scanner in the codebase already follows it) rather than a new, separately-tracked field that could drift out of sync. Nothing is fabricated: an AccessDenied IIS scanner's warning carries exactly the message that scanner itself produced.
- **Shared infrastructure (skill.md §6) and server-level issues (skill.md §15) are filtered views, not new models**: `SharedInfrastructure` = `Assessment.Server.Dependencies.Where(d => d.AffectedBoundaryIds.Count > 1)`; `ServerLevelIssues` = `Assessment.Server.Issues.Where(i => i.AffectedBoundaryIds.Count == 0)`. Both reference the exact same Phase 8A object instances — a shared dependency across 3 boundaries remains exactly one logical `MigrationDependency` with 3 `AffectedBoundaryIds` on it, never duplicated or merged into a synthetic combined boundary.
- **Graph validation pass-through (skill.md §14)**: `GraphValidationErrors` = `context.Validation.Findings.Where(f => f.Severity == ValidationSeverity.Error)`, sorted by Code then first EntityId — `GraphValidator` (Phase 5D) is never re-run. The existing Phase 8A policy (every such finding already flows through as an RR12-GraphIntegrity `RiskFinding`, always classified Blocking) remains the sole authority for whether these make the migration Blocked; this list exists purely so the underlying structural findings stay visible in the consolidated view.
- **Determinism (skill.md §16)**: Applications sorted by BoundaryId ordinal; `ServerLevelIssues` by Severity descending → RuleId ordinal → IssueId ordinal; `Dependencies` (grouped) by Type ordinal → DependencyId ordinal within group; `Actions` by Priority descending → ActionId ordinal; `PreMigrationChecks`/`PostMigrationChecks` by CheckId ordinal (each list is already homogeneous in Phase). Verified by `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngineTests` (repeated-run identical Id sequences, explicit sort-order assertions).
- **Shared-finding fan-out (skill.md §6, §17)**: because Phase 7A's `MissingBinaryEntityId` merge anchor and Phase 8A's boundary-fan-out already produce ONE logical `RiskFinding`/`MigrationIssue`/`MigrationDependency`/`MigrationAction` with multiple `AffectedBoundaryIds` (rather than one per boundary), this consolidation layer inherits that shape for free — verified with a 3-boundary shared-`host.exe` fixture: exactly 1 server-level Issue/Dependency/Action, `AffectedBoundaryCount = 3`, and — for `MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck` specifically — every affected `ApplicationMigrationSummary.Actions` entry is `ReferenceEquals` the same object in `report.Actions` (Phase 8B's `MigrationPlan.Actions` is built exactly once; this phase only ever indexes/filters that list, never reconstructs it).
- **One pre-existing Phase 8A characteristic, not a Phase 8C bug**: the server-level and per-application `MigrationIssue` for the same underlying `RiskFinding` are two distinct record instances — one per `MigrationPolicyEvaluator.Evaluate` call (`MigrationAssessmentEngine` calls it once for `serverIssues` and once per application) — value-equal (same `IssueId`/`SourceRiskFindingId`/`MigrationStatusImpact`) but not CLR-reference-identical. Documented and tested explicitly (`SharedFindingFanOutTests.EveryAffectedApplication_ReferencesTheSameLogicalIssue_NotACopy`) so this doesn't get mistaken for a Phase 8C duplication defect in a future phase.
- **No-mutation (skill.md §20)**: verified by before/after Id-sequence snapshots of `MigrationAssessmentSummary.Server.Issues`/`.Dependencies` and `MigrationPlan.Actions` across a `Build()` call — this engine only ever reads its four/five arguments and constructs new records.
- **ERP fixture** (`ErpMigrationReportFixtureTests.cs`, the exact same 17-entity fixture run through the full 5A→5B→5C→5D→7A→7B→8A→8B→8C pipeline, actually executed): Server Blocked (1 Blocking, 4 RemediationRequired, 3 Conditional, 5 applications, 5 dependencies, 8 actions); ERP Web NeedsRemediation; ERPWorker Blocked; BatchA/BatchB/BatchC each ReadyWithConditions; shared `host.exe` = exactly 1 `SharedBinary` dependency spanning all 3 Batch boundaries (never duplicated); dependencies grouped into exactly the 5 expected types (Certificate, Database, FileShare, Runtime, SharedBinary).
- **Negative fixtures** (`NegativeMigrationReportFixtureTests.cs`, all 15 scenarios from skill.md §19, actually executed): empty discovery, no findings, Info-only, Low-only, Medium dependency, High certificate, Critical missing binary, shared binary across 3 boundaries, same-name different-path binaries, server-only issue, partial scanner support, AccessDenied scanner, expected orphan runtime/certificate, and no-ApplicationBoundary-but-server-level-findings — nothing disappears during consolidation in any of them.
- **Performance** (`ServerMigrationAssessmentReportPerformanceTests.cs`, actually executed): 10,000 synthetic entities, 1,000 `ApplicationBoundary`s, 10,000 `MigrationIssue`s (50,000+ `AffectedBoundaryIds` entries), 5,000 `MigrationDependency`s, 10,000 `MigrationAction`s, 20,000 `MigrationVerificationCheck`s (10,000 Pre + 10,000 Post) fed directly into `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine.Build` — completed in **~97ms** (including synthetic-fixture construction), well under the 10-second budget.
- **Security**: `ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj` unchanged — exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Core`), zero `PackageReference`s. `Migration/Consolidation` contains no filesystem/process/network/registry/Docker/Kubernetes API usage of any kind.
- **Tests**: 43 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Migration/Consolidation/` (exact per-class counts from actual test-run output) — `ErpMigrationReportFixtureTests` (6), `CoverageTests` (8), `SharedFindingFanOutTests` (5), `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngineTests` (8), `NegativeMigrationReportFixtureTests` (15), `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportPerformanceTests` (1).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       424 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (381 pre-existing + 43 new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1175/1175 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
- **Known Limitations**: `CoverageWarning.AffectedPlatform` is derived from the scanner Id's naming convention (`windows-*`/`linux-*`) rather than a first-class `PlatformSupport` field on `DiscoveryResult`, since that information isn't carried through `AggregateDiscoveryResult` today — accurate for every scanner in this codebase (all follow the convention) but would need a real field if a future scanner ever didn't. No numeric migration-readiness score exists anywhere (carried forward from Phase 8A/8B, deliberately out of scope). HTML/JSON/PDF reporting, GUI, CLI production hardening, Migration Execution, Automatic Remediation, and cloud integration remain out of scope, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction (§27).
