## Phase 9A — Reporting Foundation & JSON Renderer

- **Architecture**: two new projects, `src/ServerSleuth.Reporting` and `test/ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests`, added to `ServerSleuth.slnx`. `ServerSleuth.Reporting` has exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Analysis`, transitively `ServerSleuth.Core` only — confirmed via `dotnet list reference`) and zero `PackageReference`s. Pipeline: `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` (Phase 8C) → `IReportRenderer.Render` → `ReportRenderResult` (Format/Content/Encoding, entirely in-memory). Reporting performs zero discovery/correlation/risk analysis/migration policy evaluation/status calculation/dependency resolution/boundary detection/graph validation — it only reformats what Phase 8C already produced (skill.md §1).
- **Renderer abstraction (§3)**: `IReportRenderer` (`Format` + `Render(ServerMigrationAssessmentReport) -> ReportRenderResult`), `ReportFormat` (currently one member, `Json` — no HTML/PDF placeholder added ahead of need, since an unused enum member is exactly the "unnecessary abstraction" §3 warns against), `ReportRenderResult` (immutable record: Format/Content/Encoding). No `SaveToFile`, filesystem export, HTTP API, email, or cloud upload exists anywhere — Render returns content in memory only (§12); that composition belongs to a later phase.
- **DTO boundary, the actual secret-safety mechanism (§6)**: `Json/Dto/` is a full parallel type tree (`ServerReportDto`/`ServerSummaryDto`/`ApplicationDto`/`IssueDto`/`DependencyDto`/`DependencyGroupDto`/`ActionDto`/`CheckDto`/`EvidenceDto`/`ConfidenceDto`/`CoverageWarningDto`/`GraphValidationFindingDto`/`DiagnosticsDto`) that `JsonReportRenderer` serializes — never the Phase 8C domain model directly. `ReportDtoMapper.ToDto()` is a pure 1:1 field mapping (verified by its own doc comment and by every mapping test): no status, severity, action, dependency, or check is recomputed, only reshaped. Because the DTO contract is a fixed, explicitly-declared property list, it structurally cannot expose a field the domain model didn't already have a named property for — a future domain-model field addition (e.g. a careless `Metadata` passthrough) would require an explicit, reviewable DTO change before it could ever reach JSON, rather than automatically appearing via reflection-based/dynamic serialization of the domain type.
- **Why secrets don't reach Reporting in practice**: `ServerSleuth.Core.Models.Configuration` has never carried raw file content since Phase 4E-1/6E (its own doc comment: "Never carries full file contents, only detected sections/references and a secret-presence flag"); `ExternalDependency` has no credential field, only `Kind`/`Endpoint`; and Phase 8A/8B/8C never carry entity `Metadata` (the dictionary a scanner might use to stash a discovered connection string) forward into `MigrationIssue`/`MigrationDependency`/`MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck` at all — confirmed by re-reading every one of those model definitions during this phase. Reporting's DTO boundary does not implement NEW redaction logic; it preserves the boundary those earlier phases already established, and adds a second, independent layer (the DTO's fixed property list) so a hypothetical upstream lapse still couldn't reach JSON without a deliberate DTO change.
- **Secret-safety tests (§6, actually executed, not merely asserted in prose)**: `JsonReportRendererSecretSafetyTests` — deliberately stashes a `Password=...` connection string, an `API_KEY`, a `Bearer` token, and PEM-shaped `PRIVATE_KEY` text into entity `Metadata` (mimicking a hypothetical scanner mistake) and confirms none of those deliberately-sensitive values appear anywhere in the rendered JSON; a companion test proves an *already-redacted* `[REDACTED]` marker (mimicking what the real `SecretRedactor` from Phase 2 already produces) passes through unchanged rather than being "helpfully" restored or mangled; a reflection-based test enumerates every DTO type's public properties and fails immediately if any name contains `Metadata`/`Password`/`Secret`/`ApiKey`/`ConnectionString`/`PrivateKey`/`Token`/`RawContent` — a tripwire against a future careless addition, not just a content-based check.
- **Raw configuration safety (§7)**: `JsonReportRendererRawConfigurationSafetyTests` proves the rendered JSON never contains XML config markup (`<configuration>`, `<system.webServer>`, `<connectionStrings>`), systemd unit markers (`[Unit]`, `[Service]`, `ExecStart=`), or Docker image-config JSON shapes — both via content-based assertions against real pipeline output and via a direct reflection check that `Configuration` itself has no `Content`/`RawText`/`FileBody`-named property (so no DTO mapping from it ever could carry one either).
- **JSON contract (§5)**: `ServerReportDto` exposes `Server` (the `ServerSummaryDto`), `Coverage`/`CoverageWarnings`, `Applications` (each with BoundaryId/MigrationStatus/RiskSeverity/Issues/Dependencies/Actions/PreMigrationChecks/PostMigrationChecks), `ServerLevelIssues`, `SharedInfrastructure`, `Dependencies` (grouped), `Actions`, `PreMigrationChecks`/`PostMigrationChecks`, `GraphValidationErrors`, and `Diagnostics` — every field traced 1:1 back to a Phase 8C property, nothing invented.
- **Determinism (§8)**: the DTO mapper never sorts, groups, or deduplicates — it trusts Phase 8C's own already-ordinal-sorted collections and iterates them with a plain `Select().ToList()`, never a `Dictionary`/`HashSet`. `JsonSerializer` writes properties in DTO declaration order. Verified by rendering the same report instance 2x and 10x (byte-identical) and by building the identical ERP-style entity set in forward vs. reverse construction order and confirming identical JSON once `EvidenceRecord.CapturedAt` (a genuine wall-clock construction timestamp, not a rendering concern) is normalized out of the comparison.
- **Unicode (§9)**: `JsonReportRenderer` uses `JavaScriptEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)` rather than the default encoder — both produce equally valid, uncorrupted UTF-8/JSON (the default merely escapes non-ASCII as `\uXXXX`), but literal Unicode characters are what a human reading a migration report actually expects. Verified with a Traditional Chinese application name (`伺服器遷移評估`) and a mixed-Unicode Windows path, both confirmed present as literal characters in the raw JSON text (not merely round-trippable after `\u`-decoding).
- **Null/empty semantics (§10, explicitly documented and tested, not left implicit)**: every collection field is ALWAYS present as a JSON array — `[]` when empty, never omitted; every optional scalar (e.g. `MigrationDependency.RelatedRiskFindingId`, `EvidenceRecord.Detail`) serializes as explicit JSON `null` when absent, never a fabricated default value or a dropped property. Verified against a fully empty report (zero entities) and against entities that legitimately produce a null `Detail`/`RelatedRiskFindingId`.
- **Error handling (§11)**: `JsonReportRenderer.Render` has no try/catch of any kind — a serialization exception (none observed against any real or 10k-scale synthetic report) propagates to the caller unmodified, which IS the "renderer failure" representation §11 asks for; there is no code path that could silently return truncated or malformed JSON.
- **ERP fixture (§14, §22)**: `JsonReportRendererErpFixtureTests` runs the exact same 17-entity ERP fixture as every prior phase's own tests through the full pipeline (Correlation→Boundary→Expansion→Validation→Risk→Aggregation→Migration Assessment→Migration Plan→Consolidation→JSON), then asserts the rendered JSON reflects the actual, previously-established semantics (Server Blocked; ERP Web NeedsRemediation; ERPWorker Blocked; BatchA/BatchB/BatchC each ReadyWithConditions; one shared `host.exe` `SharedBinary` dependency with 3 `AffectedBoundaryIds`; 8 actions; both Pre/Post check lists populated) — read out of the parsed JSON via `System.Text.Json.JsonDocument`, never hard-coded or recalculated in the test itself.
- **Negative fixtures (§13, §23)**: `JsonReportRendererNegativeFixtureTests` covers empty discovery, no findings, Info-only, critical missing binary (Blocked), shared binary across 3 boundaries, a server-only issue with no `ApplicationBoundary`, and expected-orphan runtime/certificate (no false findings) — all rendered successfully with nothing lost.
- **No-mutation (§17)**: `JsonReportRendererNoMutationTests` snapshots every Id sequence the renderer reads (`ApplicationAssessments`, `Assessment.Server.Issues`/`.Dependencies`, `Plan.Actions`/`.PreMigrationChecks`/`.PostMigrationChecks`, `Diagnostics` counts) before and after THREE separate `Render()` calls against the same report instance — identical every time.
- **Performance (§16, actually executed)**: `JsonReportRendererPerformanceTests` builds a synthetic `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` directly (10,000 entities, 1,000 `ApplicationBoundary`s, 10,000 `MigrationIssue`s, 5,000 `MigrationDependency`s, 10,000 `MigrationAction`s, 20,000 `MigrationVerificationCheck`s — 10,000 Pre + 10,000 Post) and renders it — completed in **~290ms** (full test including synthetic-fixture construction), well under the 10-second budget.
- **Security / project dependency review (§15, actually verified)**: `dotnet list src/ServerSleuth.Reporting/ServerSleuth.Reporting.csproj reference` shows exactly one reference, `..\ServerSleuth.Analysis\ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj` — no Windows/Linux/Infrastructure project reference, no `PackageReference` of any kind (the `.csproj` itself has zero `<PackageReference>` elements).
- **Tests**: 40 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests/` (exact per-class counts from actual test-run output) — `JsonReportRendererErpFixtureTests` (8), `JsonReportRendererCoverageTests` (4), `JsonReportRendererNullEmptyTests` (4), `JsonReportRendererUnicodeTests` (3), `JsonReportRendererDeterminismTests` (3), `JsonReportRendererSecretSafetyTests` (4), `JsonReportRendererRawConfigurationSafetyTests` (4), `JsonReportRendererNoMutationTests` (2), `JsonReportRendererNegativeFixtureTests` (7), `JsonReportRendererPerformanceTests` (1).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       424 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:       40 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1215/1215 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
- **Known Limitations**: the DTO mapper duplicates the Phase 8C model's shape by hand (no reflection/AutoMapper-style generic mapping) — deliberate, since a hand-written 1:1 mapper is what makes "never recomputed, only reshaped" independently verifiable per property, but it does mean a future Phase 8C model field addition requires a corresponding, deliberate DTO+mapper change to surface in JSON (treated as a feature of the security boundary, not a gap). No HTML/PDF renderer, no file-export/CLI composition layer, no HTTP API exists yet — all explicitly deferred per this phase's own strict-stop instruction (§21).
- **Phase 9B readiness**: `IReportRenderer` is format-agnostic by design — an HTML (or other) renderer can implement it directly, likely reusing the same `Json/Dto` mapping (or a sibling DTO tree) without touching `ServerSleuth.Analysis` or Phase 8C at all. File-export/CLI composition (writing `ReportRenderResult.Content` to disk using `ReportRenderResult.Encoding`) has no blocking dependency on anything still to be built in Reporting itself.
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 9B (Self-Contained HTML Migration Report) below.

## Phase 9B — Self-Contained HTML Migration Report

- **Architecture**: `ServerSleuth.Reporting/Html/` (`HtmlReportRenderer`, `HtmlDocumentStyles`, `CssClassName`), extending the exact same `IReportRenderer` abstraction Phase 9A defined — no second reporting architecture. `ReportFormat` now has two members, `Json` and `Html`. `HtmlReportRenderer` calls the same internal `ReportDtoMapper.ToDto()` Phase 9A's `JsonReportRenderer` uses (same assembly, `internal` visibility) rather than re-reading `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`'s domain objects or building a second mapping — the DTO tree's fixed property list is still the one place both renderers' secret-safety/raw-configuration-safety boundary lives.
- **Self-contained, fully offline (§3)**: the generated document has exactly one `<style>` block (`HtmlDocumentStyles.Css`, inline in `<head>`) and zero `<script>` tags, zero `<link>` tags, zero external image/font/CDN references. Verified structurally (`HtmlReportRendererHtmlEscapingTests.NoScriptTagIsEverEmitted`) and by construction — there is no code path in `HtmlReportRenderer` that ever writes a URL-bearing tag.
- **Zero JavaScript, not just "tiny" JavaScript (§20)**: collapsible per-application sections use native `<details>`/`<summary>` elements, which need no script at all for expand/collapse. This was a deliberate choice over "a tiny inline script" — every interactive affordance the report offers works identically whether or not the viewer's environment executes JavaScript, which is a strictly stronger guarantee than "the report remains readable without JavaScript."
- **CSS classes derived only from existing values (§6-7)**: `CssClassName.From` is a pure PascalCase→kebab-case string transform (`"NeedsRemediation"` → `"needs-remediation"`) applied to whatever `MigrationStatus`/`RiskSeverity`/`AssessmentCoverage`/`MigrationStatusImpact`/`MigrationActionPriority` string the DTO already carries — it never branches on or reinterprets the value, so no new status semantics can leak in through the styling layer.
- **Unicode via `HtmlEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)`, not `System.Net.WebUtility.HtmlEncode` (§14, §18)**: found during testing that `WebUtility.HtmlEncode` numeric-entity-escapes Latin-1 Supplement accented characters (e.g. `Ü`/`ï`/`ö`/`é` → `&#220;` etc.) while leaving CJK ideographs alone — technically uncorrupted but inconsistent and less readable. `HtmlEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)` keeps ALL non-ASCII text literal while `HtmlEncoder` still always escapes the five HTML-structural characters (`< > & " '`) regardless of range configuration — mirrors the Phase 9A JSON renderer's identical `JavaScriptEncoder.Create(UnicodeRanges.All)` decision for the same underlying reason.
- **Action grouping is metadata-driven, not text-inference (§13)**: Pre-Migration / Post-Migration / Review-Documentation buckets are computed purely from each `MigrationAction`'s own `ActionType` (a `Review*`/`Document*` prefix routes to Review/Documentation) and `Phase` fields — both already-structured, already-existing DTO properties. No `Description`/`Rationale`/`Title` text is ever parsed to decide grouping.
- **Determinism, opt-in timestamp (§22)**: `HtmlReportRenderer(DateTimeOffset? generatedAt = null)` — the default constructor produces a document with NO wall-clock timestamp, GUID, or other non-reproducible content at all, so rendering the same report N times is always byte-identical. A caller that explicitly wants a "Generated: ..." line supplies `generatedAt` themselves; the deterministic-rendering tests exercise only the default (no-argument) path, exactly as skill.md §22 asks ("exclude it from the deterministic renderer tests").
- **Secret/raw-config safety (§16-17) inherited from Phase 9A, not reimplemented**: since `HtmlReportRenderer` never touches anything but the already-safe DTO tree, the same structural guarantees apply verbatim — `Configuration` never carried raw file content (Phase 4E-1/6E), and Phase 8A/8B/8C never carry entity `Metadata` into any Migration-layer model. Verified independently for HTML with the same class of deliberately-sensitive test input Phase 9A used for JSON (`Password=`/`API_KEY=`/`Bearer `/PEM-shaped/`DB_PASSWORD=` values stashed in entity Metadata, confirmed absent from rendered HTML) plus an already-redacted-marker pass-through test.
- **HTML escaping (§18, actually adversarially tested)**: `HtmlReportRendererHtmlEscapingTests` feeds `<script>alert(1)</script>`, an attribute-breakout payload (`"><img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`), a `</script><script>evil()</script>` closing-tag-injection attempt, and mixed `& ' " < >` text as application names and as `Evidence.Detail`/executable-path content flowing through the real Risk/Migration pipeline — every one of the four renders as inert, encoded text; the exact unescaped dangerous substrings (`<script>alert(1)</script>`, `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`, `<Prod>`, `<script>document.location`) never appear anywhere in the output.
- **ERP fixture (§24)**: `HtmlReportRendererErpFixtureTests` runs the exact same 17-entity ERP fixture as every prior phase's own tests through the full pipeline, then asserts the rendered HTML contains the actual, previously-established semantics (`badge status-blocked` for the server, `badge status-needs-remediation` for ERP Web, `badge status-ready-with-conditions` for all three Batch boundaries, all 5 dependency-type group headings) read directly out of the rendered markup — never hard-coded or recalculated in `HtmlReportRenderer` itself. The shared `host.exe` dependency is confirmed to appear exactly once (not three times) within the Shared Infrastructure section specifically, by scoping the assertion to that section's own HTML slice.
- **Negative fixtures (§25)**: `HtmlReportRendererNegativeFixtureTests` covers empty report, no findings, Info-only, Low/Medium/High/Critical-risk scenarios (asserting the matching status/severity badge each time), shared dependency across 3 boundaries (one logical row), a server-only issue with no `ApplicationBoundary`, and expected-orphan runtime/certificate (no false actions) — 11 scenarios total, nothing lost in any of them.
- **No-mutation (§23)**: `HtmlReportRendererNoMutationTests` snapshots the same Id sequences Phase 9A's own no-mutation test checks (`ApplicationAssessments`, `Assessment.Server.Issues`/`.Dependencies`, `Plan.Actions`/`.PreMigrationChecks`/`.PostMigrationChecks`, `Diagnostics` counts) before and after three separate `Render()` calls — identical every time.
- **Performance (§26, actually executed)**: `HtmlReportRendererPerformanceTests` and `JsonReportRendererPerformanceTests` now share one extracted fixture, `Fixtures/SyntheticLargeReportBuilder` (10,000 entities, 1,000 `ApplicationBoundary`s, 10,000 `MigrationIssue`s, 5,000 `MigrationDependency`s, 10,000 `MigrationAction`s, 20,000 `MigrationVerificationCheck`s) — HTML rendering completed in **~384ms**, JSON in **~433ms** (run together), both well under the 10-second budget.
- **Project dependency review (§27, actually verified)**: `dotnet list src/ServerSleuth.Reporting/ServerSleuth.Reporting.csproj reference` still shows exactly one reference, `ServerSleuth.Analysis`; the `.csproj` still has zero `<PackageReference>` elements. No `ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Linux`/Infrastructure-scanner reference was added for Phase 9B, and no filesystem/process/network API is used anywhere in `Html/`.
- **Tests**: 50 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests/` (exact per-class counts from actual test-run output) — `HtmlReportRendererBasicTests` (6), `HtmlReportRendererErpFixtureTests` (7), `HtmlReportRendererCoverageTests` (3), `HtmlReportRendererHtmlEscapingTests` (6), `HtmlReportRendererSecretSafetyTests` (6), `HtmlReportRendererRawConfigurationSafetyTests` (2), `HtmlReportRendererUnicodeTests` (3), `HtmlReportRendererDeterminismTests` (5), `HtmlReportRendererNoMutationTests` (1), `HtmlReportRendererNegativeFixtureTests` (11), `HtmlReportRendererPerformanceTests` (1) — plus `Fixtures/SyntheticLargeReportBuilder` extracted (no new tests of its own; `JsonReportRendererPerformanceTests` refactored to use it with unchanged assertions).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       424 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:       90 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (40 pre-existing + 50 new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1265/1265 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
- **Known Limitations**: the HTML renderer's table-based layout degrades to a card-per-row layout below a 40rem viewport (a fixed CSS breakpoint, not a full component-level responsive redesign) — adequate for the "desktop/laptop/normal tablet width" range §21 asks for, not tuned for very narrow phone screens. No PDF renderer, no file-export/CLI composition layer, no HTTP API exists yet — all explicitly deferred per this phase's own strict-stop instruction (§32).
- **Phase 9C readiness**: `IReportRenderer` now has two working implementations sharing one DTO/security boundary, confirming the abstraction generalizes correctly. A CLI/file-export composition layer can write either renderer's `ReportRenderResult.Content` to disk using `.Encoding` with no further Reporting-layer changes needed; a future PDF renderer has a proven pattern to follow (reuse `Json/Dto`, escape everything, no analysis).
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 9C (Report Artifact & Local Export) below.

## Phase 9C — Report Artifact & Local Export

- **Architecture**: `ServerSleuth.Reporting/Export/` (`ReportArtifact`, `ReportBundle`, `ReportArtifactFactory`, `ReportOverwritePolicy`, `ReportExportResult`, `ReportBundleExportResult`, `ReportManifest`/`ReportManifestEntry`, `ReportFileNameValidator` (internal), `IReportExporter`, `LocalFileReportExporter`). Pipeline exactly as specified: `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` → `IReportRenderer` (unchanged, Phase 9A/9B) → `ReportRenderResult` → `ReportArtifact` (via `ReportArtifactFactory`) → `IReportExporter` → `LocalFileReportExporter`. No Phase 1-9B code was touched — `RiskEngine`/`RiskAggregator`/`MigrationAssessmentEngine`/`MigrationPlanEngine`/`ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine`/`JsonReportRenderer`/`HtmlReportRenderer` are all byte-for-byte unchanged; `ReportArtifactFactory` only ever calls the existing renderers.
- **First filesystem-writing component in Reporting, still platform-neutral (skill.md §14, §23)**: `LocalFileReportExporter` uses only `System.IO` BCL types (`Directory.CreateDirectory`, `FileStream`, `File.Move`, `File.Exists`, `File.Delete`) against the caller-specified output directory — no registry/process/network/Docker/Kubernetes access anywhere, and no reference added to `ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Linux`/any Infrastructure scanner project. Verified: `dotnet list src/ServerSleuth.Reporting/ServerSleuth.Reporting.csproj reference` still shows exactly one reference (`ServerSleuth.Analysis`); `dotnet list ... package` shows zero package references for `net8.0`.
- **ReportBundle consistency (skill.md §9-10)**: `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle` is the ONLY place a `ReportBundle` is constructed, and it renders both JSON and HTML from the same `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` argument in one call — there is no code path that could build a bundle whose two artifacts describe different assessments. Verified end-to-end by `ReportBundleConsistencyTests.JsonAndHtmlArtifacts_RepresentTheSameAssessment`, which follows skill.md §10's own 6-step scenario exactly (create assessment → render JSON → render HTML → export both → verify both exist → verify content corresponds to the same report) and cross-checks the shared `host.exe` dependency's Id appears identically (per-format-encoding) in both exported files.
- **Atomic write strategy (skill.md §6, documented in the exporter's own doc comment)**: write to a randomly-named temp file in the SAME directory as the final target (required — a cross-filesystem move/rename is never atomic), `FileStream.Flush(flushToDisk: true)`, close, then `File.Move(temp, final, overwrite)` — Windows uses a replace-file primitive when overwriting, Linux `rename(2)`, both atomic for same-filesystem renames. The one documented limitation: this is not atomic if the output directory itself spans a network-filesystem/bind-mount boundary, since that's outside any single-process API's control. Verified by `LocalFileReportExporterTests.Export_NeverLeavesATemporaryFileBehind_*` (asserts exactly one file — the final report, never a stray `.tmp`) both on success and on an expected `FailIfExists` failure.
- **Path safety (skill.md §7)**: `ReportFileNameValidator` (internal, defense-in-depth at the actual write boundary regardless of how a `ReportArtifact` was constructed) rejects any file name containing `..` (anywhere, not just a leading segment — catches `../../evil` and `..\..\evil`), a directory separator, a colon, any of `< > " | ? *`, control characters, or a rooted/absolute path. Deliberately does NOT rely solely on `Path.GetInvalidFileNameChars()`/`Path.IsPathRooted` — both are platform-dependent (e.g. `GetInvalidFileNameChars()` only returns NUL on Linux, which would let `<script>` through unchecked there) — so the check is an explicit, fixed blacklist that behaves identically on Windows and Linux. Verified against every example skill.md §7 names (`../../evil`, `..\..\evil`, `C:\evil`, `/var/tmp/evil`, `<script>`, `server:name`) plus `..`/`.`/empty/embedded-null/bare-separator variants — all 15 rejected, none ever reach the filesystem, and a parent-directory-escape attempt is confirmed to leave the parent directory's own file listing unchanged. `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle`'s optional `filePrefix` is validated the same way and throws `ArgumentException` immediately for an unsafe prefix rather than silently stripping characters.
- **Overwrite policy (skill.md §8)**: `ReportOverwritePolicy.FailIfExists` is the default on every public entry point; a second export to the same directory without explicitly requesting `Overwrite` fails as a `ReportExportResult` (never an exception, never a silent overwrite) and leaves the original file's content untouched. `Overwrite` still goes through the identical atomic temp-file-then-move path — verified by `Overwrite_ReplacesExistingFile_Successfully`.
- **Manifest / integrity (skill.md §11-12), opt-in and off by default**: `ExportBundle(..., includeManifest: true)` writes `report-manifest.json` — Format/FileName/ContentLength/SHA-256 per artifact only, no secret/credential/raw-configuration/environment-variable content (verified with the same class of deliberately-sensitive test input Phase 9A/9B used, confirmed absent from the manifest too). SHA-256 is computed over the EXACT byte buffer written to disk (`Encoding.GetBytes(Content)`, the same buffer `FileStream.Write` receives), so `SHA256(read(file)) == manifest.Sha256` holds by construction — verified by re-reading each exported file's bytes and independently recomputing the hash. `CreatedAt` is `null` unless a caller explicitly supplies one (mirrors `HtmlReportRenderer`'s `generatedAt` opt-in pattern from Phase 9B) — the hash/entry list itself is otherwise fully deterministic for the same bundle. The hash is documented as never used for server/report identity, only integrity verification (skill.md §12).
- **No analysis in the export layer (skill.md §2, §15)**: `LocalFileReportExporter`/`ReportArtifactFactory` never reference `DiscoveryEntity`, `RiskFinding`, `MigrationIssue`, or any Risk/Migration-rule type directly — their only inputs are `ReportRenderResult`/`ReportArtifact`/`ReportBundle` (already-rendered strings) and a target directory. No status/severity/dependency/application calculation exists anywhere in `Export/`.
- **No-mutation (skill.md §16)**: verified by reference/value snapshots of `ReportArtifact.Content` (string reference equality via `Assert.Same`, since strings are immutable — a mutating implementation would have to construct a new artifact, which none of the export code does), `ReportBundle`, and the underlying `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`'s own Issue/Action Id sequences, all unchanged after export.
- **Failure handling (skill.md §17)**: expected failures (existing file under `FailIfExists`, an output directory colliding with an existing plain file, an unsafe file name) are returned as `ReportExportResult { Success = false }` with a human-readable diagnostic — never thrown, never a stray/partial file left at the final path. Genuine programming errors (`null` artifact, `null`/empty output directory) still throw `ArgumentNullException`/`ArgumentException`, per skill.md's own "unexpected programming errors may still throw." `Export_ReportsSuccess_OnlyWhenFileActuallyExists` pins the "never claim success without a file on disk" invariant directly.
- **Windows/Linux path handling (skill.md §18)**: tested with both a relative output directory (resolved against the process's current working directory, verified via `Directory.SetCurrentDirectory`) and an absolute one (`Path.GetFullPath`), both succeeding and producing a rooted `OutputPath` in the result. `ReportFileNameValidator`'s explicit, non-platform-dependent character blacklist (above) is itself the primary §18 compliance mechanism — the same file name is accepted/rejected identically regardless of host OS.
- **ERP fixture (skill.md §21)**: `LocalFileExportErpFixtureTests` runs the exact same 17-entity ERP fixture as every prior phase's own fixture tests through the real pipeline, exports both `report.json`/`report.html` to a temp directory, and reads the files back from disk — confirming Server Blocked, ERP Web NeedsRemediation, BatchA/BatchB ReadyWithConditions, and the shared `host.exe` dependency appearing as exactly one logical entry (with 3 `AffectedBoundaryIds`) in both the parsed JSON and the HTML's own shared-infrastructure section. None of these values are hard-coded anywhere in `Export/` — the exporter has no awareness of what it's writing beyond bytes and a file name.
- **Negative fixtures (skill.md §20)**: `LocalFileExportNegativeFixtureTests` covers empty report, no applications, a critical-Blocked scenario, and both relative and absolute output directories — all exporting successfully with nothing lost.
- **Performance (skill.md §19, actually executed)**: `LocalFileExportPerformanceTests` reuses `SyntheticLargeReportBuilder` (10,000 entities, 1,000 `ApplicationBoundary`s, 10,000 `MigrationIssue`s, 5,000 `MigrationDependency`s, 10,000 `MigrationAction`s, 20,000 `MigrationVerificationCheck`s — the same fixture Phase 9A/9B's own performance tests use) and measures render (`ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle`, which itself calls both renderers) PLUS export (including manifest) together — completed in **~558ms**, well under the 10-second budget.
- **Security**: `ServerSleuth.Reporting.csproj` unchanged in reference shape — still exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Analysis`), zero `PackageReference`s. `LocalFileExportSecuritySafetyTests` re-verifies the Phase 9A/9B secret/raw-configuration-safety guarantee holds all the way through to the actual bytes on disk (JSON, HTML, and manifest files), not just the in-memory `ReportRenderResult.Content` those earlier phases checked.
- **Tests**: 56 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests/Export/` (exact per-class counts from actual test-run output) — `LocalFileReportExporterTests` (8), `LocalFileReportExporterPathSafetyTests` (15), `LocalFileReportExporterManifestTests` (6), `LocalFileReportExporterUnicodeTests` (5), `LocalFileReportExporterFailureTests` (4), `LocalFileReportExporterNoMutationTests` (3), `LocalFileExportErpFixtureTests` (2), `LocalFileExportSecuritySafetyTests` (6), `ReportBundleConsistencyTests` (1), `LocalFileExportNegativeFixtureTests` (5), `LocalFileExportPerformanceTests` (1).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       424 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:      146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (90 pre-existing + 56 new this session)
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1321/1321 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
- **Known Limitations**: atomic replace is only guaranteed for a same-filesystem temp-file-then-move — an output directory that itself spans a network-filesystem/bind-mount boundary is outside any single-process API's atomicity guarantee, and this implementation does not attempt to detect or work around that case. No CLI/GUI/PDF/HTTP-API/email/cloud-upload/service-installation/self-update capability exists anywhere — all explicitly out of scope, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction (§27).
- **Phase 10 CLI readiness**: `IReportExporter`/`LocalFileReportExporter` are exactly the primitive a CLI `scan --output <dir>` command needs — `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle(report)` then `exporter.ExportBundle(bundle, outputDirectory)` is the entire integration surface, with `ReportOverwritePolicy` and `includeManifest` already exposed as the two behavioral knobs a CLI would want to map to flags. No further Reporting-layer work is needed before a CLI composition layer can be built on top of it.
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 10A (CLI Foundation) below.

## Phase 10A — CLI Foundation

- **Architecture**: `src/ServerSleuth.Cli` (`serversleuth` executable) + `test/ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests`, added to `ServerSleuth.slnx`. Layout matches skill.md (Phase 10A) §3's suggestion: `Commands/` (`ScanCommand`), `Options/`, `Pipeline/`, `Output/`, `ExitCodes/`, plus `Composition/` (the DI composition root) and `CliApplication`/`Program.cs` at the project root. The CLI is thin by design — every file either parses/formats/orchestrates; none contains a business rule.
- **Multi-targeting, not `EnableWindowsTargeting`, for the Windows/Linux split (skill.md §2, §4)**: `ServerSleuth.Cli.csproj` targets `net8.0-windows;net8.0`. Discovered during implementation that a plain `net8.0` project cannot restore a `ProjectReference` to the `net8.0-windows`-only `ServerSleuth.Windows` project at all (NuGet's NU1201 compatibility check rejects it outright) — `EnableWindowsTargeting` only helps compile against Windows Desktop SDK *package* references, not an incompatible project-to-project reference. Multi-targeting is the correct, standard fix: the `net8.0-windows` build is what ships/runs on Windows (Windows discovery compiled in); the plain `net8.0` build is what ships/runs on Linux. A `SERVERSLEUTH_WINDOWS` compilation symbol (defined only for the `net8.0-windows` TFM) gates the `using ServerSleuth.Windows.DependencyInjection;`/`AddServerSleuthWindows()` call in `Composition/CompositionRoot.cs` — but the actual runtime decision of WHETHER to call it is still `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()`, identical in shape to the `OperatingSystem.IsLinux()` branch for `AddServerSleuthLinux()`. `DiscoveryEngine` itself remains completely untouched — still a plain `foreach (var scanner in registry.Scanners)` loop with zero platform awareness, exactly as skill.md §4 requires.
- **Composition root (`CompositionRoot.Build()`)**: `AddLogging()` (some scanners, e.g. `WindowsOsScanner`, take an `ILogger<T>` constructor dependency — discovered via a failing `CompositionRootTests` run against the real Windows registration, fixed by mirroring `CrossPlatformCompositionTests`' own `.AddLogging()` call) → `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` → (`AddServerSleuthWindows()` if Windows / `AddServerSleuthLinux()` if Linux) → `AddServerSleuthDiscoveryEngine()` (registered last, deliberately, per that method's own doc comment — it needs the platform registration's `IDiscoveryScanner`s already present). No second scanner registry, no manual scanner enumeration, no scanner instantiated directly by the CLI (skill.md §9).
- **Pipeline wiring (`ScanPipelineRunner`, skill.md §7-8)**: `DiscoverAsync` calls `IDiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` unchanged; `Analyze` runs `CorrelationEngine.Correlate` → `ApplicationBoundaryEngine.Analyze` → `DependencyExpansionEngine.Expand` → `GraphValidator.Validate` → `RiskAnalysisContext` → `RiskRuleEngine.Analyze` (the same 12 `IRiskRule` implementations as `RiskPipeline`/`TestPipeline` in the Analysis/Reporting test suites) → `RiskAggregator.Aggregate` → `MigrationAssessmentEngine.Assess` → `MigrationPlanEngine.Plan` → `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine.Build` — every call unmodified, in the exact order skill.md §7 lists. `AggregateDiscoveryResult`/`RiskAggregationResult`/`ServerMigrationAssessmentReport` are treated purely as pipeline artifacts the CLI passes along (skill.md §8) — no field of any of them is reinterpreted.
- **Output goes exclusively through Phase 9C (skill.md §10-11)**: `ReportExportRunner.Export` calls `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle(report)` (renders both JSON and HTML internally, from the one report instance — the bundle's own same-report guarantee) then `IReportExporter.Export` per-artifact — `--format json`/`--format html` write only the requested file; `--format both` writes both. There is no `File.WriteAllText` anywhere in `ServerSleuth.Cli`.
- **Exit codes (skill.md §15, no existing spec to conform to — verified: zero matches for "exit code" anywhere in skill.md/ARCHITECTURE.md/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md/PROGRESS.md, so this phase defines its own)**: `0` Success, `1` GeneralFailure, `2` InvalidArguments, `3` ExportFailure, `4` PartialDiscovery, `5` Cancelled (an addition beyond skill.md §15's "at minimum" four — collapsing a deliberate Ctrl+C into GeneralFailure would make the two indistinguishable to a calling script). `PartialDiscovery`'s trigger set (PartiallySupported/AccessDenied/Failed) deliberately mirrors Phase 8C's own `AssessmentCoverage.Limited`/`Partial` semantics rather than inventing a new classification — NotApplicable/NotInstalled are neutral, never treated as a fatal or even a "partial" condition (skill.md §16).
- **Cancellation (skill.md §18)**: `Program.cs` subscribes to `Console.CancelKeyPress`, setting `e.Cancel = true` (preventing the CLR's default abrupt-terminate behavior) and signaling a `CancellationTokenSource` whose token flows into `DiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` and is checked between the post-discovery pipeline stages. Combined with Phase 9C's own atomic-write guarantee, a cancelled scan never leaves a partially-written report file — verified by `ScanCommandCancellationTests` with a pre-cancelled token: exit code 5, a plain "Scan cancelled." message (no stack trace), and zero files written.
- **Error handling (skill.md §17)**: `CliArgumentException` (invalid command line) → exit 2, message only. `ReportExportRunner`/`IReportExporter` failures (existing file without `--overwrite`, an output directory colliding with an existing plain file) → exit 3, the exporter's own diagnostic messages printed, never a stack trace. Any other exception → exit 1, `ex.Message` only — there is no verbose/diagnostic flag in Phase 10A's own option surface (§6) to opt into more detail, so none was added (skill.md §17: "do not add excessive logging infrastructure in this phase").
- **Console output (`ScanProgressReporter`, skill.md §12)**: every number printed (`Entities`/`Scanners`/`Partial`, `Risk: Critical/High/Medium`, `Migration: Blocked/NeedsRemediation/ReadyWithConditions`) is read directly off an already-computed artifact (`AggregateDiscoveryResult`, `ServerRiskSummary`, `ServerMigrationSummary`) — the reporter formats, it never counts or classifies. `--quiet` suppresses every progress line; errors still print to stderr regardless (verified by `ScanCommandQuietModeTests`).
- **Security (skill.md §19, §25)**: the CLI never executes/connects-to/inspects a discovered command, service, application, database, HTTP endpoint, Docker socket, or `kubectl` — it only ever calls `IDiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` (which already owns every platform-specific read-only operation) and the existing Reporting/Export layers. `ScanCommandSecretSafetyTests` re-verifies, at the CLI's own console-output AND exported-file boundary, that deliberately-injected `Password=`/`API_KEY=`/`Bearer `/`DB_PASSWORD=`/PEM-private-key-shaped values never appear — reusing the exact class of test input Phase 4E/6E/8/9A/9B/9C already established.
- **Path safety (skill.md §20)**: the CLI passes only an output DIRECTORY to `IReportExporter` — report file names are always the fixed `ReportArtifactFactory` defaults (`report.json`/`report.html`); no discovered entity name is ever used to construct a path anywhere in the CLI. Phase 9C's own `ReportFileNameValidator` remains the enforcement point; Phase 10A adds no new path-construction logic of its own to bypass or duplicate it.
- **ERP fixture (skill.md §24)**: `ScanCommandErpEndToEndTests` runs the exact same 17-entity ERP fixture as every prior phase's own tests through a FAKE `IDiscoveryEngine` (substituting only for real OS scanning) followed by the REAL Correlation→Boundary→Expansion→Validation→Risk→Aggregation→Migration Assessment→Migration Plan→Consolidation→Render→Export pipeline — confirming Server Blocked, ERP Web NeedsRemediation, ERPWorker Blocked, BatchA/BatchB/BatchC each ReadyWithConditions, and the shared `host.exe` dependency as exactly one logical entry with 3 `AffectedBoundaryIds`, read directly out of the actual exported `report.json`/`report.html` files and the actual console output (`Blocked: 1`, `ReadyWithConditions: 3`) — nothing hard-coded in the CLI itself.
- **Platform composition tests (skill.md §23)**: `CompositionRootTests` verifies `CompositionRoot.Build()` resolves `IDiscoveryScannerRegistry`/`IDiscoveryEngine` without throwing on the current platform, that at least one scanner is registered whenever this specific build variant has that platform's registration compiled in (the plain `net8.0` TFM legitimately registers zero scanners when run on a Windows test host, since Windows scanning isn't compiled into that variant at all — verified via the same `SERVERSLEUTH_WINDOWS` symbol the production code uses), that no scanner Id is ever registered twice, and that repeated `Build()` calls produce independent providers. A genuine WSL2 Linux smoke-test run (the same validation approach Phase 6G used) was not repeated in this phase — the Linux registration path itself is unchanged from Phase 6A-G, already validated there; Phase 10A's own new surface (the composition root wiring) is exercised identically regardless of which platform's scanners it ends up registering.
- **Performance (skill.md §27)**: `ScanCommandErpEndToEndTests` (fake discovery, 17 entities, real pipeline start to finish, run inside the test suite) completes in **~130ms** — startup+discovery+analysis+rendering+export combined. A genuine real-machine run was also executed against this dev machine (`dotnet publish` → `serversleuth.exe scan`): discovery completed in well under 20 seconds, finding **34,930 entities across 12 scanners (4 partially supported)** — but the post-discovery analysis stage did not complete within a 9.5-minute observation window on that real, densely-interconnected entity set. See Known Limitations below; this is flagged as a pre-existing Analysis-layer performance characteristic at real-world scale, not a Phase 10A CLI defect — the CLI's own pipeline wiring is a direct, unmodified sequence of calls into already-existing, already-unit-tested engines, and no synthetic performance-budget regression was found in any existing Analysis/Reporting test (all of which continue to pass at their own established 10k/50k-scale budgets).
- **Security / project dependency review (skill.md §26, actually executed)**: `dotnet list src/ServerSleuth.Cli reference` — five `ProjectReference`s (`ServerSleuth.Core`, `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`, `ServerSleuth.Linux`, `ServerSleuth.Analysis`, `ServerSleuth.Reporting`) unconditionally, plus `ServerSleuth.Windows` conditionally under the `net8.0-windows` TFM only (visible directly in `ServerSleuth.Cli.csproj`'s own `<ItemGroup Condition="...">`, and functionally confirmed by `CompositionRootTests` actually resolving real Windows scanners under that TFM). `dotnet list src/ServerSleuth.Cli package` — `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection` and `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging`, both `10.0.11`, matching the exact versions already used elsewhere in the solution (`ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests`) — no new/different package version introduced.
- **Tests**: 46 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests/` (exact per-class counts from actual test-run output; each runs once per TFM — 92 executions total) — `CliApplicationHelpTests` (7), `ScanOptionsParserTests` (13, including `[Theory]` cases), `ScanCommandErpEndToEndTests` (5), `ScanCommandExportBehaviorTests` (4), `ScanCommandPartialDiscoveryTests` (3), `ScanCommandCancellationTests` (2), `ScanCommandQuietModeTests` (2), `ScanCommandSecretSafetyTests` (6), `CompositionRootTests` (4).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):

  ```
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       424 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ```

- **Known Limitations**: a real-machine scan of this dev host (34,930 discovered entities) did not complete the post-discovery analysis stage within a 9.5-minute observation window — flagged as a pre-existing Analysis-layer (Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation/Risk) scaling characteristic on dense real-world data, surfaced for the first time by this phase's own real-machine CLI run (no prior phase exercised the full pipeline against real discovery output at this scale — Phase 6G's real-machine validation covered Discovery only). Not fixed in Phase 10A, which is explicitly CLI-foundation-only (§30) and must not "reimplement any stage" or "move logic into the CLI" (§7); recommended as the first item for a future Analysis-layer performance investigation. No `--profile` CLI flag exists yet (Phase 10A's own §6 deliberately restricts the option surface; `DiscoveryContext.Profile` is fixed internally to `ScanProfile.Migration`, and no scanner currently reads `Profile` at all — confirmed by grep, zero references outside its own type definitions). GUI, PDF, HTTP API, email, cloud upload, migration execution, automatic remediation, service installation, and self-updating mechanisms remain out of scope, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction (§30).
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 10A-H (Real-Scale Analysis Performance Investigation) below.

## Phase 10A-H — Real-Scale Analysis Performance Investigation

- **Objective**: determine exactly which Analysis stage causes the real-machine hang documented as a Phase 10A known limitation (34,930 real entities; discovery completed under 20s; the post-discovery analysis stage did not complete within a 9.5-minute observation window). Diagnosis only — no production code was modified anywhere in this phase (skill.md §13, §18).
- **Instrumentation**: `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Diagnostics/StageMeasurement.cs` wraps an existing pipeline call with a plain `System.Diagnostics.Stopwatch` and an optional diagnostic-only observation timeout (`Task.Run` + `Task.Wait(timeout)`) — the wrapped call itself is never altered, reimplemented, or given different arguments than production supplies. `StageTiming.cs` is a safe-only record (durations/counts only — no raw entity/configuration content, no secrets, per §15). No telemetry, no network call, nothing sent anywhere — output goes to the test's own `ITestOutputHelper` and a local scratch file.
- **Realistic synthetic fixture**: `RealisticScaleFixtureBuilder.cs` builds a COM-heavy entity set (≈40% `ComComponent`, ≈40% `Dll`, remainder Services/ScheduledTasks/Applications/Configurations/Runtimes/Certificates) at a given scale, modeled on the real dev-machine scan that motivated this investigation (Phase 4B's own doc comment separately recorded that real machine's COM scanner alone finding ~14,189 real CLSIDs) — deliberately NOT the flat, disconnected entity lists Phase 8C/9A/9B's own synthetic performance fixtures use (those were built to exercise Reporting/Consolidation cheaply, not to reproduce Correlation/Expansion's real graph-density characteristics). No real configuration content or credentials anywhere.
- **Stage timings — actually executed, real numbers** (`PipelineTimingInvestigationTests`, a `[Theory]` over 1,000/10,000/35,000 entities; a 60-second per-scale, per-stage diagnostic observation cap applies — never a production timeout):

  | Stage | 1,000 entities | 10,000 entities | 35,000 entities |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Correlation | 1.5ms (997 in, 560 edges out) | 87.9ms (9,970 in, 5,600 edges out) | 1,255.6ms (34,720 in, 19,600 edges out) |
  | Boundary | 1.1ms (80 boundaries) | 76.5ms (800 boundaries) | 852.8ms (2,800 boundaries) |
  | Expansion | 271.5ms (590 edges out) | **>60,012ms (ABORTED)** | **>60,001ms (ABORTED)** |
  | Validation | 14.9ms (0 findings) | not reached | not reached |
  | Risk Analysis | 10.6ms (0 findings) | not reached | not reached |
  | Risk Aggregation | 3.4ms | not reached | not reached |
  | Migration Assessment | 3.0ms | not reached | not reached |
  | Migration Planning | 1.4ms | not reached | not reached |
  | Consolidation | 3.3ms | not reached | not reached |

  This directly explains the Phase 10A real-machine observation: the CLI prints "Analyzing dependencies..." immediately before this exact call sequence begins, and the transcript never showed a subsequent line — matching Expansion being the stage that never returns.
- **Bottleneck identification (confirmed, not merely suspected)**: `ServerSleuth.Core.Graph.DependencyGraph.AddEdge` does `_edges.FindIndex(e => ...)` — an O(edge-count) linear scan on every single edge insertion — and `EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo` are O(edge-count) `List<T>.Where(...)` scans, not indexed lookups. A grep across `src/` found 15+ call sites invoking `EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo` once per entity inside a loop (`GraphValidator`, `DependencyExpansionEngine`, `ApplicationBoundaryEngine`, and four Risk rules) — each such call site costs O(entities × edges) on its own. `DependencyExpansionEngine.BuildComAssociations` (`Correlation/Expansion/DependencyExpansionEngine.cs`) compounds this further: `foreach (var boundary in boundaries) { foreach (var com in context.ComComponents) { foreach (var comEdge in graph.EdgesFrom(com.Id)...) { ...memberIds.FirstOrDefault(m => graph.EdgesFrom(m).Any(...)) } } }` — an O(Boundaries × ComComponents × Edges) cost at minimum, explaining why Expansion's growth is measurably steeper than Correlation's/Boundary's own already-super-linear trend (see Complexity Assessment below).
- **Complexity assessment (from the actual measured numbers, not assumed)**: Correlation's exponent between 1k→10k is ≈1.77 and between 10k→35k is ≈2.13 (average ≈O(N^1.9), consistent with `AddEdge`'s O(E) cost applied E times ⇒ O(E²), with E∝N in this fixture). Boundary shows the same shape (≈1.84 then ≈1.92). Expansion's 1k timing (271.5ms) extrapolated at the SAME ~quadratic exponent would predict ≈25-30s at 10,000 entities — but it did not complete within 60,000ms, meaning its true exponent is measurably higher than quadratic, consistent with `BuildComAssociations`' additional nested-loop multiplier (Boundaries × ComComponents, both ∝N, on top of the O(E) `EdgesFrom` cost ⇒ ≈O(N³) at this fixture's proportions).
- **Graph size / entity distribution / edge distribution** (actual synthetic-fixture output, 35,000-entity scale): 34,720 entities built — Dll 14,000, ComComponent 14,000, Service 1,750, WebSite/ApplicationPool/Application/Configuration 1,050 each, ScheduledTask 700, Certificate 50, Runtime 20. 19,600 correlation edges — References 14,000, Runs 2,450, Hosts 1,050, Uses 1,050, Configures 1,050. (The real machine's own actual entity-type/edge-type breakdown was not separately re-captured in this phase — the synthetic fixture's proportions were deliberately modeled to approximate it, per §11's own instruction to use synthetic fixtures rather than real discovery data for the scaling investigation.)
- **Memory diagnostics**: not separately captured in this pass — every synthetic run completed (or was aborted) well within normal test-process memory limits with no `OutOfMemoryException`/GC-pressure symptom observed; a dedicated GC-allocation/working-set measurement was not performed, since the wall-clock bottleneck was unambiguous and conclusive without it. Documented here as an explicit gap, not silently omitted.
- **Real-machine re-run**: not repeated in this phase — the synthetic 10,000/35,000-entity investigation already reproduces the exact same stage (Expansion) failing to complete within a bounded diagnostic window, without needing a second multi-minute real-machine observation. This satisfies §9's intent (confirm which stage is responsible) without re-running an uncontrolled, multi-minute process against the dev host a second time.
- **No behavior changes (§13)**: `DependencyGraph`, every Correlation rule, `ApplicationBoundaryEngine`, `DependencyExpansionEngine`, `GraphValidator`, every Risk rule, `MigrationAssessmentEngine`, `MigrationPlanEngine`, `ServerMigrationAssessmentReportEngine`, and the CLI are all byte-for-byte unchanged from Phase 10A. Every stage call in the new diagnostic tests is the exact existing public API.
- **Security**: diagnostic output contains only stage names, durations, entity/edge/finding/boundary/dependency counts, and entity Type names — no raw configuration content, no secret-shaped value, no full entity metadata dump anywhere in `StageTiming`/`PipelineTimingInvestigationTests`.
- **Tests**: 3 new test cases in `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Diagnostics/PipelineTimingInvestigationTests.cs` (`Investigate(1000)`, `Investigate(10000)`, `Investigate(35000)`) — all "pass" in the xUnit sense (the test's job is to measure and report, not assert a performance bound; a stage exceeding the diagnostic cap is captured as data, not a test failure).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            42 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       427 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (424 pre-existing + 3 new)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests:             92 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:      146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1416/1416 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
  The full solution `dotnet test` run now takes ≈2 extra minutes versus Phase 10A's own baseline, entirely from the two 60-second diagnostic-observation timeouts the 10,000/35,000-entity cases deliberately hit — an accepted, documented cost of measuring this for real rather than only at a scale small enough to always finish quickly.
- **Known Limitations**: memory/GC diagnostics were not captured (see above). The synthetic fixture's proportions approximate, but were not verified byte-for-byte against, the real machine's own actual entity/edge-type breakdown. `GraphValidator`'s own per-node `EdgesFrom(node.Id).Any() || EdgesTo(node.Id).Any()` orphan-detection loop (line 333) was identified via code review as a further likely O(N×E) contributor but was never actually reached in the timed runs (Expansion aborted first at every scale ≥10,000) — flagged as suspected-but-not-empirically-confirmed. No optimization was implemented anywhere — per this phase's own strict-stop instruction (§18), the two identified root causes (`DependencyGraph`'s O(E) `AddEdge`/`EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo`, and `DependencyExpansionEngine.BuildComAssociations`' nested-loop structure) are documented as the recommended optimization targets only.
- **Recommended optimization target for a future phase**: replace `DependencyGraph`'s linear `_edges` scan-per-operation with an indexed structure (e.g. `Dictionary<string, List<DependencyEdge>>` keyed by source/target entity Id, maintained alongside the existing `List<DependencyEdge>`) so `AddEdge`'s duplicate-merge check and `EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo` become O(1)/O(degree) instead of O(E) — this alone would very likely resolve Correlation's and Boundary's super-linear growth and substantially reduce Expansion's. `BuildComAssociations`' own nested-loop structure (Boundaries × ComComponents) would still benefit from a second pass — e.g. pre-indexing COM→target edges once, outside the per-boundary loop — even after the `DependencyGraph` fix, since it currently re-scans the same COM component's edges once per boundary rather than once total.
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 10A-I (Dependency Graph Performance Optimization) below.

## Phase 10A-I — Dependency Graph Performance Optimization

- **Objective**: fix the two root causes Phase 10A-H diagnosed — `DependencyGraph`'s O(edge-count) `AddEdge`/`EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo`, and `DependencyExpansionEngine.BuildComAssociations`' O(Boundaries × ComComponents × Edges) nested-loop structure — while preserving every existing semantic (node/edge identity, edge dedup, evidence merging, highest-confidence-wins, edge/node ordering, graph cloning, graph validation, deterministic output) byte-for-byte. No new filesystem/process/network access anywhere; `DependencyGraph` remains pure in-memory; no thread-safety was added (none existed before, and nothing in this phase's own scope requires it).
- **`DependencyGraph` indexing (`src/ServerSleuth.Core/Graph/DependencyGraph.cs`)**: two new `private readonly Dictionary<string, List<int>>` fields — `_edgeIndicesBySource`/`_edgeIndicesByTarget` — store the **index into the canonical `_edges` list** for every edge whose source/target matches a given entity Id, rather than a second reference to the edge itself. `_edges` (the `List<DependencyEdge>`) remains the single authoritative, ordered collection; the two dictionaries are pure lookup accelerators, never exposed publicly (no public property, method, or constructor parameter references them). This index-of-index-not-index-of-value design was a deliberate deviation from the literal `Dictionary<string, List<DependencyEdge>>` shape a first read of the task suggests: storing indices instead of edge references means `AddEdge`'s existing evidence-merge/highest-confidence-wins behavior can update the one authoritative copy in place with a single `_edges[index] = merged` write, without needing to also locate and replace the same logical edge inside two more collections (source and target index) on every merge — avoiding a second O(in-degree) scan that a value-storing design would otherwise require on the (rarer, but real) duplicate-edge path.
  - **`AddEdge`**: the duplicate-merge lookup (`FindExistingEdgeIndex`) now scans only `_edgeIndicesBySource[edge.SourceEntityId]` — O(out-degree of the source entity) instead of O(total edge count). A genuinely new edge is appended to `_edges` and its index appended to both dictionaries — O(1) amortized. A duplicate is merged via `_edges[existingIndex] = existing with { Evidence = ..., Confidence = ... }` — identical evidence-union/highest-confidence-wins expression as before this phase; the index entries for that edge are untouched (source/target/type never change on a merge, so the recorded index still points at the right slot).
  - **`EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo`**: now `_edgeIndicesBySource`/`_edgeIndicesByTarget`, `TryGetValue` + `.Select(i => _edges[i])` — O(out-degree)/O(in-degree) instead of O(total edge count). Public signature (`IEnumerable<DependencyEdge> EdgesFrom(string sourceEntityId)`) is completely unchanged — no public API break anywhere in this phase.
  - **Ordering/determinism**: each per-source/per-target index list is built by appending in the exact same order edges are inserted into `_edges`, so `EdgesFrom("x").Select(i => _edges[i])` returns edges in identical relative order to the pre-Phase-10A-I `_edges.Where(e => e.SourceEntityId == "x")` — both are "canonical insertion order, filtered." Verified with a new `[Theory]` test (`EdgesFrom_And_EdgesTo_AreDeterministic_RegardlessOfInsertionOrder`) that builds the same five logical edges via two different insertion orders and asserts `EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo` return them in the order each run actually inserted them — never a raw `Dictionary`/`HashSet` enumeration order.
  - **Graph cloning**: the only clone path in the codebase, `DependencyExpansionEngine.CloneGraph`, builds a brand-new `DependencyGraph()` and calls the public `AddNode`/`AddEdge` for every source node/edge — since every `DependencyGraph` instance owns its own private `_edges`/`_edgeIndicesBySource`/`_edgeIndicesByTarget`, the clone's indexes are structurally guaranteed independent from the source's (no static or shared state anywhere in the class). `CloneGraph` itself required zero code changes. Independence was still verified explicitly with two new tests — `Expand_MutatingOriginalGraphAfterExpansion_NeverAffectsExpandedGraph` and `Expand_MutatingExpandedGraphAfterExpansion_NeverAffectsOriginalGraph` — each adds a probe edge to one graph after `Expand` returns and asserts the other graph's `Edges.Count`/`EdgesFrom` are completely unaffected, in both directions.
- **`DependencyExpansionEngine.BuildComAssociations` optimization (`src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Correlation/Expansion/DependencyExpansionEngine.cs`)**: replaced the `foreach (boundary) { foreach (com in ALL ComComponents) { EdgesFrom(com.Id)... } }` pattern with a pre-computed index built once for the whole expansion stage (not per boundary): `comsByReferencedBinary` (binary entity Id → every COM component with a References edge targeting it, built in one O(ComComponents) pass using the now-indexed `EdgesFrom`) and `comOrder` (COM Id → its position in `context.ComComponents`, to preserve original relative ordering among candidates). For each boundary, a small `candidateBinaryIds` set is built from the boundary's own members plus every binary any member IMPORTS — exactly the two target shapes Tier 1/Tier 2 already check, never a new association path — and `comsByReferencedBinary` is used to resolve this into a `candidateComIds` set (only COMs that could possibly match SOMETHING for this boundary). The original per-com, per-edge Tier-1-then-Tier-2, break-on-first-match logic is then run **unchanged**, just restricted to `candidateComIds` (ordered via `comOrder` to match `context.ComComponents`' original relative order) instead of iterating every COM component for every boundary. Tier 1 (COM's referenced binary is directly a boundary member → `Confidence.High()`) and Tier 2 (a boundary-member binary IMPORTS the COM-referenced binary → `Confidence.Medium()`) remain the only two association paths, byte-for-byte as before.
- **ERP fixture (skill.md §12 in the phase spec)**: `BuildErpFixture()`'s existing `DependencyExpansionEngineTests` (Certificate/COM/External-dependency/Runtime associations) all pass unchanged against the optimized code — confirmed by the full `Analysis.Tests` run below; no edge disappeared or newly appeared.
- **200-unrelated-COM negative fixture**: `Expand_LargeUnrelatedComPopulation_NeverAttachesToErpBoundary` (pre-existing, 200 synthetic unrelated COM registrations) still asserts exactly one legitimate COM association for the ERP boundary and `UnresolvedComRelationships.Count >= 200` — unchanged pass. A new, larger companion test (`Expand_ManyBoundariesAndManyUnrelatedComComponents_CompletesQuicklyAndOnlyAssociatesRealMatches`, 51 boundaries × 3,000 unrelated COM registrations) asserts the same zero-false-association guarantee at a scale specifically chosen to exercise the boundary × candidate-COM restructuring, plus a `< 10s` wall-clock bound (previously this exact shape of workload was the one that never completed within 60s at 10k+ scale).
- **Synthetic performance re-run** (`PipelineTimingInvestigationTests`, the exact same `[Theory]` over 1,000/10,000/35,000 entities from Phase 10A-H, actually re-executed 2026-08-24):

  | Stage | 1,000 entities | 10,000 entities | 35,000 entities |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Correlation | 0.6ms (997 in, 560 edges out) | 16.2ms (9,970 in, 5,600 edges out) | 36.8ms (34,720 in, 19,600 edges out) |
  | Boundary | 0.7ms (80 boundaries) | 48.1ms (800 boundaries) | 419.5ms (2,800 boundaries) |
  | Expansion | 5.8ms (590 edges out) | **16.5ms** (7,700 edges out) | **60.7ms** (26,950 edges out) |
  | Validation | 1.1ms (0 findings) | 36.4ms (0 findings) | 94.6ms (0 findings) |
  | Risk Analysis | 0.3ms | 32.7ms | 11.9ms |
  | Risk Aggregation | 0.1ms | 3.5ms | 0.5ms |
  | Migration Assessment | 0.1ms | 3.1ms | 0.2ms |
  | Migration Planning | 0.0ms | 1.5ms | 0.1ms |
  | Consolidation | 0.0ms | 3.1ms | 0.1ms |

  Expansion at 35,000 entities: **60.7ms**, down from Phase 10A-H's **>60,001ms (aborted, did not complete)** — over a 1,000x improvement, and no longer the dominant stage at any scale (Boundary — untouched by this phase, `ApplicationBoundaryEngine` — is now the largest single stage at 35,000 entities, at 419.5ms; flagged as a remaining bottleneck for a future phase, not addressed here per this phase's own strict scope). The complete synthetic Correlation→Consolidation pipeline finishes in ≈652ms at 35,000 entities, well under the phase's own "prefer under 10 seconds" target.
- **Real Windows machine re-run** (2026-08-24, same dev host as Phase 10A/10A-H, `dotnet build -c Release -f net8.0-windows` then `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch-dir> --overwrite`, bounded with a 300-second external timeout): Discovery completed as before — **34,925 entities across 12 scanners (4 partially supported)**. Analysis then **progressed past Expansion** (the stage that previously hung indefinitely, per Phase 10A's 9.5-minute observation and Phase 10A-H's diagnosis) and reached the Risk Analysis stage, where it failed with `Error: The given key 'MissingBinaryEntityId' was not present in the dictionary.` — exit was a clean, immediate error message (no stack trace printed, no hang), the CLI process exited on its own (no background process left running), and the output directory was left empty (no partial/corrupt report artifact was written). This is direct, real-machine confirmation that this phase's fix works: Correlation, Boundary, Expansion, and Validation now all complete successfully on the exact entity population that previously hung. The crash itself is a **separate, pre-existing bug**, unrelated to `DependencyGraph`/`BuildComAssociations` — traced to `RiskRuleEngine.Merge` (`src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Risk/Engine/RiskRuleEngine.cs`, Phase 7A), which unconditionally reads `primary.Metadata["MissingBinaryEntityId"]` after grouping findings by `f.Metadata.TryGetValue("MissingBinaryEntityId", ...) ? $"anchor:{anchor}" : $"solo:{f.Id}"` — a `"solo:{f.Id}"` group can legitimately contain more than one member if two distinct findings (from rules other than the four `MissingBinaryEntityId`-opt-in rules) happen to share the same `RiskFinding.Id` at real-world scale, and `Merge` is then called on a group where no member actually has the anchor key, throwing `KeyNotFoundException`. This bug was never reachable before this phase, because Expansion always hung first on real machine-scale data. Per this phase's own strict-stop scope (DependencyGraph indexing + COM lookup optimization + performance validation only), it was **not fixed** here — flagged as a Known Limitation and recommended as the next real-machine-driven follow-up phase.
- **Memory trade-off**: not separately profiled with a memory tool (no `dotnet-counters`/`dotnet-trace` run in this phase, consistent with Phase 10A-H's own "documented gap, not silently omitted" approach to memory diagnostics) — estimated instead from the index design: each edge now costs two additional `int` entries (one in `_edgeIndicesBySource`, one in `_edgeIndicesByTarget`, 4 bytes each plus `List<int>` growth overhead) versus zero before, and each distinct source/target entity Id that participates in at least one edge costs one additional `Dictionary` entry per index (bounded by distinct entity count, not edge count). At the 35,000-entity/26,950-edge synthetic scale this is on the order of a few hundred KB to low single-digit MB of additional managed heap — a deliberate, justified trade-off for converting O(edge-count) operations into O(degree) ones; no synthetic or real run in this phase showed any memory-pressure symptom.
- **Thread safety**: unchanged — `DependencyGraph` was not, and is still not, documented or used anywhere as a thread-safe type (every call site in the codebase constructs and mutates a `DependencyGraph` from a single thread/`async` continuation). No `ConcurrentDictionary`, lock, or other synchronization primitive was introduced in this phase, per its own explicit instruction not to add thread safety unless the existing contract already required it.
- **Public API**: unchanged. `DependencyGraph.Nodes`/`Edges`/`AddNode`/`TryGetNode`/`AddEdge`/`EdgesFrom`/`EdgesTo` all keep their exact pre-existing signatures; the two new index dictionaries are `private readonly` fields with no public accessor of any kind.
- **Security**: no new filesystem/process/network access anywhere in either changed file; `DependencyGraph` remains pure in-memory, `DependencyExpansionEngine` remains analysis-only over already-discovered entities/edges. The real-machine scan reused the exact same read-only `serversleuth.exe scan` entry point as Phase 10A — no service was stopped/restarted, no registry/IIS/systemd/file was modified, and no credential was ever read or used.
- **Tests**: 8 new tests. `test/ServerSleuth.Core.Tests/Graph/DependencyGraphTests.cs`: `EdgesFrom_UnknownSourceId_ReturnsEmpty`, `EdgesFrom_And_EdgesTo_AreDeterministic_RegardlessOfInsertionOrder` (`[Theory]`, 2 cases), `EdgesFrom_LargeGraph_ReturnsOnlyOwnEdges_AndScalesWithDegreeNotEdgeCount` (50,005-edge graph, 10,000 repeated lookups, structural + `<5s` bound), `AddEdge_ManyDuplicateInsertions_MergeInPlaceWithoutGrowingEdgeCount` (20,000-edge graph, 2,000 duplicate merges, structural + `<5s` bound). `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Correlation/Expansion/DependencyExpansionEngineTests.cs`: `Expand_MutatingOriginalGraphAfterExpansion_NeverAffectsExpandedGraph`, `Expand_MutatingExpandedGraphAfterExpansion_NeverAffectsOriginalGraph`, `Expand_ManyBoundariesAndManyUnrelatedComComponents_CompletesQuicklyAndOnlyAssociatesRealMatches` (51 boundaries × 3,000 unrelated COM components, `<10s` bound). All prefer structural assertions (edge counts, membership, confidence/evidence content, mutation independence) over wall-clock-only assertions; wall-clock bounds are included only as a generous regression guard (5-10s), not as the primary proof of correctness.
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            47 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (42 pre-existing + 5 new)
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       430 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (427 pre-existing + 3 new)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests:             92 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ```
