- **Known Limitations**: `ApplicationBoundaryEngine` (Boundary stage) is now the single largest synthetic-pipeline stage at 35,000 entities (419.5ms) — untouched by this phase, flagged as a candidate for a future targeted investigation if real-machine scale grows further. A separate, pre-existing bug in `RiskRuleEngine.Merge` (`MissingBinaryEntityId` key assumed present whenever a `Deduplicate` group has more than one member, which does not hold for `"solo:{f.Id}"` groups formed by an `Id` collision between findings from non-opt-in rules) was surfaced for the first time by this phase's real-machine run and was deliberately **not fixed**, per this phase's own strict-stop scope — recommended as the next phase's starting point, since it is now the sole remaining blocker to a real-machine scan completing end-to-end. Memory/GC impact of the new indexes was estimated, not measured with a profiling tool (see Memory Trade-off above).
- **Phase 10B readiness**: `DependencyGraph` and `DependencyExpansionEngine.BuildComAssociations` are no longer a scaling risk at real-machine entity counts — both complete in well under a second at 35,000 synthetic entities and were confirmed, on the real dev machine, to no longer be the stage that hangs. The one concrete blocker to a fully successful real-machine `scan` run is the pre-existing `RiskRuleEngine.Merge` bug documented above; everything else in the post-Expansion pipeline (Validation, Risk Aggregation, Migration Assessment, Migration Planning, Consolidation) already completes in milliseconds at 35,000-entity synthetic scale per the table above, and was never touched by this phase.
- **Next phase**: not started. Per this phase's own strict-stop instruction, no work on Phase 10B, CLI UX, configuration files, GUI, HTTP API, PDF, cloud upload, migration execution, remediation, or service/package installation has begun. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding — recommended next step is fixing the `RiskRuleEngine.Merge` bug this phase's own real-machine run surfaced.

## Phase 10A-J — RiskRuleEngine.Merge Real-Scale Bug Fix

- **Objective**: reproduce, isolate, and minimally fix the `RiskRuleEngine.Merge` `KeyNotFoundException` Phase 10A-I's real-machine run surfaced, so the CLI can complete a full end-to-end scan on this dev host. Correctness-only — no optimization, no CLI/Migration/Reporting/Export changes, no `ApplicationBoundaryEngine` work.
- **Reproduction (exact, actually executed 2026-08-24)**: since the CLI's own error path prints only `ex.Message` (by design, per Phase 10A §17 — no verbose/diagnostic flag exists), a throwaway diagnostic console harness (scratchpad-only, never checked into the repo) was built referencing the real `ServerSleuth.Core`/`Infrastructure`/`Windows`/`Analysis` projects directly, replicating `ScanPipelineRunner.Analyze`'s exact stage sequence (Discovery → Correlation → Boundary → Expansion → Validation → `RiskAnalysisContext` → `RiskRuleEngine.Analyze`) with a full `try/catch` around the Risk stage to capture the complete exception:
  ```
  Type: System.Collections.Generic.KeyNotFoundException
  Message: The given key 'MissingBinaryEntityId' was not present in the dictionary.
  StackTrace:
     at System.Collections.Generic.Dictionary`2.get_Item(TKey key)
     at ServerSleuth.Analysis.Risk.Engine.RiskRuleEngine.Merge(List`1 members) in ...\RiskRuleEngine.cs:line 100
     at ServerSleuth.Analysis.Risk.Engine.RiskRuleEngine.Deduplicate(List`1 findings, RiskDiagnostics diagnostics) in ...\RiskRuleEngine.cs:line 86
     at ServerSleuth.Analysis.Risk.Engine.RiskRuleEngine.Analyze(RiskAnalysisContext context) in ...\RiskRuleEngine.cs:line 50
  ```
  Re-run twice against the same real machine — both runs reproduced the identical exception type/message/stack trace (entity count varied slightly run-to-run, 34,904–34,930, from ordinary transient real-machine state — the failure shape itself was stable). Confirmed deterministic before any source line was touched, per this phase's own §2 instruction.
- **Root cause (proven, not assumed)**: instrumenting the harness to replicate `RiskRuleEngine.Deduplicate`'s own grouping key (`f.Metadata.TryGetValue("MissingBinaryEntityId", ...) ? $"anchor:{anchor}" : $"solo:{f.Id}"`) against the real 34,900+-entity raw finding set (100,972 raw findings from 12 rules) found **4,398 groups with more than one member, of which 4,348 were `"solo:"` groups** (no member carrying the `MissingBinaryEntityId` anchor at all) — exactly the shape `Merge` could not handle. Every one of these traced to `MissingDependencyRule` (RR1): it emits one `RiskFinding` per `GraphValidator` `"UnresolvedBinary"` validation finding (`MissingDependencyRule.cs:38`), and `GraphValidator.ValidateUnresolvedDependencies` (`GraphValidator.cs:507-515`) emits one such validation finding **per unresolved import name** on a DLL — so a real-world DLL with several unresolved imports (common for vendor/native DLLs importing multiple system libraries never discovered as entities; the worst observed case was 17 unresolved imports on one binary) produces that many `RiskFinding` objects for the exact same `SourceEntityId`. Since `RiskFinding.Id` is computed purely as `risk:{RuleId}:{SourceEntityId}:{normalized-related-ids}` (`RiskFinding.cs:46-53`) with `RelatedEntityIds` always empty for RR1, every one of those emissions computes the **identical deterministic Id** — a genuine, reachable "duplicate finding identity" case (one of the categories this phase's own §4 named), not a hash collision or a bug in `RiskFinding.ComputeId` itself. This is unreachable at every prior phase's own fixture scale: the 17-entity ERP fixture and every Analysis synthetic performance fixture only ever gave a binary zero or one unresolved import, never two or more.
- **`RiskRuleEngine.Merge` behavior (traced from the actual implementation, not assumed)**: `Merge` is called by `Deduplicate` for any group with `Count > 1`, regardless of whether that group is an `"anchor:"` group (explicit cross-rule merge opt-in via `Metadata["MissingBinaryEntityId"]` — the one case skill.md Phase 7A §25 actually specifies) or a `"solo:"` group (same-Id collision, no opt-in). Before this phase, `Merge`'s very first computed value, `anchor`, unconditionally indexed `primary.Metadata["MissingBinaryEntityId"]` — correct for the anchor case (every member of an `"anchor:"` group has that key, by construction of the grouping key itself), but never valid for a `"solo:"` group, where by definition no member has it. Every other part of `Merge` (evidence union via `GroupBy((Type,Location,Detail))`, `Severity = members.Max(...)`, `Confidence = ...OrderByDescending(...).First()`, `ContributingRules` metadata, the final `Id = RiskFinding.ComputeId("merged", anchor, relatedIds)`) is anchor-value-agnostic and already correct for either case once a valid anchor is available.
- **Minimal fix (`src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/Risk/Engine/RiskRuleEngine.cs`, `Merge`, one changed statement)**: the `anchor` computation now falls back to `primary.SourceEntityId` when `Metadata["MissingBinaryEntityId"]` is absent:
  ```csharp
  var anchor = primary.Metadata.TryGetValue("MissingBinaryEntityId", out var explicitAnchor)
      ? explicitAnchor
      : primary.SourceEntityId;
  ```
  For every existing `"anchor:"` group this is byte-for-byte the same value as before (the `TryGetValue` succeeds, taking the identical code path `primary.Metadata["MissingBinaryEntityId"]` always took). For a `"solo:"` group, every member already shares the exact same `SourceEntityId` by construction (that's what made their Ids identical in the first place per RR1's own Id-computation shape) — so `primary.SourceEntityId` is never a guess, it is the one value every member already agreed on. No other line in `Merge`, `Deduplicate`, or `Analyze` was touched. `MissingDependencyRule`/`GraphValidator` were **not** modified — the fix deliberately stays at the `RiskRuleEngine` layer per this phase's own "do not change RiskRule semantics" instruction, rather than changing what any rule emits.
- **Regression test** (`test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Risk/RiskRuleEngineTests.cs`, 3 new tests, synthetic — never depends on the real dev machine): `Analyze_SameRuleEmitsMultipleFindingsWithIdenticalId_NeverCrashes_MergesIntoOne` (one `FakeRule` emitting 3 distinct `RiskFinding` instances sharing one `RuleId`+`SourceEntityId`, no anchor metadata — the exact real-machine shape, minimized to 3 members instead of up to 17) asserts the engine no longer throws, produces exactly one merged finding, preserves all 3 distinct evidence records (none silently dropped), keeps `SourceEntityId` unchanged, and records `FindingsDeduplicated == 2`. `Analyze_SameRuleEmitsMultipleFindingsWithIdenticalId_RepeatedRuns_AreDeterministic` re-runs the same scenario twice and asserts identical merged Id/severity/evidence-set ordering. `Analyze_AnchorMerge_MergedFindingSourceEntityId_IsTheExplicitAnchor_NeverAFallback` is a regression guard on the pre-existing anchor-merge path, confirming the new fallback branch is never taken when an explicit anchor is present.
- **Shared infrastructure verification**: `SharedInfrastructureRuleTests`, `SharedInfrastructureAttributionHardeningTests`, and `SharedInfrastructureAttributionPerformanceTests` (Phase 7B's own multi-boundary hardening suite) all pass unchanged — the one-finding-at-server-level-with-all-affected-boundaries semantics, no arbitrary first-boundary ownership, and deterministic ordering established in Phase 7B were never touched by this fix (which only affects `Merge`'s anchor computation, not `SharedInfrastructureRule` or the aggregation layer that consumes its output).
- **Determinism**: `Analyze_RepeatedRuns_ProduceIdenticalResults_Deterministic` (pre-existing) and the two new same-Id-collision determinism tests all pass. The real-machine scan's own re-run (see below) produced the same shape of result (Critical/High/Medium counts, Blocked/NeedsRemediation counts) each time it was invoked in this phase.
- **No mutation**: `Merge`/`Deduplicate`/`Analyze` operate purely on the already-materialized `List<RiskFinding>` `raw` list built earlier in `Analyze` — none of the changed code reads or writes `DiscoveryEntity`/`DependencyGraph`/`ApplicationBoundary` state at all, so the pre-existing no-mutation contract for those inputs is structurally unaffected; no new test was needed to prove this, since the changed code has no reference to any of them.
- **Performance** (synthetic `PipelineTimingInvestigationTests`, same 1,000/10,000/35,000-entity `[Theory]`, re-executed 2026-08-24): Risk Analysis stage — 0.3ms → 11.1ms → 7.2ms (all `findings=0`, since the synthetic COM-heavy fixture's DLLs never have more than one unresolved import — the collision shape this fix handles is a real-machine characteristic, not reproduced by the existing synthetic fixture). No material regression versus Phase 10A-I's own numbers (0.3ms → 32.7ms → 11.9ms) — the fix adds one `Dictionary.TryGetValue` call only inside `Merge`, which is only ever invoked for an already-rare multi-member group. No new scaling problem was found or needed to be deferred.
- **Real Windows machine re-run** (2026-08-24, same dev host, `dotnet build -c Release -f net8.0-windows` then `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch-dir> --overwrite`): **the scan completed end to end for the first time.**
  ```
  Discovery complete
    Entities: 34930
    Scanners: 12
    Partial:  4

  Analyzing dependencies...
  Analysis complete

  Migration assessment complete

  Risk:
    Critical: 317
    High:     17343
    Medium:   253

  Migration:
    Blocked:             72
    NeedsRemediation:    30
    ReadyWithConditions: 0

  Writing reports...
    report.json
    report.html

  Completed.
  ```
  Risk Analysis completed without error. The pipeline then continued, unprompted, through Migration Assessment, Migration Planning, Consolidation, and Reporting/Export — `report.json` (263MB) and `report.html` (200MB) were both written to the scratch output directory, matching the CLI's own `--format both` default. No further blocker appeared anywhere in the pipeline. The process exited cleanly (exit code 0) with no background process left running; the scratch output directory was deleted after inspection (report files are session-scratch artifacts, not part of the repository). The scanned machine's configuration was never modified — the same read-only `serversleuth.exe scan` entry point as every prior real-machine run in this project was used, with no service/registry/IIS/scheduled-task/configuration change, no database connection, and no discovered application executed.
- **Security**: the fix is a one-line change to an existing in-memory `Dictionary<string,string>` lookup inside `ServerSleuth.Analysis` — no filesystem, registry, process, or network access was added anywhere; `dotnet list src/ServerSleuth.Analysis/ServerSleuth.Analysis.csproj reference`/`package` still shows exactly one `ProjectReference` (`ServerSleuth.Core`) and zero `PackageReference`s, unchanged from every prior Analysis-layer phase.
- **Tests**: 3 new tests in `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Risk/RiskRuleEngineTests.cs` (listed above under Regression Test). No existing test was deleted, weakened, or had its assertions loosened.
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```

  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       433 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (430 pre-existing + 3 new)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  62 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped

  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1427/1427 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```

- **Known Limitations**: `MissingDependencyRule` itself still emits one `RiskFinding` per unresolved import (now safely absorbed by `Merge`'s generalized dedup rather than crashing) — the underlying report will show one finding per DLL with all its missing-import evidence merged together, which is the semantically correct end state, but this phase deliberately did not touch `MissingDependencyRule`/`GraphValidator` themselves (that would be a RiskRule-semantics change, explicitly out of scope). `ApplicationBoundaryEngine` remains the largest single synthetic-pipeline stage at 35,000 entities (documented in Phase 10A-I, untouched here).
- **Next bottleneck**: none found. This was the sole blocker to a complete real-machine end-to-end scan; the real-machine run in this phase completed Discovery through Export without any further error.
- **Phase 10B readiness**: the full pipeline (Discovery → Correlation → Boundary → Expansion → Validation → Risk → Aggregation → Migration Assessment → Migration Planning → Consolidation → Reporting → Export) now completes successfully end to end on real, dense, 34,900+-entity machine data for the first time in this project's history. No known blocker remains to a real-machine scan completing.
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 10B (CLI Production Hardening) below.

## Phase 10B — CLI Production Hardening

- **Objective**: turn the existing Phase 10A/10A-J CLI foundation into a production-quality command-line interface, without reimplementing Discovery/Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation/Risk/Migration/Reporting/Export — the CLI stays a thin composition/orchestration layer over all of them.
- **Inspection first**: read every file under `src/ServerSleuth.Cli/**` and `test/ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests/**` before changing anything (skill.md Phase 10B §2). Confirmed already present and working: `Program.cs` (Ctrl+C wiring), `CliApplication` (top-level dispatch/error-mapping), `ScanCommand` (pipeline orchestration), `ScanOptions`/`ScanOptionsParser` (hand-rolled, 4 options), `ScanProgressReporter` (concise progress), `HelpText`/`VersionInfo`, `CliExitCode` (6 codes), `CompositionRoot` (platform selection via `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()`/`IsLinux()`), `ReportExportRunner`/`ScanPipelineRunner` (Phase 9C/pipeline reuse), and 46 existing tests across 8 test files plus fakes/fixtures. Genuinely missing, per this phase's own §5-9/§13/§22: a `--verbose` option surfacing real per-scanner status/counts and stage durations (§5-6), and test coverage for `CliExitCode.GeneralFailure` and for cancellation requested *during* discovery rather than only before it starts (§8-9, §22-G/I).
- **CLI command contract (unchanged)**: `serversleuth scan` remains the entire primary workflow; `--output`/`--format`/`--overwrite`/`--quiet` are unchanged. One new option added, after evaluating it against the existing architecture and testability (§3): `--verbose`. Considered and deliberately **not** added: `--no-color`/color output at all — the existing progress reporter carries zero ANSI/color today, every stage/status is already unambiguous as plain text, and introducing conditional color would add untested-in-CI complexity (a headless test runner) for no required behavior change; `--verbose` alone satisfies §5-6's actual requirement (clear stage/scanner communication). No CLI parsing framework was introduced — the existing 4-option hand-rolled `switch`-based parser now has 5 arms, still simpler and more directly testable than a library dependency would be.
- **Platform selection (unchanged)**: `CompositionRoot.Build()` was not touched — `AddServerSleuthWindows()`/`AddServerSleuthLinux()` selection via `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()`/`IsLinux()` remains the sole platform-dispatch point; no `if Windows then ... if Linux then ...` branch of any kind exists anywhere in `ScanCommand`/`ScanPipelineRunner` (both call only the platform-agnostic `IDiscoveryEngine.RunAsync`).
- **Progress reporting (`ScanProgressReporter`)**: `WriteDiscoveryComplete`/`WriteAnalysisComplete` now take a `TimeSpan elapsed` (measured by a plain `System.Diagnostics.Stopwatch` in `ScanCommand`, a CLI-layer-only concern — no domain model changed) and print a `Duration: X.XXs` line **only when `--verbose` is set** — the pre-existing exact console text (`"Discovery complete"`, `"Analysis complete"`, `"Completed."`, etc.) that existing tests already assert on byte-for-byte was left untouched, so no existing test needed to change. A new `WriteScannerBreakdown` method (verbose-only) prints one line per scanner in `AggregateDiscoveryResult.ScannerResults`' own registry order — `ScannerId`/`Status`/`Entities.Count`, all read directly off that scanner's own `DiscoveryResult`, never fabricated, reclassified, or reordered (skill.md §6). `--quiet` and `--verbose` together: quiet wins (there is no progress output left for verbose to add detail to) — verified by a dedicated test.
- **Partial support (unchanged, re-verified at real scale)**: a scanner reporting `AccessDenied`/`PartiallySupported`/`Failed` never aborts the pipeline — confirmed on both real machines in this phase's own acceptance runs (Windows: 4 partial of 12 scanners; Linux: 6 partial of 11 scanners), both producing a full report and exiting `PartialDiscovery` (4), never silently converted to `Success`.
- **Exit codes (unchanged — all 6 now have direct test coverage)**: `CliExitCode` itself was not modified. The one previously-untested code, `GeneralFailure` (1), now has 2 dedicated tests (`ScanCommandGeneralFailureTests.cs`) using a new `ThrowingFakeDiscoveryEngine` that throws a non-cancellation exception — confirms the concise-message/no-stack-trace/no-partial-report contract `CliApplication`'s existing `catch (Exception ex)` block already implemented. `Success`/`InvalidArguments`/`ExportFailure`/`PartialDiscovery`/`Cancelled` already had coverage from Phase 10A and remain unchanged.
- **Cancellation (deepened, not changed)**: the existing pre-cancelled-token tests remain; a new `CancellingFakeDiscoveryEngine` and test (`CancellationDuringDiscovery_StopsBeforeAnalysis_ReturnsCancelledExitCode_NoReportWritten`) deterministically reproduces cancellation requested **while** discovery is running (not only before the scan starts) — the fake cancels the exact `CancellationTokenSource` the test holds from inside its own `RunAsync`, then throws via `ThrowIfCancellationRequested`, exactly like a real scanner loop checking its token mid-run would. Confirms: cancellation exit code, concise "Scan cancelled." message, no stack trace, and — critically — no report file written, since cancellation is detected before Analysis/Export ever run. Phase 9C's atomic-write guarantee was never touched.
- **Output formats/directory (unchanged)**: `ReportExportRunner`/`LocalFileReportExporter`/`ReportArtifactFactory`/`IReportExporter` are byte-for-byte unchanged — no `File.WriteAllText`, no second export implementation, no PDF/HTTP/email/cloud-upload/database output anywhere in the CLI. Existing tests already cover valid/existing/colliding output directories and `FailIfExists`/`Overwrite`; no new export-path test was needed since none of that layer changed.
- **Verbose mode secret safety**: `ScanCommandSecretSafetyTests`'s existing deliberately-injected-secret test now also runs with `--verbose` (the mode that surfaces the MOST console detail) — confirms the existing secret-safety guarantee holds under the new, larger output surface, not just the default progress view. No new secret-shaped data source was introduced by `--verbose` — scanner Id/Status/count are structural metadata, never raw configuration/credential content.
- **Real Windows machine acceptance** (2026-08-24, same dev host, `dotnet build -c Release -f net8.0-windows` then `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch> --overwrite --verbose`):

The Linux CLI binary (`net8.0` TFM) was run via `dotnet serversleuth.dll scan --output /tmp/... --verbose` (an apphost-vs-hostfxr resolution quirk in this container-less WSL setup required invoking through `dotnet` directly rather than the native apphost — a tooling detail, not a ServerSleuth behavior):

Both `report.json`/`report.html` were written and verified on disk; no lingering process; scratch output removed after inspection.
- **Performance**: not modified in this phase (§18) — Analysis-layer performance work is Phase 10A-I/10A-J's, already complete. CLI-layer overhead (argument parsing, progress rendering, `Stopwatch` measurement, export orchestration) is negligible next to Discovery/Analysis on both real machines above (Windows: 22.1s total, of which Discovery+Analysis account for 19.2s; Linux: total scan completed in low single-digit seconds). No optimization was attempted or needed.

Both real-machine runs in this phase used only ServerSleuth's own existing discovery — no service/registry/IIS/systemd/cron/Docker/Kubernetes modification, no discovered application executed, no discovered database connected to, no report uploaded anywhere.
- **Project dependencies**: unchanged — no new package or project reference was added anywhere in `ServerSleuth.Cli`/`ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests` for this phase; `--verbose` was implemented entirely with the existing hand-rolled parser and `System.Diagnostics.Stopwatch` (BCL, no package).

No existing test was deleted or weakened.

- **Known Limitations**: no color/`--no-color` was implemented (a deliberate scope decision, documented above and in ARCHITECTURE.md's Decisions Log) — every progress line remains plain, uncolored text. No `--version`-source-of-truth beyond the existing `VersionInfo` (unchanged from Phase 10A — this phase found no duplicated version metadata to reconcile, so none was introduced). Verbose duration figures are wall-clock `Stopwatch` measurements of the CLI's own two coarse stages (Discovery, Analyze) — no finer per-sub-stage (Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation/Risk/Migration individually) timing is exposed at the CLI layer, since that would require either threading `Stopwatch`s through `ScanPipelineRunner.Analyze` (a pipeline-layer change, arguably beyond "thin CLI orchestration") or re-deriving stage boundaries the CLI doesn't otherwise need — left as a candidate for a future phase if finer-grained verbose timing is ever requested. The Linux CLI binary requires running via `dotnet <dll>` rather than its native apphost in this specific WSL setup (a `libhostfxr.so` resolution quirk unrelated to ServerSleuth's own code) — not a ServerSleuth defect, and not investigated further since it did not block the real Linux acceptance run.
- **Phase 10C readiness**: the CLI is now production-hardened per this phase's own scope — real per-scanner visibility, complete exit-code test coverage, deterministic mid-run cancellation coverage, and two genuine real-machine (Windows + Linux) end-to-end acceptance runs, both producing correct exit codes and complete reports. No known blocker remains in the CLI layer itself.
- **Next phase**: Complete — Phase 10C (Target Abstraction & Remote Discovery Architecture) below.

## Phase 10C — Target Abstraction & Remote Discovery Architecture

- **Objective**: introduce the architectural foundation for a future explicit scan-target concept (local today, remote someday) — target identity, a local target implementation, a remote-transport boundary, a security/credential model — WITHOUT implementing any actual remote transport (no SSH/WinRM/PowerShell Remoting/RDP/remote shell/remote command execution/credential storage anywhere in this phase).
- **Inspection first (skill.md §1)**: read `DiscoveryContext`, `IDiscoveryScanner`, `IDiscoveryEngine`/`DiscoveryEngine`, `CompositionRoot`, `ScanCommand`, and `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`/`IPortInspector` before writing anything. Confirmed: `DiscoveryEngine` (`src/ServerSleuth.Core/Orchestration/DiscoveryEngine.cs`) is ALREADY fully platform-neutral — a plain `foreach (scanner in registry.Scanners)` loop with zero `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()`/`IsLinux()` branching anywhere in it; platform selection already happens exactly once, in `CompositionRoot.Build()`. `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` were ALREADY structured (`ProcessRequest.Executable`+`Arguments` kept separate, never a shell string) and platform-agnostic in shape — their current implementations (`ProcessRunner`, `FileSystemReader`) are local-only, but nothing in their signatures assumes that. The one genuine gap: nothing anywhere represented "what is being scanned" as an explicit, nameable concept — `scan` implicitly always meant "this machine," with no data structure saying so.
- **Target model (`src/ServerSleuth.Core/Targets/`)**: `ScanTarget` (a `sealed record` — Id/Kind/Platform/DisplayName), `TargetKind` (`Local`/`Remote`), `TargetPlatform` (`Unknown`/`Windows`/`Linux` — deliberately distinct from the existing `PlatformSupport` flags enum, which describes what a SCANNER supports, not what a TARGET is). `ScanTarget.Local(platform, displayName)` always produces the same fixed literal Id, `"local"` — never a hostname/GUID/timestamp, since there is exactly one logical "the machine ServerSleuth is running on" (skill.md §2, §15: "do not use random GUIDs for the same logical target... two identical local targets must produce the same target identity"). `ScanTarget.Remote(hostName, displayName)` exists ONLY as data — normalizes (trim + lowercase) the host name into a deterministic `"remote:{host}"` Id, but nothing in the codebase can act on a `Remote`-kind target; it exists so the CLI can represent "the user asked for a remote target" and reject it clearly (see below) rather than needing a second, parallel concept. `ScanTarget` carries NO password/API-key/SSH-key/token/credential field of any kind — mechanically verified, not just documented (`ScanTargetTests.PublicProperties_NeverLookLikeACredential`, a reflection-based negative test over every public property name).
- **`DiscoveryContext.Target`**: a new, NON-`required` property (`ScanTarget Target { get; init; } = ScanTarget.Local();`) — deliberately not a second, parallel "target execution context" type. `DiscoveryContext` already IS the execution context passed to every scanner (Profile/CancellationToken/ScanRoots), so reusing it (skill.md §1: "reuse existing abstractions whenever possible") avoids introducing a redundant type. Being non-required means every one of the dozens of existing `new DiscoveryContext { ... }` call sites across the whole test suite kept compiling completely unchanged — verified by the full regression below.
- **Remote transport boundary (`src/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Targets/`)**: `ITargetTransport` (Target/ProcessRunner/FileSystemReader — three properties, zero methods of its own) and `LocalTargetTransport` (its only implementation), living in `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`, not `ServerSleuth.Core` — `ITargetTransport` composes `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` (Infrastructure types), and Core must never depend on Infrastructure (the existing, one-directional Core→Infrastructure→Windows/Linux→Analysis→Reporting→Cli layering — confirmed unbroken by a new architecture test, `TargetAgnosticismTests.ServerSleuthAnalysis_HasNoProjectReferenceToServerSleuthInfrastructure`, applied one layer further up the same chain). `LocalTargetTransport` is a plain, zero-behavior-change wrapper around the SAME already-registered `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` singletons every scanner already receives via direct DI injection — registering it (`AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()`, one new `services.AddSingleton<ITargetTransport>(...)` call) changes nothing about how any scanner runs. This directly answers skill.md §5's "Target → Transport abstraction → Remote target" shape: a future remote transport would be a second `ITargetTransport` implementation (e.g. an SSH-backed one) supplying its OWN `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` implementations — Discovery/`IDiscoveryScanner`/`DiscoveryEngine` would never need to know or care which one is in play, since scanners already only ever depend on the existing `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` interfaces, not on `ITargetTransport` itself.
- **Command execution security (skill.md §6, re-verified, not re-designed)**: `IProcessRunner` still has exactly one method, `RunAsync(ProcessRequest, CancellationToken)` — no `Execute(string command)` was added, and none could be, without also changing the single existing interface every scanner already depends on. `ITargetTransport` defines zero methods of its own (only property getters) — mechanically verified by `NoArbitraryShellExecutionTests` (4 new tests): `IProcessRunner` has exactly one method taking a structured `ProcessRequest`; `ProcessRequest.Executable`/`Arguments` remain separate; `ITargetTransport` has no `Execute`/`RunCommand`/`Shell`/`Invoke`/`Eval`-named member of any kind; `ITargetTransport` declares no method beyond its property accessors.
- **Filesystem/process/network abstraction review (skill.md §7)**: `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` require NO signature change to support a future remote implementation — both already operate on structured requests/results with no assumption baked in that execution is local (no `Process.Start`-specific type, no local-only path validation in the interface itself). `IPortInspector` was reviewed but deliberately left OUT of `ITargetTransport` — it has no cross-platform default implementation to wrap (Windows/Linux each register their own `IPortInspector` directly, not through `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()`), so including it in a cross-platform transport boundary would require DI restructuring beyond this phase's own scope; documented as a Known Limitation below, not silently omitted.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §8)**: no credential type, interface, or store was introduced anywhere — a deliberate, explicit non-decision, not an oversight. `ScanTarget`/`ITargetTransport`/`LocalTargetTransport` carry no credential-shaped field (mechanically verified, see above). No `DiscoveryEntity`/`EvidenceRecord`/`RiskFinding`/`MigrationIssue`/Report DTO gained any new field in this phase at all — the target model exists entirely in Discovery/CLI-layer types, never touching a domain/report model, so there is no new surface for a credential to leak through even in principle.
- **Platform resolution (skill.md §9)**: a local target's platform is resolved exactly once, in `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` (`ResolveLocalPlatform()`, a private static helper calling `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()`/`IsLinux()` — the SAME cheap, instantaneous, non-network runtime query `CompositionRoot.Build()` already uses for platform DI selection), never by probing a remote machine (there is nothing to probe — no remote transport exists). `AddServerSleuthWindows()`/`AddServerSleuthLinux()` were not touched.
- **DiscoveryEngine (skill.md §10)**: unchanged, zero platform branching — confirmed both by direct inspection (unchanged file) and by the fact that no scanner or the engine itself references `ScanTarget`/`ITargetTransport` anywhere (grep-verifiable, and implicitly covered by the target-agnosticism architecture tests below being scoped to the WHOLE `ServerSleuth.Analysis` assembly, one layer further downstream, which structurally could not compile if `DiscoveryEngine`/scanners routed target-specific data through it).
- **CLI target option (skill.md §11)**: `serversleuth scan --target local` is accepted (case-insensitive) and behaves identically to omitting `--target` entirely — verified by a direct exit-code-equality test. Any other value (`--target server1`, `--target 192.168.1.10`, `--target ""`, etc.) is rejected with `CliExitCode.InvalidArguments` and a message naming the rejected value plus "not yet implemented" — never silently treated as local, and never reaching the discovery pipeline at all (verified: no report file is ever written for a rejected target). No `--ssh`/`--winrm`/credential-shaped option of any kind exists.
- **Local behavior regression (skill.md §12)**: `serversleuth scan` (no `--target`), `--output`/`--format`/`--overwrite`/`--quiet`/`--verbose`, and all 6 `CliExitCode` values are unchanged — confirmed by the full regression below (every pre-existing test, including every Phase 10A/10B test, passes unmodified) plus two genuine real-machine acceptance runs (see below) producing semantically identical results to Phase 10B's own runs.
- **Analysis is target-agnostic (skill.md §13)**: verified structurally, not just by convention — `TargetAgnosticismTests` (3 new tests, `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Architecture/`) reflect over the WHOLE compiled `ServerSleuth.Analysis` assembly and assert no public method parameter, no public method return type, and no public property anywhere in it is `ScanTarget`/`ITargetTransport`/`DiscoveryContext` — plus a direct assembly-reference check confirming `ServerSleuth.Analysis` has no reference to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` at all (so it could not even compile against `ITargetTransport` if it tried). `Correlation`/`Boundary`/`Expansion`/`Validation`/`Risk`/`Migration`/`Reporting` were not touched.
- **Reporting security (skill.md §14)**: no report DTO, JSON schema, or HTML template gained a field for target/transport/credential data — Reporting was not touched at all in this phase. Target identity is surfaced ONLY at the CLI console, ONLY in `--verbose` mode (`Target: local (Windows)`/`Target: local (Linux)` — `ScanTarget.Id`/`Platform` only, nothing else exists on the type to leak), and never written to any exported report file.
- **Determinism (skill.md §15)**: `ScanTarget.Local(...)` called 100 times in a loop produces exactly one distinct Id (`ScanTargetTests.Local_NeverUsesARandomIdentifier_AcrossManyConstructions`); two `ScanTarget.Local(TargetPlatform.Windows, "Dev Box")` instances are `record`-equal (structural equality, free). Since exactly one target now exists per scan (`DiscoveryContext.Target` defaults to the one local target, and the CLI always resolves the SAME `ITargetTransport.Target` from DI), entity Id/dependency-graph/risk/migration/report ordering are provably unaffected — none of those computations read `DiscoveryContext.Target` at all (per the target-agnosticism tests above).
- **Real Windows machine acceptance** (2026-08-24, same dev host, `dotnet build -c Release -f net8.0-windows` then `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch> --overwrite --verbose`): **34,939 entities**, `Target: local (Windows)` printed first, 4/12 scanners partial, Discovery 17.66s + Analysis 1.41s, Risk 317/17343/253, Migration 72 Blocked/30 NeedsRemediation. Confirmed real exit code (direct capture, not through a piped `tail`): **4 (PartialDiscovery)**. Both `report.json`/`report.html` written under a separate `--quiet --overwrite` re-run. Additionally verified: `serversleuth.exe scan --target bogus-remote-host` printed `Unsupported target 'bogus-remote-host'. Only 'local' is supported in this version of ServerSleuth — remote scanning is not yet implemented.` and exited **2 (InvalidArguments)** — no discovery, no report file, no network contact of any kind. Scratch output deleted after inspection; no lingering process; the scanned machine's configuration was never modified.
- **Real Linux machine acceptance** (2026-08-24, same reused WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS instance, built/run directly from the Windows-mounted path as in Phase 10B): `serversleuth scan --output /tmp/... --verbose` — **1,642 entities**, `Target: local (Linux)` printed first, 6/11 scanners partial, Discovery 3.92s + Analysis 0.07s, Risk 21/10/19, Migration 21 Blocked/13 ReadyWithConditions. Confirmed real exit code (single-line WSL invocation, avoiding the multi-line-heredoc `$?` artifact Phase 10B's own notes flagged): **4 (PartialDiscovery)**. `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests` also re-run natively inside WSL2: 68/68 passed, both TFMs. Scratch output removed after inspection; no remote machine was contacted anywhere in this validation (WSL2 is itself a local VM on this same physical machine, not a remote target).
- **Performance (skill.md §19, not optimized, merely confirmed negligible)**: the new `ITargetTransport` resolution adds one extra `GetRequiredService<ITargetTransport>()` DI call and one `ScanTarget` record construction per scan — both real-machine runs above show Discovery/Analysis timings statistically indistinguishable from Phase 10B's own numbers (Windows Discovery 17.94s→17.66s, Analysis 1.28s→1.41s; Linux Discovery 3.80s→3.92s, Analysis 0.06s→0.07s — normal run-to-run real-machine variance, not a regression). No optimization was attempted.
- **Security**: no filesystem/process/network/credential-storage capability was added anywhere — `ITargetTransport`/`LocalTargetTransport` only ever wrap the SAME already-existing local `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`. Both real-machine acceptance runs used only ServerSleuth's own existing, unmodified, read-only discovery entry point; the `--target bogus-remote-host` rejection test proves no network attempt is ever made for an unsupported target (the CLI fails during argument parsing, before `IDiscoveryEngine`/`ITargetTransport` is even resolved).
- **Project dependencies (skill.md §18)**: `ServerSleuth.Core` still has zero `PackageReference`s and zero project references (confirmed via `dotnet list reference`/`package`). `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`'s package set is unchanged — still exactly `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions` + `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Abstractions`, both `10.0.11`. No SSH/WinRM/PowerShell-remoting/networking-framework package was added anywhere in the solution.
- **Tests**: 42 new tests. `test/ServerSleuth.Core.Tests/Targets/ScanTargetTests.cs` (9): local/remote identity determinism, equality, no-random-identifier, no-credential-shaped-property (reflection), `DiscoveryContext` default target. `test/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests/Targets/LocalTargetTransportTests.cs` (4): DI registration, singleton-sharing with scanners, real-OS platform resolution, direct construction. `test/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests/Targets/NoArbitraryShellExecutionTests.cs` (4): `IProcessRunner`'s single structured method, `ProcessRequest`'s separated Executable/Arguments, `ITargetTransport`'s absence of any Execute/RunCommand-shaped member, `ITargetTransport`'s zero declared methods. `test/ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests/Architecture/TargetAgnosticismTests.cs` (3): no public method parameter/return type, no public property, and no assembly reference to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` anywhere in the compiled `ServerSleuth.Analysis` assembly mentions a target/transport/context type. `test/ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests/ScanCommandTargetOptionTests.cs` (11): `--target local` parses/behaves identically to the default, missing-value rejection, unsupported-target rejection (4 `[Theory]` cases) with message content, exit-code-equality between default and explicit-local, unsupported-target-never-reaches-discovery, verbose target line present/absent, help documents `--target`. No existing test was deleted or weakened.
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-24):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            56 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (47 pre-existing + 9 new)
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       436 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (433 pre-existing + 3 new)
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  70 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (62 pre-existing + 8 new)
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests:             68 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (57 pre-existing + 11 new, × 2 TFMs)
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:      146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1491/1491 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
  `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests` also independently re-run natively inside WSL2 Ubuntu: 68/68 passed there too, 0 failures.
- **Known Limitations**: `IPortInspector` remains outside `ITargetTransport` (documented above — no cross-platform default implementation exists to wrap; Windows/Linux each still register their own directly). `DiscoveryContext.Target` is currently inert data — no scanner reads it, and none is expected to until an actual remote transport exists to make that meaningful; it is threaded through purely so the CLI can display target identity and so a future phase has a concrete, already-wired place to read it from. `ScanTarget.Remote(...)` can be constructed but nothing in the codebase can act on a `Remote`-kind target — attempting to pass one to `ITargetTransport`/discovery would require a remote transport implementation that does not exist and was deliberately not built in this phase.
- **Phase 10D readiness**: the architectural seams needed for a future remote transport are now in place and structurally verified — `ScanTarget` (identity), `ITargetTransport` (the transport boundary a future SSH/WinRM implementation would satisfy), a confirmed-unnecessary-to-change `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` contract, a confirmed-target-agnostic Analysis layer, and a CLI that already cleanly rejects unsupported targets. No remote transport, credential storage, or remote scanning capability exists yet — that remains explicitly future work, not started.
- **Next phase**: not started. Per this phase's own strict-stop instruction, no SSH/WinRM/PowerShell Remoting/RDP/remote shell/remote command execution/remote file copy/credential storage/credential management UI/remote authentication/remote server scanning/GUI/Web UI/HTTP API/PDF/email/cloud upload/migration execution/automatic remediation was implemented. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase 10D-1 — Remote Transport Foundation

- **Objective**: establish the smallest secure abstraction for a future Linux→SSH / Windows→WinRM remote transport — target → transport → structured operation → result — WITHOUT implementing either transport, per this phase's own strict-stop instructions.
- **Inspection first (skill.md §2)**: re-read `ITargetTransport`/`LocalTargetTransport`/`ScanTarget`/`TargetKind`/`TargetPlatform`/`DiscoveryContext`/`IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`/`IPortInspector`/`CompositionRoot`/`ScanOptionsParser` (all Phase 10C). Confirmed `ITargetTransport` already exposes exactly two structured, non-shell operation surfaces — `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` — both already request/result-based with Executable/Arguments kept discrete. The one genuine gap: no closed vocabulary existed to CLASSIFY which category of operation a structured call represents (needed to later distinguish approved discovery operations from arbitrary execution), and `OperationStatus` had no values for transport-level failure modes (connection unreachable, protocol violation, tool not installed on the target) distinct from a local process/file failure.
- **`RemoteOperationKind` (`src/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Targets/RemoteOperationKind.cs`)**: `ProcessQuery`/`FileRead`/`DirectoryQuery` — deliberately sized to match exactly what `ITargetTransport` exposes today, NOT skill.md's own larger example list (`RegistryQuery`/`NetworkQuery`/`ServiceQuery` were considered and rejected — no cross-platform Infrastructure abstraction exists yet for Registry/WMI/COM-based Windows scanners to classify against; recorded as a Known Limitation, not silently dropped).
- **`RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationResult` (same folder)**: `RemoteOperation` preserves Target/Kind/Executable+Arguments (ProcessQuery only)/Path (FileRead|DirectoryQuery only)/Timeout as separate typed fields — never a raw command string — and gained `DescribeForLogging(ISecretRedactor)`, reusing the SAME `ISecretRedactor` (Phase 2/6E) every discovered configuration value already goes through, rather than inventing a second redaction mechanism. Cancellation is deliberately NOT a stored field (immutable records cannot meaningfully hold a `CancellationToken`) — it stays a parameter supplied at execution time, exactly matching `IProcessRunner.RunAsync`'s existing shape. `RemoteOperationResult` mirrors `ProcessResult`'s established shape and reuses `OperationStatus` rather than a second status enum.
- **`OperationStatus` extended** (`src/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Common/OperationStatus.cs`) with `NotInstalled`/`TransportUnavailable`/`ProtocolError` — additive, non-breaking (confirmed by the full regression: no exhaustive `switch` over the enum anywhere lacked a default/discard arm).
- **`RemoteTransportKind`/`RemoteTargetTransportFactory` (same folder)**: the Windows/Linux transport-selection seam skill.md §10 asks for, living in Infrastructure (never Analysis/Core — no `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()` was added to either). `ResolveTransportKind(TargetPlatform)` is pure mapping data (`Windows`→`WinRm`, `Linux`→`Ssh`, `Unknown`→throws rather than guessing). `Create(ScanTarget)` ALWAYS throws `NotSupportedException` for a `Remote`-kind target — no code path anywhere can construct a working remote `ITargetTransport`. Nothing in the codebase calls this factory yet (the CLI's own `--target` parsing already rejects remote before DI resolves `ITargetTransport` at all, unchanged since Phase 10C) — it exists purely as the obvious, already-tested plug-in point for Phase 10D-2+.
- **`ScanTarget.Remote(...)` gained validation** (skill.md §6): empty/whitespace/null host names now throw `ArgumentException` (data-shape validation only — no DNS resolution, no connection). Gained an optional `platform` parameter (default `Unknown`, inserted before the pre-existing `displayName`) — confirmed safe via a full-repo grep showing every existing call site only ever passed a single positional `hostName`.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §7) — deliberate non-decision**: no credential type/interface/provider was introduced. Nothing in this phase constructs, stores, or references a credential (`RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` throws before any authentication step could exist), so no provider boundary is yet "necessary" per the phase's own conditional instruction. Re-verified (reflection-based negative tests) that `RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationResult` carry no password/token/key/secret/credential-shaped property, extending Phase 10C's existing `ScanTarget` check.
- **Filesystem/Process/Windows/Linux mapping (skill.md §13-16) — documentation only, nothing implemented**: full mapping table (which `IFileSystemReader`/`IProcessRunner` member maps to which future SSH/WinRM operation, and which Windows/Linux scanner categories are/aren't representable by `RemoteOperationKind` today) is in `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s Phase 10D-1 addendum. Headline finding: Linux discovery's remote-transport requirement is, structurally, entirely satisfiable by the `RemoteOperationKind` vocabulary defined in this phase (every Linux scanner already goes through `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` exclusively); several Windows scanner categories (Registry, WMI-backed paths, COM, IIS, Task Scheduler, Certificates) are NOT yet representable, since they call Windows-only BCL/COM APIs directly today with no cross-platform Infrastructure abstraction to classify against.
- **No network connection (skill.md §20-21)**: verified structurally by `NoNetworkGuaranteeTests` — no Phase 10D-1 public member signature references `System.Net.Sockets`/`System.Net.Http`/`System.Net.WebSockets`/`System.Net.Security`, and `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.csproj` still has exactly the same 2 `PackageReference`s Phase 10A already used (no SSH.NET/Renci.SshNet/WinRM/PowerShell package of any kind). `RemoteTargetTransportFactoryTests.Create_NeverThrowsANetworkOrTimeoutRelatedException` asserts the exact, immediate `NotSupportedException` rather than merely "it did not hang."
- **Local transport regression (skill.md §9)**: `LocalTargetTransport`/`ITargetTransport`/`AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` were not touched at all — confirmed by the full regression (every pre-existing test, including Phase 10C's `LocalTargetTransportTests`, passes unmodified) and by both real-machine acceptance runs below reproducing Phase 10C's own local-target behavior.
- **Real Windows machine acceptance** (2026-08-25, same dev host, `dotnet build -c Release -f net8.0-windows` then `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch> --overwrite --verbose --target local`): **34,934 entities**, `Target: local (Windows)`, 4/12 scanners partial, Discovery 17.55s + Analysis 1.36s, Risk 317/17343/253, Migration 72 Blocked/30 NeedsRemediation. Confirmed real exit code (direct capture): **4 (PartialDiscovery)** — statistically indistinguishable from Phase 10C's own 34,939-entity run. Additionally verified `serversleuth.exe scan --target bogus-remote-host` still prints the same unsupported-target message and exits **2 (InvalidArguments)** — no discovery, no network contact. Scratch output deleted after inspection.
- **Real Linux machine acceptance** (2026-08-25, same reused WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 instance, built/run directly from the Windows-mounted path via PowerShell→`wsl.exe` rather than Git Bash, which mangles paths passed through to WSL): `dotnet build` succeeded natively (0 warnings/errors); `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests` re-run natively: **68/68 passed, both TFMs**. `serversleuth scan --output /tmp/... --overwrite --verbose --target local` — **1,654 entities**, `Target: local (Linux)`, 5/11 scanners partial (container/kubernetes correctly `NotInstalled`, neither installed nor forced to pass), Discovery 2.53s + Analysis 0.06s, Risk 21/10/19, Migration 21 Blocked/13 ReadyWithConditions. Confirmed real exit code via `$LASTEXITCODE` after a separate `--quiet` run: **4 (PartialDiscovery)** — small entity-count/partial-scanner drift vs. Phase 10C's 1,642/6-partial is normal environment variance (package updates on the shared WSL2 instance since Phase 10C), not a regression, since `LocalTargetTransport` itself was not touched. `serversleuth scan --target bogus-remote-host` exited **2**, same message. Scratch output (`/tmp/scan10d1`) removed after inspection; no remote machine was contacted anywhere in this validation.
- **Performance**: not modified, and not expected to be — every new Phase 10D-1 type is either pure unconsumed data (`RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationResult`) or a pure mapping/validation function (`RemoteTargetTransportFactory.ResolveTransportKind`, `ScanTarget.Remote`'s new validation) that no scanner or pipeline stage calls. Both real-machine runs above are statistically indistinguishable from their Phase 10C counterparts, confirming zero measurable overhead was introduced.
- **Security**: read-only contract unchanged — no filesystem/process/network/credential capability was added anywhere; `RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` always throws before anything resembling a connection attempt could occur. Both real-machine acceptance runs used only ServerSleuth's own existing, unmodified, read-only local discovery entry point; no service/registry/IIS/systemd/cron/Docker/Kubernetes was modified, no discovered application was executed, no discovered database was connected to, no report was uploaded anywhere.
- **Project dependencies (skill.md §21)**: unchanged. `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` still has exactly `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions` + `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.Abstractions` (both `10.0.11`) — confirmed by `NoNetworkGuaranteeTests.InfrastructureProject_AddedNoNewPackageDependency_ForPhase10D1` reading the `.csproj` directly. No SSH.NET/Renci.SshNet/WinRM/PowerShell-remoting/HTTP-client package was added anywhere in the solution.
- **Tests**: 36 new tests. `test/ServerSleuth.Core.Tests/Targets/ScanTargetTests.cs` (+5): empty/whitespace/null host rejection (3 `[Theory]` cases), explicit-platform acceptance, default-platform-stays-Unknown. `test/ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests/Targets/RemoteOperationTests.cs` (10): factory-method field preservation per classification, target preservation, timeout default/override, no-`CancellationToken`-property (reflection), secret-shaped-argument/path redaction, no-raw-command-API (reflection), no-credential-shaped-property (reflection). `RemoteOperationResultTests.cs` (11): every factory method's status mapping (`Ok`/3 `Failure` cases/`TransportUnavailableResult`/`ProtocolErrorResult`/`NotInstalledResult`/`TimedOutResult`/`CancelledResult`), all-distinct-statuses, no-credential-shaped-property. `RemoteTargetTransportFactoryTests.cs` (8): Windows→WinRm/Linux→Ssh mapping, determinism, Unknown-platform rejection, Linux/Windows remote-target rejection with correct message content, local-target rejection, never-a-network-exception. `NoNetworkGuaranteeTests.cs` (2): no networking-namespace reference in any Phase 10D-1 type's public signature, no new package dependency. No existing test was deleted or weakened.
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-25):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:            61 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (56 pre-existing + 5 new)
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:       436 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests: 101 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (70 pre-existing + 31 new)
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:     14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:          334 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests:             68 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (× 2 TFMs)
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:      146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:        299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1527/1527 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
  `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests` also independently re-run natively inside WSL2 Ubuntu: 68/68 passed there too (both TFMs), 0 failures.
- **Known Limitations**: `RemoteOperationKind` has no classification for Registry/WMI/COM/`ServiceController`-shaped operations — several Windows scanner categories (Registry, WMI-backed Service/Software paths, COM, IIS, Task Scheduler, Certificates) cannot be represented as a `RemoteOperation` yet; a future phase will need either a genuine cross-platform registry/WMI abstraction or a documented local-only exception for those categories. No credential provider boundary exists yet — deliberately deferred until an actual authentication mechanism is chosen. `IPortInspector` remains outside `ITargetTransport`, unchanged from Phase 10C.
- **Phase 10D-2 readiness**: `RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationResult`/`RemoteOperationKind`/`RemoteTransportKind` give a future SSH (Linux) transport implementation an already-defined, injection-safe operation/result shape to fill in — and Linux discovery's own operation surface (`IProcessRunner`+`IFileSystemReader` only) is already fully representable by that vocabulary, per the mapping review above. A future WinRM (Windows) transport has the same shape available but will additionally need the Registry/WMI/COM classification gap closed (Known Limitations) before most Windows scanner categories could run against it. `RemoteTargetTransportFactory` gives both a single, pre-tested plug-in point. No remote transport, credential storage, or remote scanning capability exists yet.
- **Next phase**: not started. Per this phase's own strict-stop instruction, no SSH/WinRM/PowerShell Remoting/RDP/remote shell/remote command execution/remote filesystem access/remote registry access/remote authentication/credential storage/credential management/actual remote scanning/GUI/Web UI/HTTP API/PDF/email/cloud upload/migration execution/automatic remediation was implemented. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase 10D-2 — Linux Remote Discovery over SSH

- **Objective**: implement the first real remote transport — Linux discovery over SSH — filling the `RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` seam Phase 10D-1 left always-throwing, ONLY for Linux/SSH, without redesigning `DiscoveryEngine`/Analysis/Risk/Migration/Reporting and without implementing Windows/WinRM.
- **Inspection first (skill.md §2)**: read `ITargetTransport`/`RemoteOperation*`/`OperationStatus`/`IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`, every Linux provider (`LinuxProcProvider`, `LinuxPortInspector`, `SystemctlProvider`, package providers, `LinuxRuntimeDiscoveryScanner`, `LinuxScheduledTaskScanner`, container/Kubernetes providers, `LinuxConfigurationScanner`, native-dependency providers), `AddServerSleuthLinux()`, `DiscoveryEngine`, `DiscoveryContext`, `CompositionRoot`, CLI target parsing. Two findings drove the whole design: (1) every Linux provider already depends ONLY on `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` — never a raw BCL/OS call — except `LinuxProcProvider.ResolveExeTarget`, which called `new FileInfo($"/proc/{pid}/exe").LinkTarget` directly on the local disk, a genuine interface gap (see below); (2) every provider/scanner is registered `AddSingleton`, and the SAME local `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` singleton is resolved once at process startup by `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure()` — meaning the real seam for "target-aware" behavior was always the COMPOSITION ROOT's registration of those two interfaces, not anything inside a scanner.
- **SSH library decision (skill.md §3)**: `SSH.NET` (`Renci.SshNet`) — a live event-driven host-key hook (`SshClient.HostKeyReceived`), structured `PrivateKeyAuthenticationMethod`/`PasswordAuthenticationMethod`, a per-command `CommandTimeout`, and a dedicated `SftpClient` for structured file access were all needed and none exist in the system `ssh`/`scp` executables without re-introducing a shell-command-construction risk this whole architecture exists to avoid. `dotnet add package SSH.NET --version 2024.2.0` (the then-latest) surfaced NU1903 (a High-severity advisory, GHSA-q939-rpr3-3284); removed and re-added at `2026.0.0` (current latest), which restored with zero advisories. MIT-licensed, no telemetry, `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` is the only project that references it (`ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis` unaffected).
- **`ISshSession` seam (skill.md §27)**: a thin interface (`Connect`/`ExecuteCommand`/`SftpExists`/`SftpReadBytes`/`SftpGetAttributes`/`ReadLinkTarget`/`SftpListDirectory`) between this codebase and Renci.SshNet's own (largely non-mockable, sealed-ish) `SshClient`/`SftpClient` — `SshNetSession` is the only real implementation; `FakeSshSession`/a minimal Linux.Tests-local double stand in for every deterministic test, so the entire security test suite runs with NO live SSH server.
- **Command construction (skill.md §8)**: `SshCommandLineBuilder.Build(executable, arguments)` is the ONE place, inside the transport only, where discrete Executable/Arguments become the single string the SSH "exec" channel protocol (RFC 4254 §6.5 — a protocol fact, not a design choice) requires — via POSIX single-quote escaping (`'` → `'\''`), applied independently to every token. Verified two ways: 23 `[Theory]` cases round-tripping every listed injection character class (space/quote/`;`/`|`/`&`/backtick/`$()`/newline/CRLF/redirection/`&&`/`||`/`#`/glob/`~`/Unicode/empty string) through an independent reference POSIX un-quoter written in the test itself; AND a real `bash -c` round-trip against the WSL2 Ubuntu host — see the transcript below. No `sh -c`/`bash -c`/`sudo sh -c` wrapper was ever constructed by this codebase (sshd applies its own server-side shell hand-off regardless, outside any client control).
- **Real shell injection verification transcript** (2026-08-25, WSL2 Ubuntu, via a throwaway scratchpad script, deleted after the run): reimplemented the exact same C# `Quote()` algorithm in bash, built commands via `eval "'echo' $(quote "$input")"` for 12 malicious inputs including `'dollar$(touch /tmp/PWNED)'` and `'double&&touch /tmp/PWNED3'`, asserted `echo`'s output exactly equaled the original (unmangled) input for every case, and asserted `/tmp/PWNED`/`/tmp/PWNED2`/`/tmp/PWNED3` were never created. Result: `INJECTION_DID_NOT_HAPPEN` — no case broke quoting, no injected command executed.
- **Host-key verification (skill.md §5)**: `IHostKeyVerifier.Verify(host, port, fingerprint) → Trusted|Rejected` — a closed two-value result, no "ask interactively" option. `TrustedFingerprintHostKeyVerifier` (the only implementation) holds an explicit host:port→fingerprint allow-list, empty by default, fails closed for anything not an exact (case-insensitive) match. `SshNetSession.Connect` computes the SHA-256 fingerprint of the OFFERED key inside `SshClient.HostKeyReceived` and asks the verifier on every single connection attempt; `e.CanTrust` is only ever assigned from that verdict. `NoBlindTrustImplementationExists_AnywhereInTheAssembly` reflects the whole `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` assembly for any `AcceptAny`/`TrustAll`/`AutoAccept`/`AlwaysTrust`-shaped member — none found.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §4)**: `RemoteCredential` (private key + optional passphrase, preferred; password, secondary) and `IRemoteCredentialProvider`/`StaticRemoteCredentialProvider` — added now because the chosen SSH implementation genuinely requires authentication material, the exact condition Phase 10D-1 left open. Never a property of `ScanTarget`/`DiscoveryContext`/any domain/report model. A real near-miss caught before shipping: a plain `sealed record`'s compiler-generated `ToString()` prints every field's raw value — `RemoteCredential.ToString()` is explicitly overridden to print only the username; verified by two dedicated tests. The CLI reads the private-key file and (if named) an environment-variable-held passphrase exactly once, from exactly the sources named on the command line (`--ssh-key <path>`, `--ssh-key-passphrase-env <VAR>` — never a passphrase as a direct argument).
- **`IFileSystemReader.ReadLinkTarget` — a disclosed interface-gap fix (skill.md §11)**: `LinuxProcProvider.ResolveExeTarget` previously called `FileInfo.LinkTarget` directly on the LOCAL disk, which would have silently resolved the wrong machine's `/proc` for any remote target. Fixed with one new, additive interface member, implemented identically (same behavior) in the existing local `FileSystemReader` and the new `SshFileSystemReader`, with `LinuxProcProvider` now routed through it. Every pre-existing `LinuxProcProviderTests` case was unaffected (none previously asserted `ExecutablePath`); two new tests plus one proving the SAME `LinuxProcProvider`, unmodified, works correctly against `SshFileSystemReader`.
- **A second disclosed gap**: Renci.SshNet 2026.0.0's public `SftpClient` (confirmed by reading its own shipped XML doc comments) has no SSH_FXP_READLINK operation — only the write-direction `SymbolicLink(path, linkPath)` exists. `SshNetSession.ReadLinkTarget` falls back to the single, well-known, read-only `readlink` utility over the same safely-quoted exec path as any other `ProcessQuery` — the same class of narrow exception already accepted for `ldconfig -p`/`systemctl show` elsewhere in this codebase.
- **Remote process/filesystem mapping (skill.md §9-10)**: `SshProcessRunner` — one exec channel per call, `ProcessResult.Ok(exitCode, ...)` for ANY exit code (Status=Success regardless, matching local `ProcessRunner`'s own existing semantics exactly), `TransportUnavailable`/`Timeout`/`Cancelled` mapped from the session. `SshFileSystemReader` — SFTP for everything but symlink-target reads; `EnumerateFiles`/`EnumerateDirectories` REJECT `recursive: true` outright (`OperationStatus.InvalidInput`), never walking a remote subtree.
- **Linux scanner integration required ZERO scanner/provider code changes (skill.md §21)**, beyond the disclosed `LinuxProcProvider` fix above — every Linux provider already depended only on `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`. Locked in by `ReadOnlyCommandVocabularyTests` (8 source-scan tests proving no mutating verb exists in any provider's source for `systemctl`/package managers/`docker`/`podman`/`kubectl`, plus confirming `LinuxScheduledTaskScanner` has no process dependency at all).
- **Target-aware composition (skill.md §20-22) — the one real architectural change**: `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure` gained one optional parameter (`ITargetTransport? remoteTransport`) — when supplied, `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` register as THAT transport's own instances instead of constructing the local ones. `CompositionRoot.Build(ScanOptions)` builds (never connects) an `SshRemoteTargetTransport` when `options.Remote` is set, and registers `AddServerSleuthLinux()` based on the TARGET's platform for a remote scan rather than the host OS — a remote scan from Windows never registers Windows scanners. The critical safety property (skill.md §22, "a remote target is never indistinguishable from local") is proven three ways: unit-level (`SshRemoteTargetTransportTests`), DI-level comparing a local-composed provider's singletons against a remote-composed one's (`RemoteCompositionTests.Build_RemoteTarget_NeverReusesTheLocalSingletons`), and transport-identity (`Build_RemoteTarget_RegistersTheSshTransport_NeverTheLocalOne`).
- **Connection timing (skill.md §6)**: `SshRemoteTargetTransport` is always constructed unconnected; `ScanCommand.RunAsync` calls `Connect(cancellationToken)` explicitly, immediately before discovery starts. A connect failure is caught there, surfaced as a concise message, exit code `GeneralFailure` — never a partial/garbled report, never a scanner run against a half-open connection.
- **CLI surface**: `scan --target <host> --ssh-user <u> --ssh-key <path> --ssh-host-fingerprint <sha256> [--ssh-port <n>] [--ssh-key-passphrase-env <VAR>]`. `--target local`/omitted unchanged. Any other `--target` value now means "remote SSH host" and requires all three of `--ssh-user`/`--ssh-key`/`--ssh-host-fingerprint` — omitting any one is rejected at parse time, before any file I/O or network activity, with a message naming exactly what's missing. A passphrase is only ever accepted via an environment-variable NAME, never a direct argument.
- **Real command-line verification against a real network stack** (2026-08-25, same dev host, Release build): `serversleuth.exe scan --target 127.0.0.1 --ssh-user nobody --ssh-key <malformed-file> ...` → key parsing fails cleanly before any connection attempt (`Error: The input is not a valid Base-64 string...`, exit code 1). With a REAL (freshly `ssh-keygen`-generated, throwaway, never authorized anywhere) ed25519 key and `--ssh-port 1` (guaranteed nothing listening): `Could not connect to remote target '127.0.0.1': <OS-level connection-refused message>`, exit code 1 — a genuine TCP-level connection attempt, correctly caught and classified as `TransportUnavailable`, cleanly surfaced with no crash/stack trace/hang.
- **Real live-server acceptance run: NOT performed, by explicit user decision.** skill.md §28 requires one genuine remote acceptance test against an explicitly-authorized host. The WSL2 Ubuntu instance used for local acceptance testing has no `sshd` installed; installing one requires `sudo`, which needs a password not available in this environment. Per §28's own "do not guess/discover credentials, do not scan arbitrary hosts" instruction, this was not worked around. The user was asked (2026-08-25) whether to (a) skip and document, (b) supply `sudo` access to install `sshd` on WSL2, or (c) point at a different authorized host — and explicitly chose (a): skip the live-login run, document the exact reason, and rely on the deterministic `ISshSession`-fake test suite (which skill.md §27 itself requires to be sufficient for the normal suite) plus the real-network-stack verification above. This is disclosed as a Known Limitation below, not fabricated.
- **Local Windows regression** (2026-08-25, same dev host, Release/net8.0-windows): `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch> --overwrite --verbose --target local` — **34,974 entities**, `Target: local (Windows)`, 4/12 partial, exit code **4** — statistically indistinguishable from Phase 10D-1's 34,934. `--target bogus-remote-host` (no `--ssh-*` options) now fails with `Remote target 'bogus-remote-host' requires '--ssh-user <username>'.`, exit code **2** — still a clean, immediate rejection, just a more specific reason than Phase 10C/10D-1's generic "not yet implemented".
- **Local Linux regression** (2026-08-25, same reused WSL2 Ubuntu instance, built/run natively via PowerShell→`wsl.exe` — Git Bash mangles paths passed through to WSL, confirmed again this phase): `dotnet build` succeeded natively (0 warnings/errors); `serversleuth scan --output /tmp/... --overwrite --verbose --target local` — **1,658 entities**, `Target: local (Linux)`, 5/11 partial, Discovery 2.38s + Analysis 0.07s, exit code **4** — statistically indistinguishable from Phase 10D-1's 1,654 (and `linux-process-scanner` — which now runs the NEW `ReadLinkTarget`-based `/proc/<pid>/exe` resolution — reported 48 entities without incident, real confirmation the refactor works on a genuine Linux `/proc`, not just fakes). `--target bogus-remote-host` exits **2** with the same clearer message.
- **Performance**: not modified for local scanning (both real-machine runs above are statistically indistinguishable from Phase 10D-1's own). Remote scanning was designed to avoid the N+1-connection anti-pattern (skill.md §31) — one `SshRemoteTargetTransport`/`ISshSession` is constructed and connected once per scan, reused for every `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` call any Linux scanner makes — but no timing measurement against a real remote host could be taken (see the live-acceptance limitation above).
- **Security**: read-only contract unchanged for local scanning. For remote scanning: no mutating command exists in any Linux provider's source (locked in by `ReadOnlyCommandVocabularyTests`); no credential is ever logged, persisted, or exposed in an exception message (`RemoteCredential.ToString()` override, verified); host keys are rejected by default; command construction is injection-resistant (verified both syntactically and against a real shell); a remote scan never falls back to scanning the local machine (verified at both the unit and DI-composition level).
- **Project dependencies (skill.md §30)**: `dotnet list package`/`dotnet list reference` confirm exactly one new package anywhere in the solution — `SSH.NET` 2026.0.0 (+ transitive `BouncyCastle.Cryptography` 2.7.0) in `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` only. `ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis` gained no new package or project reference — confirmed unchanged. No WinRM/PowerShell-Remoting/HTTP-client package anywhere.
- **Tests**: 79 new tests. `SshCommandLineBuilderTests` (23, Infrastructure — injection resistance). `TrustedFingerprintHostKeyVerifierTests` (7). `RemoteCredentialTests` (5, no-secret-logging). `SshProcessRunnerTests` (6). `SshFileSystemReaderTests` (7). `SshRemoteTargetTransportTests` (6, includes the critical local-vs-remote safety proof). `NoNetworkGuaranteeTests` updated in place (net unchanged count) to allow-list exactly the SSH.NET package. `LinuxProcProviderTests` (+3, Linux — new `ReadLinkTarget`-based resolution, including against the real `SshFileSystemReader`). `ReadOnlyCommandVocabularyTests` (8, Linux — mutating-verb absence). `ScanCommandTargetOptionTests` (extended, Cli — new `--ssh-*` parsing/validation). `RemoteCompositionTests` (3, Cli — target-aware DI wiring, critical local-vs-remote safety proof at the composition level). No existing test was deleted or weakened.
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-25):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:             61 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:        436 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  155 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (101 pre-existing + 54 new)
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:      14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:           345 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (334 pre-existing + 11 new)
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests:              75 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (× 2 TFMs)
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:       146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:         299 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  Total: 1606/1606 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
  `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests`/`ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests`/`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests` also independently re-run natively inside WSL2 Ubuntu: 75/75 (both TFMs), 345/345, 155/155 — identical counts to the Windows run, 0 failures. A whole-solution `dotnet test` was ALSO attempted natively on WSL2 and surfaced two categories of PRE-EXISTING, unrelated-to-this-phase failures never touched by Phase 10D-2: 23 `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests` failures (real `System.ServiceProcess.ServiceController`/Windows-only API calls throwing `PlatformNotSupportedException` on Linux — these tests were never validated to run on Linux by any prior phase, confirmed by re-checking Phase 10B/10C's own real-Linux-acceptance notes, which only ever re-ran `Cli.Tests` natively, never the full solution) and 2 `ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests` fixture-count assertion failures (unrelated ERP synthetic-fixture entity counts, in a project this phase never touched, that pass cleanly on the same code on Windows). Documented here, exactly like Phase 10B's own WSL `$?`-propagation artifact, so a future session does not misattribute either to this phase.
- **Known Limitations**: no live-server login-and-discover acceptance run was performed (environment-blocked — no explicitly-authorized reachable host with a running `sshd` and available credentials; user explicitly chose to skip rather than have it worked around or fabricated). `RemoteOperationKind` still has no Registry/WMI/COM classification — Windows/WinRM remains fully unimplemented, unchanged from Phase 10D-1. `SshNetSession.ReadLinkTarget` depends on a `readlink` binary being on the remote `PATH` (true for essentially every real Linux distribution, but not mechanically guaranteed the way a native SFTP operation would be). Cancellation/timeout tear-down of a REAL `SshCommand` could not be verified end-to-end against a live server (same credential-availability reason) — the implementation follows Renci.SshNet's documented shape but is unverified beyond that. Two pre-existing, unrelated test failures surfaced when running the full solution natively on WSL2 for the first time (see Full Regression above) — not fixed, since fixing them is outside this phase's own scope (Windows-only integration tests, and a Reporting-layer fixture this phase never touched).
- **Phase 10D-3 readiness**: Linux remote discovery is architecturally complete and exercised by 79 new deterministic tests plus real command-line/network-stack verification; only a live-server login-and-discover run remains outstanding, blocked purely on host/credential availability, not on anything this phase left unbuilt. A future phase could: (a) obtain an explicitly-authorized SSH-reachable Linux host and run the full genuine acceptance scan skill.md §28 describes; (b) begin Windows/WinRM remote discovery, which will need the Registry/WMI/COM classification gap closed first; (c) fix the two pre-existing WSL2-native test failures surfaced above (as their own, separately-scoped fix, per this project's established pattern of not silently absorbing unrelated fixes into an unrelated phase).
- **Next phase**: not started. Per this phase's own strict-stop instruction, no Windows WinRM/PowerShell Remoting/RDP/Windows Remote Registry/Remote WMI/GUI/Web UI/HTTP API/PDF/email/cloud upload/migration execution/automatic remediation was implemented, and the Analysis architecture was not modified. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase 10D-3A — Windows Remote Capability Model

- **Objective**: prepare the architecture for future Windows/WinRM remote scanning WITHOUT implementing WinRM/PowerShell Remoting/any network transport — a structured, typed capability model only, per this phase's own strict scope (do not touch Phase 10D-2's Linux/SSH code, `DiscoveryEngine`, Analysis, Risk, Migration, or Reporting).
- **Inspection first (skill.md §2)**: read `RemoteOperation`/`RemoteOperationKind`/`RemoteOperationResult`/`OperationStatus`/`ScanTarget`/`DiscoveryContext` (Core/Infrastructure), `ITargetTransport`/`LocalTargetTransport`/`SshRemoteTargetTransport`/`RemoteTargetTransportFactory`/`RemoteTransportKind`/`SshProcessRunner`/`SshFileSystemReader`/`RemoteCredential`/`TrustedFingerprintHostKeyVerifier` (Infrastructure), and every Windows scanner/provider (`IWindowsRegistryReader`, `IProcessWmiProvider`/`ProcessWmiProvider`, `WindowsPortInspector`/`INetworkTableProvider`, `WindowsServiceScanner`/`IServiceEnumerator`/`ServiceRegistryDetailReader`, `WindowsInstalledSoftwareScanner`, `WindowsProcessScanner`/`IProcessEnumerator`, `WindowsComScanner`/`ComClsidReader`/`ComRegistrationSource`, `IIisConfigurationProvider`/`IisScanner`, `ITaskSchedulerProvider`/`WindowsScheduledTaskScanner`, `ICertificateStoreProvider`/`WindowsCertificateScanner`, `WindowsConfigurationScanner`, `WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner`, `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner`'s detectors). Two findings drove the whole design: (1) Registry/Service-detail/COM registration are ALREADY registry-only through `IWindowsRegistryReader`; runtime detectors and the binary/configuration scanners ALREADY depend only on the existing cross-platform `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` — so Phase 10D-1's `RemoteOperationKind` already covers them, unchanged; (2) `WindowsPortInspector` is already fully WMI-based. This left exactly five genuinely new capability areas: Registry (already-abstracted, needs a request-shape upgrade only), WMI, IIS, Task Scheduler, Certificates.
- **Vocabulary sizing (skill.md §5)**: `WindowsRemoteOperationKind` has 5 members, not the 7 skill.md offered as a starting menu. `ServiceQuery` folded into `WmiQuery` (`IServiceEnumerator` uses `ServiceController.GetServices()` locally, but the same data is fully representable as a `Win32_Service` WMI query — no dedicated vocabulary needed). `ComRegistryQuery` folded into `RegistryQuery` (`ComClsidReader` already reads COM registration entirely through `IWindowsRegistryReader` today).
