Both decisions documented in ARCHITECTURE.md's Phase 10D-3A addendum. Deliberately a SEPARATE enum from Phase 10D-1/10D-2's cross-platform `RemoteOperationKind` — Registry/WMI/IIS/TaskScheduler/Certificate are Windows-domain concepts with no generic ProcessQuery/FileRead/DirectoryQuery shape, so every new type lives in `ServerSleuth.Windows`, never `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`, keeping Phase 10D-2's SSH code completely untouched.
- **Registry capability (skill.md §6)**: `WindowsRegistryQuery` collapses `IWindowsRegistryReader`'s three existing methods (`GetSubKeyNames`/`GetValues`/`GetValue`) into one request shape (`IncludeSubKeys`/`IncludeValues`/`ValueNames`), reusing the existing `Microsoft.Win32.RegistryHive`/`RegistryView` BCL enums. Structurally incapable of expressing a write/delete/rename/ACL-change — no such field exists; `WindowsRegistryQuery_HasNoWriteDeleteRenameOrAclMember` locks this in.
- **WMI capability (skill.md §7)**: `WindowsWmiQuery` (`Namespace`/`ClassName`/`Properties`/`Filters`) represents exactly the shape `ProcessWmiProvider`/`NetworkTableProvider` already build by hand as WQL strings today — never assembled into WQL by this phase (a future transport's job, mirroring `SshCommandLineBuilder`'s role). `WmiFilterClause.Operator` is a closed 2-value enum (`Equals`/`NotEquals`), sized to exactly the one filter (`State = 2`) any current caller needs. **Disclosed gap**: `ProcessWmiProvider.TryGetOwner`'s `InvokeMethod("GetOwner", null)` call cannot be represented — the model has no WMI-method-invocation capability at all (a hard security requirement), and no narrow allow-list was invented to route around it, unlike Phase 10D-2's `readlink` precedent (which had an already-permitted `ProcessQuery` exec fallback to piggyback on; WMI method invocation has none). `ProcessWmiInfo.OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` therefore has no remote-capability equivalent — documented, not hacked around.
- **IIS/Task-Scheduler/Certificate capabilities (skill.md §9-11)**: each new interface (`IWindowsRemoteIisOperations`/`IWindowsRemoteTaskSchedulerOperations`/`IWindowsRemoteCertificateOperations`) reuses the EXISTING local DTO directly (`IisSnapshot`, `ScheduledTaskRow`, `CertificateStoreSource`/`CertificateRow`) rather than duplicating it — all three read-only-by-shape, no `Start`/`Stop`/`Recycle`/`Run`/`Delete`/`Register`/`Export` method anywhere. `CertificateRow.HasPrivateKey` remains a boolean flag only; no key-material-bearing member exists anywhere in the model.
- **A second, smaller disclosed gap**: `IProcessEnumerator`/`ProcessEnumerator` still hard-codes local `System.Diagnostics.Process.GetProcesses()` — the Windows analogue of the `LinuxProcProvider` gap Phase 10D-2 fixed. NOT refactored here (representable in principle by a future `Win32_Process` `WindowsWmiQuery`, but nothing consumes it remotely yet) — left for whichever future phase actually wires a transport, mirroring Phase 10D-2's own precedent.
- **Capability composition (skill.md §14)**: `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` bundles `Target`+5 narrow interfaces (`Registry`/`Wmi`/`Iis`/`TaskScheduler`/`Certificates`), mirroring `ITargetTransport`'s own Target/ProcessRunner/FileSystemReader bundle. Each of the five sub-interfaces mirrors one of the five EXISTING local interfaces (`IWindowsRegistryReader`/`IProcessWmiProvider`/`IIisConfigurationProvider`/`ITaskSchedulerProvider`/`ICertificateStoreProvider`) 1:1.
- **`WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.Create` always throws** — the same seam shape `RemoteTargetTransportFactory.Create` had before Phase 10D-2 filled it for SSH. No implementation of `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` exists in the production assembly — only a test-only `FakeWindowsRemoteCapabilities` recording double, mechanically verified by `NoConcreteImplementationOfIWindowsRemoteCapabilities_ExistsInTheProductionAssembly`.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §16)**: unchanged, deliberately not extended — nothing in the capability model needs to authenticate anything (pure request/result data). A future WinRM implementation will need its own injected credential provider, left as an explicit open boundary.
- **Security tests (skill.md §19)**: 36 new tests across `WindowsRemoteCapabilityModelTests`/`WindowsRemoteCapabilityArchitectureTests` covering all 16 numbered requirements — registry/WMI read-only shape, no `Execute`/`RunScript`/`PowerShell`/`Shell`/`Invoke` member, no registry/WMI/service/task/IIS mutation, no certificate private-key export, no COM activation, no credential property on `ScanTarget`/results/queries, no concrete production implementation, pure-data construction (no network call), no credential-looking text in JSON-serialized queries. All run against `FakeWindowsRemoteCapabilities` — zero live Windows remote host required.
- **Architecture tests (skill.md §20)**: capability-model types reference no Reporting/Analysis/GUI namespace; `ServerSleuth.Windows.csproj` gained no new `PackageReference` (still its pre-existing 5) and no project reference to Reporting/Analysis; `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.csproj` still has exactly Phase 10D-2's 3 packages (SSH.NET unchanged); the Infrastructure assembly defines no `WindowsRemote*` type at all.
- **Existing scanner compatibility (skill.md §21)**: zero scanners modified — every Windows scanner/provider file is byte-for-byte unchanged from Phase 10D-2.
- **Dependency audit (skill.md §24)**: `dotnet list package`-equivalent (direct `.csproj` inspection) confirms `ServerSleuth.Windows` still has exactly its 5 pre-existing packages (`Microsoft.CSharp`, `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions`, `System.Management`, `System.Reflection.Metadata`, `System.ServiceProcess.ServiceController`) and `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` still has exactly 3 (including `SSH.NET`, unchanged from Phase 10D-2) — zero new packages anywhere, no WinRM/PowerShell-Remoting/network-transport package introduced.
- **Local regression** (2026-08-25, same dev host): `dotnet build` — 0 warnings/errors across the whole solution. `dotnet test` (whole solution): **1642/1642 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** — the pre-existing 1606-test baseline (`ServerSleuth.Core.Tests` 61, `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests` 436, `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests` 155, `ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests` 14, `ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests` 345, `ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests` 75×2 TFMs, `ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests` 146) plus `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests` growing from 299→335 (the 36 new tests), all unchanged elsewhere. No Windows-local, Linux-local, or Linux/SSH real-machine scan was re-run in this phase specifically — none of that code path was touched, so no regression risk exists to re-verify beyond the full automated suite passing.
- **Real-machine testing (skill.md §23)**: NOT performed for remote Windows scanning, by this phase's own explicit prohibition — no WinRM transport exists anywhere to test against, and none was fabricated.
- **Known Limitations**: `WindowsWmiQuery` cannot represent WMI method invocation of any kind — `ProcessWmiInfo.OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` has no remote-capability equivalent in this model (see WMI capability above). `IProcessEnumerator`/`ProcessEnumerator` still bypasses every injectable abstraction (see the second disclosed gap above). No live WinRM/remote-Windows testing exists — `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.Create` still always throws for every target.
- **Phase 10D-3B readiness**: the capability model is architecturally complete and exercised by 36 deterministic tests. A future phase implementing real WinRM would need to: (a) choose a WinRM/WS-Man client library (following the Phase 10D-2 `SSH.NET`-selection template: inspect existing deps, evaluate a maintained library over hand-rolled protocol code, document the choice), (b) design the injectable credential boundary this phase deliberately left open, (c) implement `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` against that library without changing the five interfaces this phase defined, (d) decide whether to accept the two disclosed gaps (WMI `GetOwner`, `ProcessEnumerator`) permanently or close them, (e) wire `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory`/`CompositionRoot`/CLI `--target`/`--winrm-*` options the same way Phase 10D-2 wired the SSH equivalents, and (f) obtain an explicitly-authorized Windows host to run the genuine remote acceptance test skill.md's Linux-equivalent §28 describes.
- **Next phase**: not started. Per this phase's own strict-stop instruction, no WinRM/PowerShell Remoting/WS-Man network call/Windows Remote Registry transport/remote WMI transport/SSH-for-Windows/RDP/RPC transport/GUI/Web UI/HTTP API/PDF/email/cloud upload/migration execution/automatic remediation was implemented, and no real Windows remote host was connected to. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase 10D-3B — Windows Remote / WinRM Transport

- **Objective**: implement the first REAL Windows remote transport — WinRM/WS-Man — filling Phase 10D-3A's always-throwing `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.Create` seam, without PowerShell/arbitrary-command execution as the capability surface, and without redesigning Discovery/Analysis/Risk/Migration/Reporting.
- **Package decision (skill.md §3, §30)**: `Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure` 3.0.0 — the native CIM/WS-Man client library PowerShell's own CIM cmdlets use. Maintained (Microsoft/PowerShellTeam, 33M+ downloads), MIT-licensed, no telemetry, no `System.Management.Automation` dependency, no vulnerability advisory. Exposes `WSManSessionOptions` (a genuine WS-Man session, not DCOM), typed `CimInstance`/`CimMethodResult` objects, explicit TLS flags, per-operation timeout/cancellation. Exactly one new package — `ServerSleuth.Windows.csproj` 5→6 `PackageReference`s. No new package anywhere else in the solution.
- **Architecture**: `ScanTarget.Remote(host, Windows)` → `WindowsRemoteCapabilityFactory.CreateWinRm(target, WinRmConnectionOptions, credentialProvider)` → `WinRmWindowsRemoteCapabilities : IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` (fills the Phase 10D-3A seam) → `CimWinRmTransport`/`ICimSession`/`CimNetSession` (one shared session per target) → `WinRmWindowsProviderSet` (wraps the capabilities as the SAME nine local interfaces every Windows scanner already depends on — `IWindowsRegistryReader`/`IProcessWmiProvider`/`IProcessEnumerator`/`INetworkTableProvider`/`IProcessNameResolver`/`IServiceEnumerator`/`IIisConfigurationProvider`/`ITaskSchedulerProvider`/`ICertificateStoreProvider`) → `WindowsRemoteTargetTransport : ITargetTransport` (registered by `CompositionRoot`/`AddServerSleuthWindowsRemote`).
- **Registry (skill.md §9)**: `WinRmRegistryOperations` maps `WindowsRegistryQuery` onto `StdRegProv` (root\default)'s `EnumKey`/`EnumValues`/`Get*Value` READ methods only — via `WindowsWmiMethodAllowList`, a hard-coded closed allow-list re-checked independently at the transport level. `Registry32` is rejected outright (`Unsupported`) — `StdRegProv` has no WOW6432Node view parameter, disclosed rather than silently answered with the 64-bit view.
- **WMI (skill.md §10)**: `WinRmWmiOperations` builds a `SELECT...FROM...WHERE...` WQL string from the already-structured `WindowsWmiQuery` (Phase 10D-3A), single-quote-escaping every filter value — verified against an actual injection-shaped payload. `WindowsWmiMethodAllowList.QueryableClasses` restricts queries to exactly `Win32_Process`/`Win32_Service`/`MSFT_NetTCPConnection`/`MSFT_NetUDPEndpoint`.
- **The `Win32_Process.GetOwner()` gap is resolved deliberately**: the allow-list DOES include it (proving structural representability), but `WinRmProcessWmiProvider.GetAll()` never calls it — doing so per-process would be an N+1 remote call per scan. `OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` are permanently `null` remotely, documented not silently dropped.
- **`IProcessEnumerator` gap resolved via option B**: `WinRmProcessEnumerator` routes through `Win32_Process` (namespace `root\cimv2`) instead of local `Process.GetProcesses()`; parses CIM datetime via the pre-existing `System.Management.ManagementDateTimeConverter`.
- **IIS/Task Scheduler/Certificates are DISCLOSED gaps, not hacked around**: no verified WS-Man-reachable schema for IIS's optional `root\WebAdministration` provider (no real host available to validate against), no WMI provider for Task Scheduler 2.0 at all, certificate stores are local-only with no safe remote registry-BLOB-parsing alternative attempted. All three always return `NotInstalled`/`Failed` with a diagnostic — never fabricated data, never PowerShell/appcmd/schtasks.exe.
- **Service/COM/Port required zero new capability types** — both Phase 10D-3A folding decisions (`ServiceQuery`→`WmiQuery`, `ComRegistryQuery`→`RegistryQuery`) held under real implementation. `WinRmServiceEnumerator` queries `Win32_Service`, mapping its space-containing display strings to `ServiceController`-style PascalCase tokens (disclosed best-effort). `WindowsComScanner` needed zero code change.
- **Remote configuration/filesystem/process (skill.md §19) — the most consequential decision**: no WS-Man-reachable non-PowerShell filesystem/process bridge exists for Windows. `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner`/`WindowsConfigurationScanner`/`WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner` (need `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader`) and `WindowsOsScanner` (reads local `Environment.*` with no bridge) are EXCLUDED outright from `AddServerSleuthWindowsRemote`'s scanner set — proven never registered by a dedicated test. `WindowsComScanner`/`WindowsScheduledTaskScanner`/`IisScanner` still need `IFileSystemReader` for OPTIONAL path-verification (a pre-existing degraded mode) — satisfied by `UnavailableRemoteFileSystemReader`, which never calls `System.IO`.
- **Target-aware composition (skill.md §20)**: `CompositionRoot.Build` branches on `options.WindowsRemote is not null` before the pre-existing SSH/local branch; the whole branch compiles only under `SERVERSLEUTH_WINDOWS` — a Windows/WinRM scan can only be initiated from a Windows-hosted `serversleuth` binary (a genuine, disclosed platform-of-origin limitation; the plain `net8.0` build throws a clear `NotSupportedException`).
- **No local fallback (skill.md §21, §25 items 1-6/25) — mechanically verified**: `Build_RemoteWindowsTarget_NeverResolvesAnyLocalWindowsInterfaceImplementation` asserts all nine interfaces resolve to their `WinRm*` type, never the local one; `Build_RemoteWindowsTarget_NeverReusesTheLocalSingletons` compares `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` against a genuinely local composition's singletons; `ExactlyOneConcreteImplementationOfIWindowsRemoteCapabilities...` confirms `WinRmWindowsRemoteCapabilities` is the only production `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` implementation anywhere in the assembly.
- **TLS/server-certificate validation (skill.md §7) fails closed, no injectable bypass anywhere**: `UseSsl` defaults `true`; whenever true, `CertCACheck`/`CertCNCheck`/`CertRevocationCheck` are hard-coded `true` in `CimNetSession.Connect` — no field/flag/code path can set any to `false`. `NoEncryption` is hard-coded `false` unconditionally. Verified by reflecting the WHOLE assembly (public+non-public) for any `TrustAll`/`AcceptAny`/`SkipCertificateValidation`-shaped member — none found, mirroring Phase 10D-2's SSH host-key precedent.
- **Credential boundary (skill.md §5-6)**: `WindowsRemoteCredential` (`SecureString` password, `ToString()` explicitly overridden — the same near-miss class Phase 10D-2 caught for SSH), `IWindowsRemoteCredentialProvider`/`StaticWindowsRemoteCredentialProvider`. Three auth mechanisms — `Negotiate`(default)/`Kerberos`/`CredSsp`; `Basic`/`Digest` deliberately unsupported. CLI: `--target <host> --winrm-user <u> --winrm-password-env <VAR> [--winrm-domain] [--winrm-port] [--winrm-no-ssl] [--winrm-auth]` — password only from a named env var, never a direct argument; using both `--ssh-user` and `--winrm-user` is rejected as ambiguous.
- **A genuine real-network-stack finding, found and fixed mid-implementation**: `CimSession.Create` alone does NOT eagerly connect (WS-Man sessions are lazily connected on first real operation, unlike SSH.NET) — an initial version returned "success" from `Connect()` even against a guaranteed-closed port, with the real failure only surfacing later as scanner-level `PartiallySupported` (0 entities, 4/8 partial). Fixed by calling `CimSession.TestConnection(out _, out exception)` immediately after `Create()`. **Re-verified against the real closed port after the fix** (2026-08-25, same dev host): `serversleuth.exe scan --target 127.0.0.1 --winrm-user tester --winrm-password-env <VAR> --winrm-port 1` → immediate genuine WS-Man "client cannot connect to the specified destination... WS-Management" error, exit code 1 (GeneralFailure) — no crash, no hang. A missing `--winrm-password-env` variable was also verified to fail cleanly (exit code 1) before any connection attempt.
- **Real Windows remote acceptance: NOT performed against a genuine authorized live host — no explicitly-authorized WinRM-reachable Windows host with credentials was available in this environment**, the same class of environment constraint Phase 10D-2 hit for its own SSH acceptance run. What WAS verified against a real network stack: the missing-env-var rejection and the closed-port `TransportUnavailable` classification above (which is also what surfaced and led to fixing the eager-connect gap). No credentials guessed/discovered, no arbitrary host scanned.
- **Local Windows regression** (2026-08-25, same dev host, Debug build): `serversleuth.exe scan --output <scratch> --overwrite --verbose` — **34,942 entities**, `Target: local (Windows)`, 4/12 partial, exit code **4** — statistically indistinguishable from the pre-existing baseline; confirms zero local-scanner behavior change (no local Windows file was modified this phase). Linux local/SSH were not independently re-run — no Linux-domain or `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure/Remote` file was touched at all this phase.
- **Tests**: 31 new (38 additional executions in the regression total below, since ScanCommandWindowsRemoteOptionTests's 7 tests run under both Cli.Tests TFMs). `WinRmTransportSecurityTests` (18, Windows.Tests — registry/WMI read-only+allow-list enforcement, no-PowerShell/shell member, no-cert-bypass, credential no-logging, disclosed-gap honesty). `WindowsRemoteCompositionTests` (6, Cli.Tests, `SERVERSLEUTH_WINDOWS`-only — no-local-fallback proofs at the DI level). `ScanCommandWindowsRemoteOptionTests` (7, Cli.Tests — `--winrm-*` CLI parsing/validation/ambiguity). `FakeCimSession` (Windows.Tests/Fakes — deterministic double, zero live host required). Two Phase 10D-3A tests updated in place for the new reality (package count 5→6; exactly one, WinRM-backed `IWindowsRemoteCapabilities` implementation now exists where none did before).
- **Full regression** (actually executed 2026-08-25):
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:             61 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:      14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:           345 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:  155 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0):     82 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (75 pre-existing + 7 new)
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0-win): 88 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (75 pre-existing + 13 new)
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:       146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:        436 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:         353 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped   (335 pre-existing + 18 new)
  Total: 1680/1680 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
  ```
- **Dependency audit (skill.md §30)**: `dotnet list package --include-transitive` on `ServerSleuth.Windows` confirms exactly one new direct package (`Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure` 3.0.0, transitively pulling `Runtime.Win`/`Runtime.Unix`, neither separately referenced); `ServerSleuth.Core`/`ServerSleuth.Analysis`/`ServerSleuth.Reporting`/`ServerSleuth.Infrastructure` package sets confirmed unchanged. No PowerShell/GUI/cloud/telemetry dependency anywhere.
- **Known Limitations**: `Win32_Process.GetOwner()` never invoked from the bulk path (N+1 avoidance) — `OwnerDomain`/`OwnerUser` permanently unavailable remotely. `Registry32` unsupported remotely (`StdRegProv` has no WOW6432Node equivalent). IIS/Task Scheduler/Certificate remote discovery fully unimplemented (always `NotInstalled`/`Failed`). `RuntimeDiscoveryScanner`/`WindowsConfigurationScanner`/`WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner`/`WindowsOsScanner` never run for a remote Windows target. Windows/WinRM scanning only available from a Windows-hosted `serversleuth` binary. `WinRmServiceEnumerator`'s state/type string mapping is a disclosed best-effort. No genuine live-server acceptance run performed (environment-blocked). Per-query `CancellationToken` not threaded through the Phase-10D-3A-fixed `IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations`/`IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations` signatures — the same coarse-grained limit the pre-existing local interfaces already have.
- **Phase 10E readiness**: Windows remote discovery over WinRM is functionally real end-to-end (Registry/WMI/Process/Port/Service/COM) and exercised by 31 new deterministic tests plus genuine real-network-stack verification (including a real bug found and fixed via that verification); only a live-server login-and-discover run remains outstanding, blocked purely on host/credential availability. A future phase could: (a) obtain an explicitly-authorized WinRM host and run the full genuine acceptance scan; (b) investigate `root\WebAdministration` against a real IIS+provider host; (c) design a justified allow-list for remote-owner resolution if the N+1 cost is ever judged acceptable; (d) thread per-query cancellation through the Registry/WMI interfaces (and their local counterparts).
- **Next phase**: not started. Per this phase's own strict scope, no GUI/Web UI/HTTP API/PDF/email/cloud upload/migration execution/automatic remediation/RDP/PowerShell-Remoting-as-a-command-channel/SSH-for-Windows was implemented. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase 10E-1 — Remote End-to-End Pipeline Integration

- **Objective**: prove (and only where a genuine gap existed, complete) the integration of the already-real Linux/SSH and Windows/WinRM remote transports with the complete, single, pre-existing pipeline — no second pipeline, no new production architecture unless a concrete integration defect required it.
- **Audit finding**: the integration was ALREADY complete. `ScanCommand`/`ScanPipelineRunner` (Phase 10A) have never branched on target beyond the one-time `Connect()` dispatch; every downstream stage (Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation/Risk/Aggregation/Migration/Reporting/Export) is invoked identically regardless of `ScanTarget.Kind`. `RemoteCompositionTests`/`WindowsRemoteCompositionTests` (10D-2/10D-3B) already proved transport selection at the DI level. **No production code needed to change.**
- **The one genuine gap**: the CLI test harness (`CliTestRunner`/`TestServiceProviderFactory`) could not inject a remote `ITargetTransport` — always built local composition regardless of `--target` args, so no existing test could observe a remote target flowing through the real pipeline. Fixed additively with an optional `ITargetTransport?` parameter on both (default unchanged, every pre-existing call site unaffected), threaded through the SAME `AddServerSleuthInfrastructure(transport)` overload Phase 10D-2 already built.
- **`FakeRemoteTargetTransport`** (test-only): carries a `Remote`-kind `ScanTarget` + inert `IProcessRunner`/`IFileSystemReader` doubles that throw if invoked. Deliberately not a real `SshRemoteTargetTransport`/`WindowsRemoteTargetTransport` — skips `ScanCommand`'s `Connect()` dispatch, so real connect semantics (already exhaustively covered by 10D-2/10D-3B) are not re-tested here.
- **`RemotePipelineIntegrationTests`** (12 tests): feeds the same 17-entity ERP fixture every prior end-to-end test uses through the real CLI with `Remote` Linux and `Remote` Windows targets, plus disclosed-gap scanner statuses matching 10D-2's/10D-3B's own documented gaps (`linux-container-scanner`/`linux-kubernetes-scanner`; `windows-iis-scanner`/`windows-scheduled-task-scanner`/`windows-certificate-scanner`, all `NotInstalled`) — proves identical migration/risk numbers to a local scan, correct `Target: remote:<host> (<Platform>)` identity, and honest disclosed-gap surfacing (never dropped, never faked success).
- **No separate pipeline (skill.md §3)**: `NoSeparateRemotePipelineType_ExistsAnywhereInTheLoadedAssemblies` reflects over Core/Analysis/Cli assemblies for any `Remote*Engine`/`RemoteScanPipeline`-shaped type — none exist.
- **Analysis platform-agnostic (skill.md §5)**: `AnalysisAssembly_ReferencesNoPlatformOrTransportSpecificType` confirms no `ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Linux`/`Renci.SshNet`/`Microsoft.Management.Infrastructure` reference in the Analysis assembly.
- **Failure semantics (skill.md §9)**: `AccessDenied`/`Failed`/`PartiallySupported` on one scanner over a remote target still yields `PartialDiscovery` with a full report written — same existing contract, now proven for remote too.
- **Security (skill.md §10)**: password/SSH-fingerprint/PEM-private-key-shaped strings injected into a discovered entity's metadata never appear in `report.json`/`report.html`/console output for a remote scan — same `ISecretRedactor` path every local scan already uses.
- **Determinism (skill.md §11)**: identical fake remote input produces byte-identical JSON/HTML across two CLI runs, once the one legitimate non-deterministic field (a wall-clock timestamp, appearing in three different encodings across JSON/HTML) is normalized out.
- **Performance (skill.md §12)**: not separately re-measured — no pipeline stage was modified, so the existing local-fixture performance coverage (Phase 10A/10D-x, plus the 10k/35k synthetic fixtures in Analysis.Tests/Reporting.Tests) applies identically regardless of target.
- **Real-machine validation (skill.md §13): NOT performed against a genuine live remote host** — no explicitly-authorized SSH Linux or WinRM Windows host was available in this environment, the same constraint 10D-2/10D-3B already disclosed. No arbitrary host scanned, no credential guessed, no remote service installed to manufacture a result. Existing real local Windows/Linux validation was not re-run (no production scanner/composition file was modified this phase).
- **Tests**: 12 new (`RemotePipelineIntegrationTests`, Cli.Tests — runs under both TFMs). Two small, additive test-harness extensions (`TestServiceProviderFactory.Build`/`CliTestRunner.RunAsync` gained an optional `ITargetTransport?` parameter).
- **Full regression** (2026-08-25): **1704/1704 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, 0 build warnings/errors — the pre-existing 1680-test baseline plus 24 additional executions (`RemotePipelineIntegrationTests`'s 12 tests × 2 Cli.Tests TFMs).
- **Known Limitations**: real live-remote (SSH or WinRM) end-to-end acceptance was not performed. `FakeRemoteTargetTransport` intentionally does not exercise real transport connect/failure-classification semantics — this phase proves pipeline integration, not transport-layer correctness (already proven elsewhere). No new large-scale remote-specific performance fixture was added.
- **Phase 10E-2 readiness**: the full pipeline is proven target-agnostic end to end for fake-remote-Linux and fake-remote-Windows discovery data, with no separate pipeline anywhere and no credential leakage. A future phase could pursue genuine live-remote acceptance once an authorized host is available, or begin whatever Phase 10E-2 scope is defined next.
- **Next phase**: not started. No GUI/HTTP API/PDF/email/cloud upload/migration execution/Kubernetes remote control/new scanner was implemented, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase 10E-2 — Remote Security & Failure Hardening

- **Objective**: harden the already-real SSH/WinRM transports against failure, cancellation, credential leakage, injection, local fallback, and partial failures — hardening only, no redesign unless a concrete defect required it.
- **Audit finding**: SSH-side was already extensively tested (host-key mismatch/rejection, credential non-logging, injection resistance, connect/dispose semantics all pre-existing). Nothing needed rewriting there.
- **The one genuine defect found and fixed**: `CimWinRmTransport.Query`/`InvokeAllowedMethod` had no `try`/`catch` around the `ICimSession` call — the real `CimNetSession` never throws `OperationCanceledException` (it catches internally), so this was never reachable in production, but the `ICimSession` interface contract didn't guarantee that of every implementation, leaving no transport-level defense-in-depth. Fixed minimally: both methods now catch `OperationCanceledException` and return `OperationStatus.Cancelled`. Zero behavior change for the real path; verified by a `FakeCimSession` configured to throw exactly that exception (a scenario the pre-fix code would have let escape as an unhandled exception).
- **SSH hardening additions** (`SshFailureHardeningTests`, 12 tests): full connect-failure classification matrix (a genuine finding: `HostKeyRejected()` maps to `TransportUnavailable`, not `AccessDenied` — defensible, now explicitly tested rather than implicit), dispose-after-failed-connect, no-retry-storm, determinism, no local-implementation swap after failure, one command's failure not affecting an independent one on the same session, credential-material sweep of failure text.
- **WinRM hardening additions** (`WinRmFailureHardeningTests`, 19 tests): the cancellation fix above, dispose-exactly-once (including after failure), no local fallback after failed connection, credential-material sweep + structural no-credential-field check, full `OperationStatus` propagation audit, determinism, no-retry-storm, partial capability-failure isolation.
- **CLI-level remote failure integration** (`RemoteFailureIntegrationTests`, 4 tests, Cli.Tests): pre-cancelled token, mid-discovery cancellation, unexpected exception, and determinism — all over a remote target, reusing the exact `CliExitCode`/no-partial-report contract local scans already have.
- **Local fallback prevention (failure-path variant)**: proven that even AFTER a connection attempt has failed, the object graph remains 100% remote-shaped (no local provider swap) — extending the pre-existing before-connection guarantee.
- **Credential leakage**: no new redaction mechanism needed — existing `ToString()` overrides already redact; new tests sweep actual failure-exception text (not just report output) for sentinel secrets, finding none.
- **Injection hardening**: re-verified, not re-built — existing 23-case SSH injection suite and WinRM allow-list/parameterized-query tests already cover every adversarial input class asked for.
- **Real-machine validation** (2026-08-25, same dev host): re-ran the real closed-port WinRM check (`127.0.0.1:1`) after the `CimWinRmTransport` fix — identical genuine WS-Man connection-refused error, exit code 1, no crash, confirming the fix didn't alter the connect-failure path. Re-ran local Windows regression (exit code 4, unaffected). **No genuine live-remote (SSH or WinRM) acceptance run was performed** — no authorized host available, same disclosed constraint as every prior remote phase.
- **Tests**: 39 new executions total (12 `SshFailureHardeningTests` + 19 `WinRmFailureHardeningTests` + 4 `RemoteFailureIntegrationTests` × 2 Cli.Tests TFMs).
- **Full regression** (2026-08-25): **1743/1743 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, 0 build warnings/errors — the pre-existing 1704-test baseline plus 39 additional executions.
- **Known Limitations**: no genuine live-remote acceptance run performed. The `CimWinRmTransport` fix is defense-in-depth only (never reachable via the real session path). Per-query cancellation still doesn't thread through `IWindowsRemoteRegistryOperations`/`IWindowsRemoteWmiOperations` (pre-existing, disclosed Phase 10D-3B limitation, out of this phase's hardening-only scope).
- **Phase 10E-3 readiness**: both transports have explicit failure/cancellation/disposal/determinism/no-local-fallback coverage at both the transport-unit and CLI-pipeline-integration level, with one genuine defense-in-depth defect found and fixed.
- **Next phase**: not started. No GUI/HTTP API/PDF/email/cloud upload/migration execution/Kubernetes remote control/new scanner/unrelated performance refactoring was implemented, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase 10E-3 — Remote Production-Safety Verification

- **Objective**: verify the SSH/WinRM transports are production-safe at multi-capability session reuse, partial-failure session survival, and double-dispose safety — gaps Phase 10E-2's own hardening pass didn't target — plus one new structural credential-shape check. Verification-only.
- **Result: no production defect found. Zero production code was changed this phase.**
- **Session reuse across multiple, different operations**: not previously tested explicitly (10E-2 proved "one session call for one failing operation" and "connect called once" in isolation, but not that a MIX of different operations — process query + file read + directory listing for SSH; registry + WMI + service query for WinRM — all reuse the same session). New tests confirm `Connect` is still only ever called once across that mix. Both transports already did this correctly by construction.
- **Partial failure never disposes the shared session — a genuine gap, now closed**: a failed operation (NotFound file read / thrown WMI query) is followed by an unrelated, successful operation on the SAME transport, proving the session survives the failure. Both transports already behaved this way (neither has a dispose-on-failure code path); now explicitly, permanently tested.
- **Repeated disposal is harmless — a genuine gap, now closed**: `.Dispose()` called twice on any of `SshRemoteTargetTransport`/`CimWinRmTransport`/`WinRmWindowsRemoteCapabilities`/`WinRmWindowsProviderSet` raises no exception — all are simple straight-through delegations with no disposed-guard, relying on the standard `IDisposable` convention that a well-behaved disposable tolerates repeated disposal (true for the fakes; not independently verified against the real `SshClient`/`CimSession` objects, since no live host exists to construct one against). No disposed-guard was added — none was needed once this was confirmed for the fakes, and adding one would be speculative hardening.
- **`FakeCimSession` upgraded** from `bool ConnectCalled`/`Disposed` to `int ConnectCallCount`/`DisposeCallCount` (additive, backward-compatible — old bool properties now computed from the counters) — needed to distinguish "called once" from "called twice," and closes a fixture-parity gap with `FakeSshSession`, which already had counters.
- **Credential-shaped property structural check — new granularity**: `NoCredentialShapedPropertyTests` inspects `RiskFinding`/`MigrationIssue`/`MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck`/`ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`'s TYPE SHAPE directly (not rendered report text, already swept by 10E-1/10E-2) — no property name suggests a credential slot, and `RiskFinding.Metadata` remains `IReadOnlyDictionary<string,string>` (never a strongly-typed credential holder).
- **`--verbose` credential safety**: the existing report-credential-sweep test now runs with `--verbose` included, closing a gap where verbose-specific console output had never been checked for leaked secrets.
- **Invalid remote target rejection before transport/network**: re-verified, not re-built — already rejected at parse time, before any network-capable code runs.
- **Real-machine validation**: no genuine live SSH or WinRM host was available in this environment (same WSL2 instance, no `sshd`, no `sudo` — not worked around). Since zero production connect/query code changed this phase, the real closed-port verifications already recorded in 10D-2/10D-3B/10E-2 remain the current valid evidence and were not redundantly re-run. **Live SSH/WinRM acceptance was NOT executed.**
- **Tests**: 14 new (`SshSessionLifecycleTests` 4, `WinRmSessionLifecycleTests` 4, `NoCredentialShapedPropertyTests` 6).
- **Full regression** (2026-08-25): **1757/1757 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, 0 build warnings/errors — the pre-existing 1743-test baseline plus 14 new tests.
- **Known Limitations**: no genuine live-remote acceptance run performed. Double-dispose safety verified against fakes only, not the real `SshClient`/`CimSession` objects. No new production capability/abstraction/architecture was added.
- **Phase 10F readiness**: both transports now have session-reuse, partial-failure-survival, double-dispose-safety, and structural credential-shape coverage in addition to 10E-2's failure/cancellation/disposal/determinism hardening. No further known verification gap identified by this project's own audit process.
- **Next phase**: not started. No GUI/HTTP API/PDF/migration execution/Kubernetes remote control/new scanner was implemented, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase GUI-1 — Desktop GUI Foundation

- **Objective**: establish the GUI project, MVVM architecture, dependency boundaries, navigation, application state, theme/resources, error boundary, and test infrastructure — foundation/shell only, no scan configuration/live progress/results visualization/report export.
- **Inspection first**: confirmed no GUI code, WPF/Avalonia/WinUI/MAUI reference, ViewModel, or App.xaml existed anywhere in the repository before this phase.
- **Framework decision: WPF** — chosen over Avalonia (adds a cross-platform dependency this Windows-only-built solution doesn't need)/WinUI 3 (heavier MSIX/Windows App SDK packaging)/MAUI (mobile-first, wrong fit). WPF ships in-box with the already-installed .NET SDK's Desktop workload (confirmed via `dotnet new list wpf`) — zero new NuGet packages for the framework itself, the smallest footprint of any candidate. No MVVM toolkit package either (hand-written `ObservableObject`/`RelayCommand`, ~25 lines).
- **New project**: `src/ServerSleuth.Gui/` (`App/`, `Composition/`, `Models/`, `Navigation/`, `Services/`, `ViewModels/`, `Views/`, `Resources/`) + `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/` (44 tests). Added to `ServerSleuth.slnx`.
- **Dependency direction**: `ServerSleuth.Gui` → `ServerSleuth.Core` (`ScanTarget`) + `ServerSleuth.Analysis` (`RiskSeverity`) only — mechanically verified (assembly-reference level) to have zero reference to Windows/Linux/Infrastructure/Cli/Reporting.
- **MVVM**: `MainViewModel` (navigation items + current placeholder page), one reusable `PlaceholderPageViewModel`/`PlaceholderView` pair for all six pages (Dashboard/Scan/Results/Migration/Reports/Settings) rather than six near-identical pairs.
- **Navigation**: `INavigationService`/`NavigationService` — explicit `CurrentPage`, deterministic (plain array, never Dictionary/HashSet enumeration), same-page navigation is a no-op.
- **Application state**: `GuiApplicationState` — one immutable record snapshot (`CurrentPage`/`Target`/`IsScanRunning`/`HasResults`/`LastErrorMessage`/`IsExportAvailable`), transitioned only via `IApplicationStateService.Update`. `Target` reuses the existing credential-free `ScanTarget` type directly. `Initial` snapshot is the literal "nothing has happened yet" state.
- **Theme**: palette, typography, spacing, button/nav-item/status-indicator styles, and five `Risk.*` brushes named exactly after `RiskSeverity`'s five real members (no invented "None" value, despite the spec's own example text mentioning one — locked in by a dedicated test).
- **Error boundary**: all three .NET/WPF unhandled-exception surfaces wired in `App.xaml.cs`, delegating to `GuiExceptionHandler` (extracted as a plain, WPF-independent class specifically so it's unit-testable) — logs the full exception via the existing `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging` infrastructure, publishes only a fixed generic message (never the raw exception message or stack trace) into application state.
- **A build-configuration finding, disclosed and fixed**: WPF SDK's implicit globbing only recognizes a project-ROOT `App.xaml` as the `ApplicationDefinition` item; keeping it under `App/` (the chosen folder layout) produced `CS5001` on first build. Fixed by explicitly reclassifying the item in the `.csproj` rather than moving the file or abandoning the folder structure.
- **Launch verification (performed, not fabricated)**: built `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe`, started it via `Start-Process`, confirmed it stayed running (no crash/exit) for 3 seconds before deliberate termination — proves DI composition, `MainWindow` construction, and XAML/resource loading all succeed with no startup exception. No interactive/visual verification was performed (no interactive desktop session available in this environment) and none was claimed.
- **Security**: startup is passive — nothing in `App.OnStartup`'s path can construct a `DiscoveryEngine`, contact a remote host, or touch registry/filesystem/process APIs (the referenced-assembly graph has nothing capable of it at all). `GuiApplicationState.Initial` is all-false/all-null.
- **Performance**: `CompositionRootTests` asserts composition-and-resolution completes in under 2 seconds (a real, checked `Stopwatch` bound).
- **Tests**: 44 new (`NavigationServiceTests`, `ApplicationStateServiceTests`, `GuiExceptionHandlerTests`, `MainViewModelTests`, `CompositionRootTests`, `RiskSeverityBrushConverterTests`, `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`, `NoDuplicatePipelineEngineTests`, `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`) — all ViewModel/service/composition/architecture-level, no live WPF `Application`/STA-thread test (deliberate, per the phase's own explicit tolerance for this).
- **Full regression** (2026-08-25): **1801/1801 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, 0 build warnings/errors — the pre-existing 1757-test baseline plus 44 new GUI tests, every other project's count unchanged.
- **Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual UI verification performed. No headless UI-automation test exists. `RiskSeverityBrushConverter` unused by any View yet (no risk display exists). The GUI does nothing beyond navigate between placeholders, by design.
- **GUI-2 readiness**: shell/navigation/state/composition/theming/error-boundary foundations are in place and tested. A future phase would replace placeholder pages with real ViewModels (Scan configuration first, most likely), consuming the existing CLI orchestration rather than reimplementing it.
- **Next phase**: not started. No scan configuration/real scan execution UI/live progress/remote credential forms/result dashboard/migration dashboard/report viewer/report export UI/PDF/HTTP API/web server/migration execution was implemented, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase GUI-2 — Scan Configuration & Target Setup

- **Objective**: build the full Scan Configuration UI, ViewModel, and service layer — target kind/platform, remote transport selection, remote credential input, output configuration, deterministic validation, and a validated `ScanRequest` boundary for GUI-3 — without executing any scan.
- **Inspection first**: confirmed no `ScanConfiguration`/`ScanConfigurationViewModel`/`TargetConfiguration`/`ScanPage`/`ScanView`/`ScanSettings` code existed anywhere before this phase; GUI-1's placeholder Scan page was still in place. Confirmed the reusable backend surface: `ScanTarget`/`TargetKind`/`TargetPlatform` (Core), `RemoteCredential`/`RemoteTransportKind` (Infrastructure, off-limits to the GUI), `ReportFormatOption` (Cli, off-limits), `ReportOverwritePolicy` (Reporting, off-limits) — all forbidden-project types, which drove the mirror-type decision below.
- **Central architectural decision — GUI-local mirror enums**: since the GUI-1 dependency boundary forbids referencing `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`/`ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Cli`/`ServerSleuth.Reporting`, but the real transport-kind/output-format/overwrite-policy/WinRM-auth-mechanism enums all live in those projects, four small GUI-local mirror enums were created (`ScanTransportKind`, `ScanOutputFormat`, `ScanOverwritePolicy`, `ScanWinRmAuthenticationMechanism`) — deliberately differently named from their backend counterparts (verified by `NoDuplicateDomainModelTests`), carrying zero logic, and documented as the "GUI-specific adapter genuinely required" carve-out the spec itself anticipated. `ScanTarget` itself, by contrast, is reused directly since `ServerSleuth.Core` is already an allowed dependency.
- **Credential boundary — structurally enforced, not just conventional**: `ScanCredentialInput` (transient: `Username` + `SecureString? Password`) is the only place a password exists as data; it is never a property of `ScanConfigurationState` or `ScanRequest`, never held by `GuiApplicationState`, and `ScanConfigurationViewModel` has no gettable `Password` property at all — only a write-only `SetPassword(SecureString)` method, so the ViewModel itself cannot leak a password back out via data binding even in principle. `ScanConfigurationViewModel`'s constructor also has zero dependency on `IApplicationStateService`, meaning it has no channel to write a credential into persistent state even by mistake.
- **SSH scoped to private-key-only auth**, deliberately narrower than `RemoteCredential`'s theoretical capability — matches the actual CLI's real `--ssh-*` surface (`ScanOptionsParser` never wires up SSH password auth), per the spec's "do not expose transport combinations the existing backend does not support" instruction. WinRM accepts a direct `PasswordBox`-entered password (unlike the CLI's env-var-only convention) — justified because a WPF `PasswordBox` has none of the CLI's shell-history/process-listing leak risk; disclosed as a deliberate GUI-specific difference.
- **Deterministic platform→transport derivation**: `TransportKind` has a **private setter** on the ViewModel, always derived by `ApplyDeterministicTransportForPlatform()` (Linux→SSH, Windows→WinRM) whenever `Platform` changes on a Remote target — making an invalid combination (Linux+WinRM, Windows+SSH) structurally unreachable through the ViewModel's public API. The validator independently re-checks this as defense-in-depth, since `ScanConfigurationState` is a plain record any caller could still construct with a mismatched combination directly.
- **Validation**: `ScanConfigurationValidator` is fully deterministic and side-effect-free — no DNS/ping/TCP/SSH/WinRM/filesystem access. Checks target (Remote requires non-empty host), transport/platform match, required-credential presence (SSH: username+private-key-path+host-fingerprint; WinRM: username+non-empty password), and output directory (checked only via `Path.GetInvalidPathChars()`, never touching the filesystem). A dedicated test proves a sentinel password value never appears in any produced `ValidationError.Message`.
- **Start Scan boundary**: `IScanRequestFactory.Create(...)` builds a credential-free `ScanRequest` (wraps `ScanTarget` + output settings) only after successful validation; `ScanConfigurationViewModel.StartScan()` raises a `ScanRequested` event carrying that request rather than invoking anything scan-related. Structurally proven never to execute discovery: the ViewModel's constructor, fields, and methods contain no parameter/return/field of a type name matching `DiscoveryEngine`/`ScanPipelineRunner`/`DiscoveryScannerRegistry` (`NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`).
- **UI**: `ScanConfigurationView` (Local/Remote radio buttons, Platform combo, Remote Host textbox, read-only derived Connection label, conditionally-enabled Windows/Linux credential sub-panels, Output directory/format/overwrite/verbose controls, validation-errors list, Validate/Start Scan/Cancel buttons). Code-behind reads `PasswordBox.SecurePassword` and calls `ViewModel.SetPassword(...)` — the password is never bound through XAML. `MainWindow` gained a `DataTemplate` routing `ScanConfigurationViewModel` to this view; `MainViewModel`/`IPageViewModel` extended so the Scan nav item shows the real ViewModel while the other five pages remain placeholders.
- **Tests**: 58 new (`ScanConfigurationValidatorTests` 14, `ScanRequestFactoryTests` 5, `ScanConfigurationViewModelTests` ~25, `NoDuplicateDomainModelTests`, `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`, plus `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests` and `MainViewModelTests` extensions) — covering target/platform/transport switching, invalid combination rejection, credential requiredness, output format/overwrite/verbose toggles, validation determinism, no-execution guarantees, and repeated-request-creation equivalence.
- **Two self-identified test-authoring corrections** (no user feedback involved): the `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests` forbidden-substring list originally over-matched `SshPrivateKeyPath`/`SshPrivateKeyPassphraseEnvironmentVariable` (a file path and an env-var name, not secrets) — fixed by removing `"privatekey"`/`"passphrase"` from the substring list and adding a dedicated type-shape test instead; and `ScanConfigurationValidatorTests`' original "default configuration is valid" assertion was wrong since `ScanConfigurationState.Initial` has an empty `OutputDirectory` (a required field) — split into a "with an output directory, valid" test and a "default, invalid only for that one reason" test.
- **Launch verification**: rebuilt and relaunched `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` via `Start-Process`/`Stop-Process` (same bounded-verification method as GUI-1, since no interactive desktop session is available); confirmed no crash/exit and no orphan process. No interactive/visual confirmation that the Scan page renders correctly was performed and none is claimed — the same disclosed limitation as GUI-1.
- **Security**: all ten GUI-2 §Step14 properties verified — password absent from `ValidationResult`/`ScanRequest`/`GuiApplicationState`/navigation state/exception text/UI bindings; no network calls or filesystem writes during validation; no scanner construction at GUI startup; invalid targets rejected before any transport type is even referenced.
- **Full regression** (2026-08-25): **1859/1859 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, 0 build warnings/errors — the pre-existing 1801-test baseline plus 58 new GUI tests, every other project's count unchanged.
- **Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual UI verification performed (same as GUI-1). No "remember password"/credential-vault integration (explicitly out of scope). SSH is private-key-only (matches the CLI, not a limitation of `RemoteCredential` itself). Output-path validation is syntactic only — no existence/writability check (deliberately deferred to GUI-3/export time, per the spec's own "do NOT access the filesystem merely because the user changed the path" instruction).
- **GUI-3 readiness**: a fully validated, credential-free `ScanRequest` plus the separately-held `ScanCredentialInput` are now producible from the UI; GUI-3 can consume `ScanRequested` to begin wiring actual scan execution/progress without any GUI-2 rework.
- **Next phase**: not started. No live scan execution/DiscoveryEngine invocation from the GUI/live progress/scanner status/results dashboard/application detail/migration dashboard/report viewer/report export UI/PDF/HTTP API/migration execution was implemented, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase GUI-3 — Scan Execution & Live Progress

- **Objective**: connect GUI-2's `ScanRequested` event to the real, unmodified scan pipeline (Discovery through Export), with live progress, cancellation, concurrency protection, and a completion summary — without duplicating the pipeline or weakening the credential/dependency boundaries.
- **The core architectural conflict, resolved**: skill.md GUI-3 requires both reusing the existing `ScanPipelineRunner`/transports/`ReportArtifactFactory` AND keeping `ServerSleuth.Gui` free of any direct reference to `ServerSleuth.Infrastructure`/`ServerSleuth.Windows`/`ServerSleuth.Cli`/`ServerSleuth.Reporting` — jointly unsatisfiable in one project, since actually running a scan needs those assemblies somewhere. Resolved with two new projects: `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost` (the composition/execution boundary — structurally the WPF-analog of `ServerSleuth.Cli`'s own `CompositionRoot`/`ScanCommand`, allowed to reference every platform-specific project) and `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` (a small shared-DTO project referencing only `ServerSleuth.Core`, breaking what would otherwise be a circular `Gui` ↔ `ExecutionHost` reference). Verified, not just asserted: `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests` (unmodified) still confirms `ServerSleuth.Gui.dll`'s own compiled `AssemblyRef` table has zero direct reference to any of the five forbidden assemblies — a `ProjectReference` to `ExecutionHost` alone doesn't add one, since no source file compiled into `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself ever imports an Infrastructure/Windows/Reporting type.
- **`ScanPipelineRunner`/`ScanPipelineResult` moved from `ServerSleuth.Cli.Pipeline` to `ServerSleuth.Analysis.Orchestration`** — a relocation, not a rewrite (the type only ever used Core/Analysis types). Both `ScanCommand` (CLI) and `GuiScanExecutor` (GUI) now call the literal same class, the strongest possible guarantee of pipeline-semantic equivalence, proven by a dedicated `CliGuiEquivalenceTests` test comparing rendered report output byte-for-byte (modulo timestamp) between a direct pipeline invocation and the real `GuiScanExecutor`. `Analyze` gained one additive, optional `Action<PipelineStage>? onStageStarting` parameter for GUI progress reporting — zero behavior change for the CLI's existing two-argument call. One test (`TargetAgnosticismTests` in Analysis.Tests) needed a single, narrowly-scoped exemption for `ScanPipelineRunner` itself (the one legitimate orchestration bridge in that assembly that takes a `DiscoveryContext`) — every pure analysis engine remains fully protected.
- **Credential lifetime**: `ScanConfigurationViewModel.ScanRequested` widened to carry a new `ScanRequestedEventArgs` (pairing the still-credential-free `ScanRequest` with a separately-held `ScanCredentialInput`, never merged); `MainViewModel` hands both straight to `ScanExecutionViewModel.Start(...)` and stores neither. `ScanConfigurationViewModel` now clears its own credential field immediately after a successful `StartScan()`. `ScanExecutionViewModel`'s only constructor dependency is the abstract `IGuiScanExecutor` — no field can hold a credential. `GuiScanExecutor` converts `ScanCredentialInput` into the real `RemoteCredential`/`WindowsRemoteCredential` only at the last composition moment; the WinRM password is handed through as the SAME `SecureString` the GUI's `PasswordBox` produced (never round-tripped through a plain string or environment variable, unlike the CLI's own convention — a deliberate, disclosed GUI-specific improvement).
- **Progress**: reuses the BCL's own `IProgress<T>` — no second progress vocabulary invented. `ScanStage` (10 members: Preparing/Discovery/Analysis/RiskAnalysis/MigrationAssessment/Reporting/Export/Completed/Failed/Cancelled) matches skill.md's own minimum list; never a fabricated percentage anywhere (`ScanProgressState` has no percentage field at all).
- **A genuine race condition found and fixed during test authoring**: `Progress<T>` marshals reports asynchronously (via the ThreadPool when no `SynchronizationContext` is active, as in a plain test host) — a progress report queued just before completion could still be DELIVERED afterward, silently regressing `ScanExecutionState.Status` from `Completed` back to `Running`. Reproduced deterministically (not theorized) via `ScanExecutionViewModelTests`, fixed with a one-line guard: the progress callback now checks `!IsFinished` before applying a report. A real production correctness fix — a slow scanner's progress report arriving right as a scan finishes/is cancelled could hit the same race in the live app.
- **Cancellation**: `ScanExecutionViewModel.Cancel()` calls the existing `CancellationTokenSource.Cancel()` — no new mechanism, idempotent by construction, proven safe both mid-scan and when nothing is running. `GuiScanExecutorTests` proves cancellation during discovery reaches `Cancelled` and writes no partial report — the existing atomic `LocalFileReportExporter` remains the sole authority.
- **Concurrency protection**: a private `_isRunning` guard on `ScanExecutionViewModel.Start` — a second `Start` call while one is running is silently ignored, proven to invoke the executor exactly once even when called three times in a row.
- **Error handling**: every user-facing `ScanCompletionState.ErrorMessage` is one of a small number of fixed, generic strings; `GuiScanExecutor` logs the full exception via the existing `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging` infrastructure (matching GUI-1's `GuiExceptionHandler` convention) and never surfaces a raw exception message to the caller.
- **UI**: `ScanExecutionView` (target/platform header, current-stage display, per-scanner status list, an indeterminate progress bar — never a fabricated percentage, completion summary, Cancel/Start-New-Scan buttons). `MainViewModel` toggles which ViewModel the existing `NavigationPage.Scan` nav item shows (Configuration vs. Execution) via a plain bool flag set/cleared by the `ScanRequested`/`ReturnToConfigurationRequested` events — no new nav item, no `Dictionary`/`HashSet` state.
- **Tests**: 29 new (`ScanExecutionViewModelTests` 11 in `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests` against a `FakeGuiScanExecutor`; `GuiScanExecutorTests` 8 + `CliGuiEquivalenceTests` 1 in a new `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests` project against fake transports/discovery engines — never a real machine's own scanners or a real SSH/WinRM connection; plus extensions to `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`/`NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`/`CompositionRootTests`).
- **Launch verification (performed, not fabricated)**: `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` rebuilt, confirmed to stay running for 3 seconds with no crash and no orphan process — same bounded method as every prior GUI phase. No interactive exercise of the Scan Execution flow was performed (same disclosed environment constraint as GUI-1/GUI-2).
- **Real-machine validation**: none of this phase's tests touch a real machine's scanners or a real SSH/WinRM host, per skill.md's own "do not require real remote hosts for ordinary tests" instruction — the existing disclosed limitation (no authorized live-remote host available) is unchanged. The pre-existing CLI real-machine baseline was not re-run since no scanner/transport/pipeline business logic was modified.
- **Full regression** (2026-08-25): **1888/1888 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, 0 build warnings/errors — the pre-existing 1859-test baseline plus 29 new tests.
- **Known Limitations**: no interactive/visual UI verification performed. Discovery progress is reported at only two points (start/finish), not per-scanner-as-it-completes, since `IDiscoveryEngine.RunAsync` itself exposes no such callback (a pre-existing, inherited limitation, out of this phase's minimal-adapter-only scope). "View Results"/an actual GUI-4 Results Dashboard was not implemented — `NewScanCommand` only returns to Scan Configuration for now. No genuine live-remote execution was exercised through the GUI path specifically.
- **GUI-4 readiness**: a fully working, tested Scan Execution flow runs the real pipeline end-to-end from the GUI and produces a `ScanCompletionState` with real entity counts, scanner statuses, and written report file names — ready for a future GUI-4 phase to build an actual Results Dashboard/report viewer on top of.
- **Next phase**: not started. No Results Dashboard/risk charts/application detail/dependency graph visualization/migration dashboard/report viewer/PDF/report export controls beyond the existing pipeline/HTTP API/migration execution/Kubernetes remote control/new scanners was implemented, per this phase's own strict-stop instruction. Awaiting explicit confirmation before proceeding.

## Phase GUI-4 — Results Dashboard & Result Navigation

- **Objective**: build a real, read-only Results Dashboard over an already-completed scan's `ScanExecutionState` — Scan Summary, Risk Summary, Migration Summary, Applications table with application-detail drill-down, Dependencies, Migration Issues/Actions/Verification Checks, Scanner Status, and Report artifacts — with zero second execution of any Discovery/Correlation/Boundary/Expansion/Validation/Risk/Migration/Reporting stage, and "View Results"/"New Scan" navigation replacing/supplementing GUI-3's placeholder-only completion flow.
- **Inspection first**: confirmed no `ResultsDashboard`/`ScanResultState`/`ViewResults` code existed anywhere before this phase (`grep` across the whole repository). Read every domain type the phase spec named — `ServerMigrationAssessmentReport`, `ServerRiskSummary`/`ApplicationRiskSummary`/`RiskFinding`, `MigrationAssessmentSummary`/`ServerMigrationAssessment`/`ApplicationMigrationAssessment`/`MigrationIssue`/`MigrationDependency`/`MigrationDependencyGroup`, `MigrationPlan`/`MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck`, `AssessmentCoverage`/`CoverageWarning`, `ScanPipelineResult` — before designing anything, and confirmed the exact GUI-3 gap: `ScanCompletionState` only ever carried counts, discarding the real `pipelineResult` `GuiScanExecutor` already computes.
- **The GUI-3→GUI-4 wiring**: `ScanCompletionState.PipelineResult` (new, nullable `ScanPipelineResult`) ← `GuiScanExecutor`'s own already-in-scope `pipelineResult` local (one-line addition on the success/partial return) ← `ScanExecutionState.PipelineResult` (via the existing `WithCompletion`) ← `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s sole constructor parameter. The SAME instance at every hop — never a copy, never re-derived. `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts` gained a `ServerSleuth.Analysis` project reference to compile against `ScanPipelineResult`; `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself already had that reference since GUI-1, so nothing new became reachable from the GUI as a result.
- **No second DTO layer for most data**: `ResultsDashboardViewModel` binds directly to real `ServerSleuth.Analysis` collections (`MigrationIssue`/`MigrationAction`/`MigrationVerificationCheck`/`MigrationDependency`/`MigrationDependencyGroup`/`RiskFinding`/`CoverageWarning`) wherever the report already scopes them correctly. Two genuine, thin presentation types were written — `ApplicationRowViewModel`/`ApplicationDetailViewModel` — purely to join `ApplicationMigrationSummary` (Phase 8C, migration-side, single aggregate `RiskSeverity`) with the matching `ApplicationRiskSummary` (Phase 7B, risk-side, confidence/top-risks/finding-count detail), keyed by boundary Id — every list either type exposes is the exact same `IReadOnlyList` instance from the source record, never copied or re-filtered.
- **Result lifetime**: one `ResultsDashboardViewModel?` field on `MainViewModel`, built exactly once when `ScanExecutionViewModel.ViewResultsRequested` fires (a new event/command, alongside the unchanged `ReturnToConfigurationRequested`/`NewScanCommand`), reused for every later navigation to `NavigationPage.Results` — never rebuilt on tab-switch, never rebuilt on reopening the dashboard. Structurally credential-free: the ViewModel's only input is GUI-3's own already credential-free `ScanExecutionState`.
- **Navigation**: reuses `NavigationPage.Results` (a pre-existing GUI-1 placeholder nav item) and the exact deterministic bool-flag pattern GUI-3 established for the Scan page — no new navigation system, no `Dictionary`/`HashSet` state. `ScanExecutionView` gained a "View Results" button (enabled only once `IsFinished`) alongside the pre-existing "Start New Scan."
- **Dashboard composition**: `ResultsDashboardView.xaml` — Scan Summary/Risk Summary/Migration Summary/Dependency Summary cards, a filterable/searchable Applications `ListView`, an inline selected-application detail panel (`ApplicationDetailView`, routed via a local `DataTemplate`), and `Expander`-collapsed Risk Findings/Migration Issues/Migration Actions/Verification Checks/Scanner Status/Reports sections. Native WPF controls only (`Border`/`Expander`/`ListView`/`GridView`/`WrapPanel`) — no third-party UI or chart library, zero new NuGet packages.
- **Risk visualization**: new `AggregateSeverityBrushConverter` reuses the EXACT SAME `Risk.Info`/`Low`/`Medium`/`High`/`Critical` brushes `Theme.xaml` declared in GUI-1 (`AggregateSeverity`'s five real-finding-equivalent members share those names by construction); `AggregateSeverity.None` falls back to the existing neutral `App.MutedForeground` rather than inventing a "Risk.None" brush the domain enum has no `RiskSeverity` counterpart for. `RiskSeverityBrushConverter` — written in GUI-1, unused until now — is finally wired into the application-detail Top Risks list.
- **Filtering is presentation-only**: `SearchText`/`SeverityFilter`/`OnlyWithIssues` recompute a fresh `FilteredApplications` projection; the master `Applications` list (and every domain object any row wraps) is never reordered or mutated — proven with a before/after report-JSON-serialization equality check, not just an assertion of intent.
- **Empty/partial results**: `PipelineResult` stays `null` for `Cancelled`/`Failed` scans; `HasResults`/`HasNoResults` drive an explicit "No results are available for this scan yet." banner, and every list section (Applications/Top Risks/Migration Issues/Actions/Dependencies/Reports) carries its own explicit "No X detected/found/written." empty-state text rather than an ambiguous blank table.
- **Security boundary**: `ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests` (new) performs both a name-based sweep (extending `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`) and a genuine structural sweep — a reflective walk of every `ServerSleuth.*`-namespaced type reachable from `ResultsDashboardViewModel`'s public property graph, confirming none is `SecureString`-typed or credential/token/secret-named. `EvidenceRecord` redaction is inherited from Phase 2's construction-time `SecretRedactor` (never re-implemented at display time).
- **No pipeline re-execution**: `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests` extended with `ResultsDashboardViewModel`/`ApplicationDetailViewModel`/`ApplicationRowViewModel` in the reflected-over type list and `RiskAggregator`/`MigrationPlanEngine` added to the forbidden-name list. `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests` proves behaviorally that "View Results," switching through every other nav page, and returning to Results calls `IGuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync` exactly once for the whole sequence.
- **Determinism**: five repeated `ResultsDashboardViewModel` constructions over the identical `ScanExecutionState` produce byte-identical application/risk/dependency ordering every time — never Dictionary/HashSet-ordered.
- **Performance**: a hand-built fixture (`ScanResultFixtureFactory` — `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests` cannot reference Windows/Linux/Infrastructure to run a real pipeline) generates 1,000 applications × (10 findings, 5 dependencies, 10 actions, 20 checks) = 10,000/5,000/10,000/20,000 totals. Measured via `Stopwatch`: `ResultsDashboardViewModel` construction completes in well under 10 seconds; filtering the resulting 1,000-row list completes in well under 1 second. Fixture authoring itself is excluded from the measured window.
- **Launch verification (performed, not fabricated)**: `dotnet build` succeeded for `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` (0 warnings/errors — a `x:Type`/`StaticResource` error in the new XAML would have failed this step outright), and the same bounded `Start-Process`/3-second-survival/`Stop-Process` check every prior GUI phase used was repeated, confirming no startup exception from the new `DataTemplate`/converter registrations.
- **Real-machine validation**: **NOT performed** — this phase, like every GUI phase before it, ran in an environment with no interactive Windows desktop session available. No visual rendering of `ResultsDashboardView`, no interactive "click View Results / select an application / return to dashboard" exercise was performed or is claimed. Equivalent behavioral coverage instead comes entirely from fixture-based tests (`ResultsDashboardViewModelTests`, `ApplicationDetailViewModelTests`, `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests`, `ResultsDashboardDeterminismAndMutationTests`, `ResultsDashboardPerformanceTests`).
- **A pre-existing, environmental test flake observed, not fixed**: two already-existing GUI-3 tests (`ScanExecutionViewModelTests.Start_BeginsRunning_...`/`Start_WhenExecutorThrows_...`), which depend on `Progress<T>`'s asynchronous ThreadPool-marshalled callback completing within a short polling window, intermittently failed in roughly 2 of 7 full `ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests` runs performed during this phase's own iterative testing (always passed when re-run in isolation). Neither test's assertions nor any GUI-3 production code were modified — out of this phase's scope, and the final regression run recorded below was clean, matching the project's own established precedent of disclosing (not silently absorbing) environment-dependent timing flakiness.
- **Tests**: 47 new test executions — `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/ViewModels/Results/` (`ResultsDashboardViewModelTests`, `ApplicationDetailViewModelTests`, `ResultsDashboardDeterminismAndMutationTests`, `ResultsDashboardPerformanceTests`), `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/ViewModels/MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests.cs`, `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/Architecture/ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests.cs`, plus extensions to `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`/`NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests` and two new `GuiScanExecutorTests` cases in `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests`.
- **Full regression** (2026-08-27): with only the production (non-test) changes applied, `dotnet test` recorded **1902/1902 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. After adding this phase's own tests, the final clean run recorded:
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:              61 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:         442 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:   171 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:            345 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:          376 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0):      98 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0-win): 104 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped

## Phase GUI-4A — Interactive Acceptance Verification (attempted)

- **Objective**: perform a genuine interactive click-through of GUI-4's Results Dashboard (Scan Configuration → Execution → Results → Application Detail → New Scan) on a real Windows desktop session, per skill.md GUI-4A.
- **Outcome**: **interactive validation was not executed** — this environment (a headless coding agent) has no Windows desktop UI automation or screenshot capability; only Chrome browser automation tools exist, which do not apply to a WPF desktop app. Per the phase's own explicit fallback instruction, the interactive portion was stopped and reported rather than fabricated.
- **What WAS genuinely performed**: `dotnet build` (0 warnings/errors), a non-visual `Start-Process` liveness check (the built `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` stayed running 3 seconds, then terminated cleanly with no orphan process — explicitly NOT reported as visual validation), and a full regression run (`dotnet test`) matching the existing 1949/1949 baseline exactly, since no production code was touched.
- **No production changes**: per the phase's own instruction, since no genuine defect could be discovered without real interaction, zero production code was modified — ARCHITECTURE.md/PROGRESS.md/CHANGELOG.md were correspondingly NOT updated with an "interactive validation performed" claim for this attempt (documented here instead, in this one retrospective entry).

## Phase GUI-5 — Production GUI Completion & Export

- **Objective**: complete the GUI workflow with a Report Export UI and Report Viewer over an already-completed scan's report, plus dashboard-level "New Scan," reusing the existing `ReportArtifactFactory`/`IReportExporter`/`LocalFileReportExporter`/renderers exclusively — no second export/reporting implementation, no new discovery/analysis/migration/reporting engine, no execution of migration actions/verification checks/remote commands.
- **Inspection first**: re-read ARCHITECTURE.md's GUI-3/GUI-4 addenda and `.csproj` comments to confirm the established dependency boundary (`ServerSleuth.Gui` → Core/Analysis/Gui.Contracts/Gui.ExecutionHost only; only `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost` may reference Reporting/Infrastructure/Windows/Linux) before writing anything. Found `GuiScanExecutor`'s own private `ExportReport` method already calling `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle`/`LocalFileReportExporter.Export` at end-of-scan — the exact real API this phase needed to reach a second time, on demand, from the dashboard.
- **Export boundary**: `IGuiReportExportService` (new, `ServerSleuth.Gui.Contracts`) mirrors `IGuiScanExecutor`'s existing composition-boundary pattern exactly; its real implementation, `GuiReportExportService` (new, `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost`), is the only new code allowed to touch `ReportArtifactFactory`/`LocalFileReportExporter` directly. `GuiScanExecutor.ExportReport` was REFACTORED (not duplicated) to call the same new shared `GuiReportExportService.ExportReport` static method the dashboard's on-demand export now also calls.
- **Report Viewer boundary**: `IGuiReportViewerService`/`GuiReportViewerService` (both new) live directly in `ServerSleuth.Gui` itself, NOT routed through `Gui.ExecutionHost` — reading an already-written UTF-8 text file from a directory the user already chose is a plain local file read (`System.IO.File`), not a Reporting/Infrastructure concern; `NoDirectPlatformAccessTests`' existing "Gui references only Core+Analysis+Gui.Contracts+Gui.ExecutionHost" fact holds unchanged. Defense-in-depth path validation rejects `..`/rooted/invalid-character file names even though the only real caller only ever passes an exporter-validated name.
- **Backward-compatible wiring**: both new services are OPTIONAL (default-`null`) constructor parameters on `ResultsDashboardViewModel`/`MainViewModel` — all ~30 existing GUI-4 test call sites kept compiling unchanged; `Export`/`OpenReportCommand.CanExecute` return `false` (disabled, not throwing) when a service is absent. `ScanExecutionState.OutputDirectory` (new, non-sensitive) is threaded from `ScanRequest.OutputDirectory` via an optional second parameter on `ScanExecutionState.StartingFor` (same backward-compatibility approach).
- **Export flow**: format (JSON/HTML/Both)/overwrite-policy (FailIfExists/Overwrite)/directory (defaults to the scan's own output directory, freely editable) are bindable properties; `ExportReportCommand` hands the dashboard's existing `Report` instance straight to `IGuiReportExportService.Export` and publishes whatever `GuiReportExportResult` comes back — verified to never fabricate success (`ResultsDashboardExportAndViewerTests`).
- **Report Viewer flow**: reads the selected already-written report file's raw text (JSON and HTML both shown as plain text — no WYSIWYG HTML rendering, no script execution, since that would need a third-party control this phase's "no new NuGet packages" constraint rules out) into a read-only, scrollable `TextBox`; never regenerates the report, never starts a scan.
- **Dashboard integration/navigation**: "New Scan" added directly to the Results Dashboard (`NewScanCommand`/`NewScanRequested`), routed through a new shared `MainViewModel.GoToScanConfiguration()` (a refactor of the existing GUI-3 handler, not a second implementation) that still only calls the existing `NavigationService`/`ApplyCurrentPage` — no second navigation system. "View Application" was already satisfied by GUI-4's `SelectApplicationCommand`.
- **Security/no-mutation/no-reexecution**: `GuiReportExportResult`/`GuiReportViewResult` added to `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests`' sweeps. New `GuiReportExportServiceArchitectureTests` (in `ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests`) reflects over `GuiReportExportService` confirming no orchestration/discovery-engine type is referenced. `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests` gained two behavioral tests proving export (JSON then HTML) and opening a report each still leave `IGuiScanExecutor.ExecuteAsync`'s call count at exactly 1. Dedicated tests prove export never mutates the report (reference identity + unchanged counts, both at the ViewModel layer with a fake and at the real service layer against the actual `ReportArtifactFactory`/`LocalFileReportExporter`).
- **Determinism**: `JsonReportRenderer`/`HtmlReportRenderer`'s existing opt-in-only `generatedAt` (both default `null`, never passed by `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle`) means two exports of the same report produce byte-identical files — verified directly against real exported bytes.
- **Performance**: reused GUI-4's 1,000-application/10,000-finding/5,000-dependency/10,000-action/20,000-check fixture; one export call + one open-report call together complete well under 1 second (against fakes — proves the ViewModel orchestration itself does no per-item loop of its own over the large collections).
- **Real-machine validation**: **NOT performed** — same environment constraint as every prior GUI phase (no interactive Windows desktop session available). `dotnet build` succeeded for `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` (0 warnings/errors); the same bounded `Start-Process`/liveness/`Stop-Process` check was repeated and is explicitly NOT reported as visual validation.
- **Tests**: 46 new test executions — `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.ExecutionHost.Tests/GuiReportExportServiceTests.cs` (8), `GuiReportExportServiceArchitectureTests.cs` (3), `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/Services/GuiReportViewerServiceTests.cs` (6), `test/ServerSleuth.Gui.Tests/ViewModels/Results/ResultsDashboardExportAndViewerTests.cs`, one new `ResultsDashboardPerformanceTests` case, three new `MainViewModelResultsNavigationTests` cases, plus extensions to `NoCredentialShapedGuiStateTests`/`ResultsDashboardSecurityBoundaryTests`.
- **Full regression** (2026-08-27): `dotnet build` — 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test`:
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:              61 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:         442 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:   171 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:            345 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:          376 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0):      98 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0-win): 104 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:        146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:       14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped

  Total: 1995/1995 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 0 build warnings/errors
