  During iterative development (not in this final run), `ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests.Integration.WindowsScannerIntegrationTests.WindowsBinaryDiscoveryScanner_AgainstRealMachine_IsBoundedAndNeverExecutesAnything` failed once on a real-machine timing assertion (binary discovery took longer than its threshold under momentary system load) and passed cleanly on every other run including this final one — an environmental, real-machine-timing flake unrelated to any GUI-5 change (no `ServerSleuth.Windows` source was touched this phase), not fixed or masked.
- **Known Limitations**: Report Viewer shows raw text only (no WYSIWYG HTML rendering — would require a `WebView2`/similar third-party control, out of scope per "no new NuGet packages unless justified"). No interactive/visual verification performed (same constraint as every prior GUI phase). Export always re-renders JSON/HTML from the current report via `ReportArtifactFactory.CreateBundle` rather than caching the original end-of-scan bundle bytes (cheap/deterministic/in-memory, matching `ReportExportRunner`'s own existing pattern).
- **GUI-6 readiness**: a full inspect → export → view-report → new-scan loop now works end-to-end from a completed scan. Not started: WYSIWYG HTML viewer, dependency-graph visualization, richer per-section filtering, migration/verification execution, HTTP API, PDF, cloud/email, credential vault, Kubernetes control, new scanners — per this phase's own strict-stop instruction. Awaiting explicit confirmation before GUI-6.

## Phase GUI-6 — Final GUI Production Readiness & Acceptance Hardening

- **Objective**: a HARDENING/AUDIT phase, not feature development — verify the full GUI-1 through GUI-5 workflow is robust, deterministic, secure, and release-ready; fix only genuine defects with the smallest correct change; no GUI-7, no new functionality.
- **Audit outcome**: inspected `MainViewModel`, `ScanExecutionViewModel`, `ResultsDashboardViewModel`, `ApplicationDetailViewModel`, `GuiScanExecutor`, `GuiReportExportService`, `GuiReportViewerService`, `NavigationService`, `GuiExceptionHandler` line-by-line against every UX-state/security/no-reexecution requirement in the GUI-6 spec. **Found no genuine production defect.** Start Scan is already unreachable while a scan runs (the Scan page slot shows execution, not configuration, and `ScanExecutionViewModel.Start` itself no-ops on re-entrancy); Export/Open already correctly disable via `CanExecute` with no service/report/directory; New Scan/Cancel already gate correctly on `_isRunning`; the dashboard is only ever rebuilt from an actual completed scan, never showing stale results as a new one; failed/cancelled scans already correctly render the explicit empty state rather than fabricated success. **Zero production `.cs` files were modified.**
- **Test gaps closed** (all additive, no existing test changed):
  - `MainViewModel` added to `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`' existing reflection sweep (constructor/field/method orchestration-type check) — the one workflow type not yet directly covered by that architecture test.
  - `ScanResultFixtureFactory.Options` gained `ServerLevelIssueCount`/`SharedInfrastructureCount` so the fixture can represent "server-only findings" and "shared infrastructure findings" — two named GUI-6 §3 scenarios the existing fixture could not previously build (it always produced empty `ServerLevelIssues`/`SharedInfrastructure`). Two new `ResultsDashboardViewModelTests` verify the dashboard surfaces both correctly and never double-attributes a server-level issue into an application's own issue list.
  - New `GuiScanExecutorTests.ExecuteAsync_ForARemoteTarget_WhenTheTransportFailsToConnect_ReturnsFailed_WithAGenericMessage_AndNoPipelineResult` — the "remote transport failure" scenario named in GUI-6 §3, using a `ScanTarget.Remote(...)` target and a connect-shaped composition exception; confirms the same generic `Failed(...)`/no-leak/no-fabricated-`PipelineResult` behavior the existing local-target tests already proved, now explicitly for the remote case.
  - Every other section-3 scenario (success/partial/failed/cancelled/zero-entity/zero-application/Blocked/NeedsRemediation/ReadyWithConditions/export-failure/report-not-found/invalid-path/FailIfExists/Overwrite) — inspected and confirmed already directly covered by GUI-4/GUI-5's existing suites; not duplicated.
- **Sections re-confirmed by inspection, no change needed**: §2 no-reexecution (behavioral suite already complete), §4 no-mutation/determinism, §5 credential isolation, §6 error handling (single `GuiExceptionHandler` boundary, no second mechanism), §8 large-result performance (existing 1,000-app fixture test already measures dashboard+detail together, since `ApplicationRowViewModel` builds its `Detail` eagerly), §10 dependency-boundary architecture, §11 report-viewer raw-text-only security model, §12 export-only-through-the-real-exporter guarantee.
- **Interactive validation**: **not performed** — same environment constraint as GUI-4A/GUI-5 (no interactive Windows desktop session available). Non-visual liveness check repeated (`dotnet build` clean; `ServerSleuth.Gui.exe` launched, alive 3s, terminated cleanly, no orphan) — explicitly not visual validation.
- **Real-machine CLI/pipeline smoke check**: not performed — no backend/pipeline code was touched this phase.
- **Tests**: 6 new test executions total (1 in `GuiScanExecutorTests`, 2 in `ResultsDashboardViewModelTests`, 3 additional `[Theory]` instances from `MainViewModel`'s addition to `NoScanExecutionFromGuiTests`).
- **Full regression** (2026-08-27): `dotnet build` — 0 warnings, 0 errors. `dotnet test`:
  ```
  ServerSleuth.Core.Tests:              61 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Analysis.Tests:         442 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Infrastructure.Tests:   171 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Linux.Tests:            345 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Windows.Tests:          376 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0):      98 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Cli.Tests (net8.0-win): 104 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Reporting.Tests:        146 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ServerSleuth.Integration.Tests:       14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped
  ```

  During this phase's own iterative work (not in the final run above), `ScanExecutionViewModelTests.Start_WhenExecutorThrows_ProducesAFailedState_WithAGenericMessage` failed once under parallel-test-run load and passed cleanly both in isolation and on the clean full run recorded above — the same pre-existing, environmental, GUI-3-era concurrency-timing flake already disclosed in the GUI-5 entry above, unrelated to any GUI-6 change (no `ScanExecutionViewModel`/`FakeGuiScanExecutor` source was touched this phase). Not fixed, per this phase's own "smallest correct change only where a genuine defect is found" scope — the affected code predates GUI-6.
- **Known Limitations**: identical to GUI-5's (raw-text-only viewer, no interactive/visual verification possible in this environment, export re-renders from the in-memory `Report` rather than caching original bytes) — unchanged this phase. The parallel-run timing flake noted above remains open and undiagnosed beyond "environmental, passes in isolation."
- **Final product readiness**: the complete Scan Configuration → Execution → Results Dashboard → Application Detail → Export/Open Report → New Scan workflow is implemented, tested, and structurally guaranteed never to re-run the pipeline, leak a credential, mutate a backend result, or reference a forbidden assembly. The one remaining gap to a full "release-ready" claim is that genuine interactive/visual validation has never been possible in any environment this work has run in — disclosed plainly, not glossed over. Per this phase's own explicit "FINAL STOP" instruction: **no GUI-7 or further GUI phase was started.**
